5. Pop `_MINTED[id(m.code)]` if its value is m.
Revision 5 deletes step 6 (`_unwind_off()` at retire). Every pop now clears in the same call when it leaves no anchor, so retirement never leaves the event to clear. The reconciliation's last step covers the one other source, an outside party's change. Revision 2's step 6 raced openers (the third critic's MF1; `rev3/c5r3_retire_step6_race.py`), and revision 3's form was a clearer like any other.

**Pruning cores reached from anchors (revision 4, B1; revision 5 merges it into the prune).** After the holders are pruned: for each `(fr, o)` in a `list(_ANCHORS.items())` snapshot whose `o.core` is dead by the holder test above, `_prune(o.core)`, then `_unwind_off(fr)` (a pop by the key, in one call with the clear). Revision 4 called this the anchor sweep and gave it its own credit stop. The model check found that stop necessary for revision 4's U1 as then stated (F9). Revision 5 states U1 for live cores, whose records can still be read, and drops the stop as above. The prune above reaches only cores that still hold a registered mint. This sweep also reaches an anchor whose core has already exited or released its mints: after two faults in an open that raced its trace's exit (a fault after `_commit`'s store, then one before the `finally`'s pop; X146d). A key maps to one opening only (it is that opening's `_run` frame), so the unguarded pop cannot remove another opening's anchor.

Revision 5 deletes revision 4's announcement sweep with the announcements themselves.

**Last step of every reconciliation (revision 3, MF2; revision 5 form; revision 6 guard).** If `_TOOL[0]` is not None: `_unwind_off(None)`, one call that pops nothing and clears the event iff no anchor is registered and the id carries `_TOOL_NAME`. Revision 6 adds the guard (F20). The process's first reconciliation, at the first enter's E4 step 1 or at an X0 after a failed first enter, runs before `_ensure_tool` has stored any id, and revision 5's unguarded pipeline then called `get_tool(None)`, which raises TypeError out of the transaction (`rev6/p_first_reconcile.py`, both versions). The test needs no gate of its own: `_TOOL[0]` changes only under the mutex, which the reconciler holds. From revision 5, no machinery step and no fault leaves the event set with no anchor (M7, U3). This step repairs an outside party's leftovers only. After a free and a reclaim, the event styxx left on the freed id is still set. An outside `set_events` may also have set it. X137's free variant witnesses the step (`global_events == 0` after exit).

### M4. `__enter__` (the facade calls `_enter(core)`)
- **E0. Claim.** `me = _txn()`. If `core.marks.setdefault('entering', me) is not me`, append the REENTRY text to `core.problems` and raise **REENTRY**.
- **E1. Version.** The M0 gate, raised.
- **E2. The cut (the function `_cut_refresh`; the constructor runs it too).** For each watched binding x in (`_HANDLE_DICT[0].get('_run')`, `_LOOP_DICT[0].get('_run_once')`), read from the class dicts that the first `coverage_trace()` captured (asyncio was imported there):
  - if `type(x) is not FunctionType`, raise **CUT_UNAVAILABLE** ("... is not a plain function");
  - if `_CUT.get(id(x.__code__)) is x.__code__`, x's code is already a cut code: nothing more;
  - otherwise the code is new. Raise **CUT_UNAVAILABLE** if it is *shareable*: `'<locals>' in x.__code__.co_qualname`, which means a factory or decorator can make more functions with it, or some FunctionType other than x is in `gc.get_referrers(x.__code__)`. The message is "`<binding>` is bound to `<qualname>`, whose code other functions share; every function with that code would stop attribution for the rest of the process. Bind a dedicated module-level function". Otherwise `_CUT.setdefault(id(x.__code__), x.__code__)`.

  Revision 2 (M2): the cut is monotone and by identity. Before this check, a generic decorator's inner function bound as `Handle._run` put a code object into the cut that every other function with that decorator shares. Each of those functions then stopped attribution, in that trace and in every later trace of the process (`rev2/p2_shared_cut.py`, reproducing the second critic's A5). The scan runs once per code new to the cut. It is 1.6–2.1 ms with 100k extra tracked objects and 14–17 ms with 1M (p2). A function created *after* E2 that reuses a cut code is not caught. That is an over-block, never an over-credit (#21).
- **E3. Resolve.** Resolve every target, outside the mutex. The declared module's import here is the only import inside enter or exit.
- **E4. `_locked(_enter_txn, core, resolved)`,** which runs:
  1. reconcile;
  2. `_ensure_tool()`:
     - If `_TOOL[0]` is set: `_reclaim()` (M3 step 0), then keep the id if `_named(_TOOL[0])`. Revision 5: reclaiming here, not only in step 1's reconciliation, makes a free that lands between step 1 and this step count in `_LOST` like any other free. Revision 4 re-took the id here without counting it. Together with revision 4's name-gated read at close (`_detach`), that lost a section's raising calls silently: the section of another trace that opened meanwhile got no event, its close's gated read saw a foreign-looking id and noted nothing, and that trace's exit saw `_LOST` unchanged and the id named (`rev5/m5_modelcheck.py`, `ext: enter(X) || open(Y)`, revision 4: LOSTNOTE 2; F13). **Revision 6 (M3) corrects what revision 5 claimed about this rule.** In that shape either rule alone closes the silent loss: with the count, the exit notes it through `_LOST`; with the ungated read at close (F14), the close notes it through `UNWIND_LOST`. In `ext: enter(X) ∥ open(Y)` the model needs both removed (`ensure_nocount` alone violates no property anywhere; with `blind_read_gated` it gives LOSTNOTE; `rev6/out_m6_mutants.txt`). So the count's only observable effect on its own is a MONITOR_LOST note on a trace that lost nothing through the free, which X137e witnesses. The ungated read is independently necessary. When a greenlet switch inside a reclaim, after its re-take and before its count, lets another greenlet steal the mutex (the owner's frames are off the thread's chain), that greenlet's exit runs its whole MONITOR_LOST test before the count lands. The close's ungated read is then the only detector of a call lost while the id was freed (`rev6/m6_modelcheck.py`: `blind_read_gated` alone gives LOSTNOTE in `ext+greenlet: [exit(X), rec] ∥ open(X)`; on greenlet 3.5.6, `rev6/w6_greenlet.py`; case X137f). `_named(i)` is `get_tool(i) is _TOOL_NAME` (revision 5): an identity test, which runs no `__eq__` at all. `use_tool_id` stores and `get_tool` returns the very object passed (`rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py` part F), and `_TOOL_NAME` survives a reload (M1). Revision 4's `type(n) is str and n == _TOOL_NAME` also ran no foreign `__eq__` (N1, `critic4/a5_tool_name_eq.py`, X142b). The identity test is also what the one-call steps use.
     - Otherwise, that is, when `_TOOL[0]` is None, or is set and is still not named after `_reclaim()` (another tool holds it; revision 6 states the fall-through, N1), for id 4 then 3:
       - adopt the id if `_named(id)`. That is this copy's own id, left by a fault before `_TOOL[0]` was stored;
       - or `_take(id)` (one call: `use_tool_id` iff the id is unowned at that instant; M3 step 0);
       - then, if `_named(id)`: `r = _register(id)` (M7). If `r` is complete, the id is usable. If `r` is split, the id is not used, and the loop goes on to the next id;
       - for a usable id: if `_TOOL[0]` is not None, whether `id` is the other id (a *rebinding*, revision 6) or `_TOOL[0]` itself (a *re-take*, revision 7): `_LOST.append(True)`, then `_set_local(id, m.code, _LOCAL)` for every mint in a `list(_MINTED.values())` snapshot. Then store `_TOOL[0] = id`, last. **The one E4 counting rule (revision 7, M5).** While `_TOOL[0]` is not None, every take that ends in a complete registration is counted once in `_LOST`, whichever step makes it: the reclaim (M3 step 3) or this line. With `_TOOL[0]` None (a first acquisition or an adoption), E4 makes no count of its own: no trace has entered, so none can be live. (Revision 8 self-audit, W4: this said "nothing is counted", which the in-call count contradicts. The registration still appends one entry per previous callback that is not styxx's, five on a fresh id (M7, "The registration"), and `core.lost0` is read after it, so no record sees them; X154e.) A re-take happens when both of this transaction's reclaims (the reconciliation's and `_ensure_tool`'s) had their registration split with owner None, because an audit hook freed the id again. Revision 6's text counted it (it said "if `_TOOL[0]` is set"), but called every such case a rebinding. Its prototype counted only `t != i`, and its model never re-took the same id: three rules for one path (the seventh critic's M5 item 2). The uncounted re-take left the free that the reclaim was handling counted by nobody (`rev7/w7_witnesses.py` X154d; `rev7/m7_modelcheck.py` `retake_nocount`). `mech7.py` and `m7_modelcheck.py` implement the rule stated here.
     - An id carrying any other name is never adopted. That includes another loaded copy's `styxx.protocol/…` name.
     - Neither id usable: raise **MONITOR_BUSY**. `_TOOL[0]` keeps its value.

     **Rebinding (and re-taking) while traces are live (revision 6, N1; revision 7).** Revision 5 rebound `_TOOL[0]` (X137b's "a second tracer takes id 3") without saying so in E4, and set no local events on the new id for mints that already existed. A tracer that then joined such a mint (Minting, "An existing mint") got no events on the new id for that function, and only X5's local-events test noted it. Revision 6 sets local events for every registered mint on the new id, so a joiner and every later hit are delivered on the new id (X137g). The count makes every trace live at the rebinding note MONITOR_LOST at its exit: its sections may have lost calls between the other tool's take and the rebinding, and a section open at the rebinding has no global PY_UNWIND on the new id until the next `_unwind_on` sets it (whose capture then notes it too). Such a trace's counts are lower bounds. The old id keeps styxx's leftover local events and callbacks until its new owner changes them (the disclosed leak, L-MONITOR). If both ids then deliver an event of one minted frame, the pending entry is keyed by the frame, so the second delivery finds no entry and publishes nothing: no double count (revision 8 self-audit, W1: X137i, in which both ids deliver f's events). Openers that read the old `_TOOL[0]` before the rebinding act on the old id, and their name gate makes that a no-op.
  3. the entry CODE_SWAPPED check for all F_T;
  4. join or mint every F_T in the Minting order;
  5. `core.names = …; core.prov = …; core.lost0 = len(_LOST); core.by_code = dict(core.names)`.
- **E5. Activate.** `core.marks['active'] = True`, after the mutex is released. Until then the entering token is live (E0's frame is executing), so no reconciler prunes the core.

A failed or abandoned enter leaves a dead entering token without `'active'`. The next reconciliation prunes it and retires its mints. The tracer is single-use, so a retry refuses REENTRY.

### M5. `__exit__` (the facade calls `_exit(core)`; it never raises GateSpecError, and it returns False unless an exception propagates out of it)
Revision 6 (B1 item 5) corrects the heading. Revision 5 said "always returns False". An exception raised inside X5's transaction by user code propagates: an audit hook that raises on `sys.monitoring.register_callback` (X5 step 2's registration) or on a `__code__` write (`_retire`'s restore). `_locked` has no `try`, so the exit token dies with it and the next acquirer steals the mutex. X6–X8 do not run, so `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE, and the next reconciliation prunes the core. A registration cut short that way leaves the first k callbacks re-registered and the rest as they were, which on styxx's own id are styxx's own unless an outside party replaced them (`rev6/r6_b1_register.py`, `raise` rows). Case X157 pins this outcome.
- **X-1.** If `core.pid != os.getpid()`, return: an inherited tracer is inert.
- **X0.** If `'active'` is not in marks, run `_locked(_reconcile)` and return. A GateSpecError from `_locked` is ignored here. This completes any abandoned enter, and makes an exit after a failed enter a no-op.
- **X1. Claim.** `me = _txn()`. If `core.marks.setdefault('exiting', me) is not me`, return: a second exit is a no-op.
- **X2. Credit stop.** `core.by_code = {}`, in one store.
- **X3. Detach.** For `o` in `list(core.openings)`: `_detach(o, ('open', ("[V5:OPEN_AT_EXIT] …",)))`.
- **X4. Clone problems.** `probs` = the CLONE_CALLED texts from `dict(core.clone_called)`.
- **X5. The exit transaction.** `held, more = _locked(_exit_txn, core)`, which runs:
  1. reconcile. This core is exiting with a live token, so it is kept.
  2. MONITOR_LOST test (revision 6 order). First `r = _register(tool)` (M7), then the loss is noted if any of these holds:
     - `r` is not complete: its first gate found the id not named (the id was freed and then taken by another tool), or the id changed hands inside the registration. In that case nothing below is tested, and the id is not touched again by this exit: no event is changed and no local event is cleared;
     - `len(_LOST) != core.lost0`, read after this exit's own registration: an id was freed during this trace and a reconciliation re-took it, `_TOOL[0]` was rebound or re-taken (revisions 2, 6 and 7), or some registration, this one included, found a previous callback that was not styxx's (revision 7, B1);
     - (revision 6's third source, "some previous callback in `r` is not styxx's", is now part of the `_LOST` test above.) **The repaired callback is counted (revision 7, B1).** A party that replaced one of styxx's callbacks on its id is found by the next registration, which repairs the slot. Revision 6 repaired without counting. Another live trace that had lost calls while the callback was replaced then found all five previous callbacks styxx's, `_LOST` unchanged, local events intact and no flag, and its loss was silent (the seventh critic's B1; `critic7/c1_cbrepair_two_traces.py`; `rev7/w7_witnesses.py` X158; `rev7/m7_modelcheck.py` `cb2:` configurations). Revision 7 counts the repair *inside the registration's consuming call* (M7, "The registration"). Each exchange whose previous callback is not styxx's appends to `_LOST` in C before the next exchange's audit hook can run, and (revision 8, M1) before that previous callback can be released, so no finalizer of it runs first. So every trace live then notes MONITOR_LOST at its own exit, whichever trace lost the calls. The seventh critic proposed a count in X5 after the registration returns. That count is separated from the repair by bytecode, and an asynchronous exception there (a KeyboardInterrupt, a signal handler's exception) skips it after the repair has landed. Its model finds that silent loss with one fault (`rev7/m7_modelcheck.py`, mutant `count_after_register`), and X158b shows it on CPython. This is F22's pattern: a repair by the machinery is counted like a re-take;
     - a held mint's local events on `tool` differ from `_LOCAL`;
     - `core.flags` has `'UNWIND_LOST'`: a close found the global PY_UNWIND cleared while its section was open (M7; revision 2 replaces revision 1's test at exit, because with section scoping the event is legitimately off by X5).

     Revision 4 tested the name, then registered. A tool that freed and re-took the id between the two lost its callbacks (the fifth critic's N1, `critic5/c4_toolid_retake_race.py`). Revision 5 gated the whole registration once, and claimed that made it one call that could overwrite only a callback on styxx's own id. `register_callback` raises an audit event before its exchange, so audit hooks ran inside that "one call", after its gate: a hook that yielded the GIL let another thread free the id and a foreign tool take it, and styxx's later exchanges then replaced up to three of that tool's callbacks (the sixth critic's B1; `critic6/a2_register_audit_split.py`; `rev6/r6_b1_register.py`, `rev5` rows). Revision 6 gates every exchange and reads the owner after the last one: at most one exchange can land on another tool's id, and the report names the owner (X154's `_register` sweep).
  3. For each mint m holding the core: CODE_SWAPPED if `m.fn.__code__ is not m.code`; then `m.holders = tuple([h for h in m.holders if h is not core])` (revision 4, MF4: a list comprehension, which 3.12 and 3.13 inline, not a generator expression, which has its own code object); then `_retire(m)` if the tuple is empty.

  A GateSpecError from `_locked` (REENTRANT or MACHINERY_BUSY) is appended to `probs`, and exit continues. The unreleased holdership is pruned at the next reconciliation, because the exit token dies when `_exit` returns.
- **X6. Tripwires.** CLONE_ALIVE for each held mint, outside the mutex. Then CUT_MOVED, if `core.flags` has it or `_cut_ok()` is false now.
- **X7. Problems.** `core.problems.extend(probs)` in one call. If a loss was noted, `core.lost_note = "[V5:MONITOR_LOST] …"`.
- **X8. Commit.** `core.marks['exited'] = True`.

**Interrupted exit.**
- *Before X1:* a zombie (L-ZOMBIE). `record()` refuses TRACE_ACTIVE, and one call of `__exit__()` completes it.
- *After X1:* `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE. The exit token is dead, so the next reconciliation prunes the core, detaches its openings and retires its mints.

**Interrupted enter.**
- *Before E4:* nothing global has changed.
- *Inside E4 or before E5:* the dead entering token is pruned at the next reconciliation.
- *At the facade's return:* a zombie.

### M6. The event path: tool id 4 or 3, callbacks with no handler, no lock and no DISABLE
```python
def _on_entry(code, offset):                    # PY_START, PY_RESUME (local, minted code only)
    m = _MINTED.get(id(code))
    if m is None or m.code is not code: return
    f = sys._getframe(1)
    holders = m.holders
    if not _cut_ok():                           # CUT_MOVED: a moved Handle._run or _run_once seen at a hit
        for h in holders: h.flags['CUT_MOVED'] = True
    if f.f_globals is not m.globals:            # CLONE_CALLED: credits nothing
        for h in holders: h.clone_called[id(code)] = m.qualname
        return
    if not _ANCHORS: return                     # no section open anywhere: nothing to credit, PY_UNWIND off (rev. 2)
    out = _outcome(f, code, holders, threading.get_ident())   # the walk: stops at a cut code or a loop boundary
    if out: m.pend[id(f)] = (f, offset, out)   # pending: the only store

def _on_exit(code, offset, value):              # PY_RETURN, PY_YIELD (local)
    m = _MINTED.get(id(code))
    if m is None or m.code is not code: return
    f = sys._getframe(1); p = m.pend.pop(id(f), None)
    if p is not None and p[0] is f: _publish(code, p[2])

def _on_unwind(code, offset, exc):              # PY_UNWIND (global, set only while a section is open; rev. 2)
    m = _MINTED.get(id(code))
    if m is None or m.code is not code: return
    f = sys._getframe(1); p = m.pend.pop(id(f), None)
    if p is not None and p[0] is f and offset != p[1]: _publish(code, p[2])
```
- **The walk.** `_outcome` reads `loop = _get_running_loop()` once, then tests `_CUT.get(id(g.f_code)) is g.f_code` for the cut, `_ANCHORS.get(g)` for anchors, and `o.loop is loop` for the loop boundary. Its per-holder rule is the one under Attribution.
- **Publishing.** `_publish` re-checks `id(code) in h.by_code` for each outcome, then makes single-key increments. Every per-opening counter has a single writer: an opening's anchor is on one stack at a time, and entry and confirmation of one resumption run on one thread.
- **Where callbacks run.** They never run at a C call: the only events are PY_START, PY_RESUME, PY_RETURN and PY_YIELD (local) and PY_UNWIND (global, and from revision 2 set only while some section is open). styxx never enables INSTRUCTION, LINE, JUMP or BRANCH events. On 3.12.3, INSTRUCTION, JUMP and BRANCH events on a code object make a signal at one of its back-edges skip that code's `finally` and with-exit (7–77 of 100 trials). Uninstrumented, or under styxx's own event set, 3.12.3 skipped 0 (`rev1/p11_backedge_timer.py`, `p12_backedge_under_styxx_events.py`, `p13_backedge_line_branch.py`). 3.13.12 skips in uninstrumented code too; that is CPython #130279 itself, not styxx. Code run inside any sys.monitoring callback raises no events for any tool (synth/p_syn1.py), so the callbacks cannot re-enter themselves.
- **Exceptions.** An exception raised in a callback propagates as CPython defines (L-DELIVERY). The tool is not dropped and the events stay set (D3 p2).

### M7. `_open`, `_commit`, `_run`, `_run_async`, `_detach` (no mutex)
The open checks are listed under Attribution. The rest (revision 5; runnable in `rev5/spec_rev5_eventpath.py`, which checks this text on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12):
```python
def _run(core, section, fn, args, kwargs):      # module level: its frame is the anchor, its locals hold no facade
    o = _open(core, section, sys._getframe())   # checks and append only (At open, steps 1-6)
    if o is None: return fn(*args, **kwargs)    # forked child: pass-through
    end = "raised"
    try:
        _commit(o)                              # revision 3: inside the try
        result = fn(*args, **kwargs); end = "returned"
    finally:
        _detach(o, (end, ()))                   # no loop in the try body, nor in any function it calls
        o.frame = None                          # revision 4 (B1): the opening's own finally releases the anchor frame
    t = type(result)                            # `is` chain, never `in` (see Resolution)
    if t is GeneratorType or t is CoroutineType or t is AsyncGeneratorType: o.lazy = _lazy_text(result)
    return result

def _commit(o):                                 # At open, steps 7-8 (revision 5 order)
    _ANCHORS[o.frame] = o                       # the store; o.frame is set: only this opening's finally releases it
    if 'fin' in o.fin or 'exiting' in o.core.marks:   # step 8: a detacher claimed o, or exit began
        _detach(o, ("open", (OPEN_AT_EXIT_TEXT,)))
        o.core.problems.append(TRACE_INACTIVE_TEXT)
        raise GateSpecError(TRACE_INACTIVE_TEXT)
    _unwind_on(o)                               # one call, after the store: no clear can follow while o is registered
    o.armed = True                              # last: the body may run now

def _unwind_on(o):
    # ONE CALL { if get_tool(t) is _TOOL_NAME and _ANCHORS.get(o.frame) is o:
    #                if not (get_events(t) & PY_UNWIND):
    #                    for p in _ANCHORS.values(): if p.armed: p.core.flags['UNWIND_LOST'] = True
    #                set_events(t, PY_UNWIND) }
    t, get_tool, get_events, set_events = _TOOL[0], _MON[0][0], _MON[0][1], _MON[0][2]   # revision 6: one assignment (G_HYG)
    _CONSUME(_chain(
        _map(_setitem, _map(_FLAGS, _filter(_ARMED, _chain.from_iterable(_map(_VALUES,
            _compress(_compress(_compress((_ANCHORS,), _map(_is, _map(get_tool, (t,)), _NAME1)),
                                _map(_is, _map(_ANCHORS.get, (o.frame,)), (o,))),
                      _map(_not, _map(_and, _map(get_events, (t,)), _PYU1))))))), _LOST_KEY, _TRUE),
        _map(set_events,
             _compress(_compress((t,), _map(_is, _map(get_tool, (t,)), _NAME1)),
                       _map(_is, _map(_ANCHORS.get, (o.frame,)), (o,))),
             _PYU1)))

def _unwind_off(key):                           # _detach (key = the anchor frame), and last in every reconciliation (None)
    # ONE CALL { _ANCHORS.pop(key, None); if get_tool(t) is _TOOL_NAME and not _ANCHORS: set_events(t, 0) }
    t, get_tool, set_events = _TOOL[0], _MON[0][0], _MON[0][2]
    _CONSUME(_chain(_map(_ANCHORS.pop, (key,), _NONE1),
                    _map(set_events, _compress(_compress((t,), _map(_is, _map(get_tool, (t,)), _NAME1)),
                                               _map(_not, (_ANCHORS,))), _ZERO1)))

def _detach(o, fin):
    o.fin.setdefault("fin", fin)                # one claim: first finaliser wins
    fr = o.frame                                # never assigned here: every detacher pops by the anchor's own key
    if fr is not None:
        if o.armed and not (_MON[0][1](_TOOL[0]) & PY_UNWIND) and _ANCHORS.get(fr) is o:
            o.core.flags['UNWIND_LOST'] = True  # the event first, the anchor last: an outside party cleared it
        _unwind_off(fr)                         # one call: pop, then clear iff no anchor is left

def _take(t):                                   # M3 step 0 and _ensure_tool
    # ONE CALL { if get_tool(t) is None: use_tool_id(t, _TOOL_NAME) }; the caller then tests _named(t)
    get_tool, use_tool_id = _MON[0][0], _MON[0][5]
    _CONSUME(_map(use_tool_id, _compress((t,), _map(_is, _map(get_tool, (t,)), _NONE1)), _NAME1))

def _register(t):                               # _ensure_tool, M3 step 0, M5 X5. Revision 6: NOT a one-call step
    # ONE STATEMENT, ONE CONSUMING CALL, but register_callback raises an audit event before each exchange, so audit hooks
    # run inside it. { for i in 0..4: if get_tool(t) is not _TOOL_NAME: stop; prev = register_callback(t, ev[i], cb[i]);
    #                  if prev is not cb[i]: _LOST.append(True) }   (revision 7, B1: the count is in the same C call)
    # then owner = get_tool(t); returns [None] * (number of previous callbacks that were not styxx's) + [owner]
    # Revision 8 (M1): the exchanges are read through _tee, whose buffer keeps every prev referenced until the pipeline
    # is released after _list returns, so a replaced callback's finalizer runs only after every count is made.
    get_tool, register_callback = _MON[0][0], _MON[0][4]
    a, b = _tee(_map(register_callback,
                     _compress(_repeat(t), _map(_is, _map(get_tool, _repeat(t, 5)), _repeat(_TOOL_NAME))),
                     _EVENTS5, _CALLBACKS5))
    return _list(_chain(
        _map(_LOST_APPEND, _map(_is_not, _compress(a, _map(_is_not, b, _CALLBACKS5)), _repeat(None))),
        _map(get_tool, (t,))))

def _set_local(t, code, events):                # Minting step 4, _retire step 2, a rebinding (E4)
    # ONE CALL { if get_tool(t) is _TOOL_NAME: set_local_events(t, code, events) }
    get_tool, set_local_events = _MON[0][0], _MON[0][3]
    _CONSUME(_map(set_local_events, _compress((t,), _map(_is, _map(get_tool, (t,)), _NAME1)), (code,), (events,)))

def _named(i): return _MON[0][0](i) is _TOOL_NAME          # revision 5: identity, so no __eq__ of any kind runs

def _ours(): return _TOOL[0] is not None and _named(_TOOL[0])
```
styxx's global event set is only ever PY_UNWIND or nothing. `set_events`, `set_local_events`, `register_callback` and `use_tool_id` are called only inside the five step functions (`_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take`, `_register`, `_set_local`), except `_forget_in_child` in the single-threaded child (G_HYG). Every test of a name goes through `_named` or a step's `_is` gate (G_HYG). Revision 6 (M4 ii): each step function's first statement binds, as locals, `t = _TOOL[0]` (in `_unwind_on` and `_unwind_off`) and the `_MON[0]` items it uses, under the names shown; `_take`, `_register` and `_set_local` receive `t` as a parameter. Revision 8 (M3) freezes which items each step binds, and so which `sys.monitoring` functions it can call: `_unwind_on` binds `t` and items 0, 1 and 2 (`get_tool`, `get_events`, `set_events`); `_unwind_off` binds `t` and items 0 and 2; `_take` items 0 and 5 (`use_tool_id`); `_register` items 0 and 4 (`register_callback`); `_set_local` items 0 and 3 (`set_local_events`). So only `_unwind_on` and `_unwind_off` can write S (G_HYG). Revision 8 (M1): `_register` has one more statement between the two, `a, b = _tee(...)`, which builds the exchange pipeline and its two branches and reads nothing.

**One-call steps (revision 5; revision 6 removes `_register` from them).** A *one-call step* is one statement that builds a lazy pipeline and hands it to one consuming builtin, `_CONSUME` or `_list`. The pipeline is made of `map`, `filter`, `itertools.chain`/`compress`/`repeat`, and `operator` callables. It calls only C callables: `operator` functions, `dict.get`, `dict.pop` and `dict.values`, `attrgetter` on `__slots__` fields, and the `sys.monitoring` functions in `_MON`, each checked to be the C builtin when it was bound (M0, revision 6). Building the pipeline reads no shared state. The statement reads only `_TOOL[0]` and `o.frame`, which only the mutex and the opening's own thread change. Every read and every write happens inside the one consuming call, in C. CPython runs no Python code inside a C call that makes no Python-level call and raises no audit event: no eval-breaker check runs there. So no thread switch, signal handler, cyclic-gc finalizer (3.12 and later run gc only at eval-breaker checks, and schedule it only from `_PyObject_GC_Link`: `critic6/a6_gc_inside_step.py`, 0 of about 3,600 gc callbacks and finalizers inside a consuming call with 2,000 tracked allocations at threshold 1) or asynchronous exception lands there, and no greenlet switch. No trace or profile function and no sys.monitoring callback fires for calls made from C. RecursionError cannot land after a step's first write: every pipeline callable is a vectorcall callable on both versions (`critic6/a4_vectorcall.py`, `a5_recursion_inside_step.py`), and a depth sweep across the recursion boundary finds only full effects or none (`critic8/c4`; frozen as G_ATOM part R in revision 8, N3). Of the `sys.monitoring` functions styxx calls, `get_tool`, `get_events`, `set_events`, `set_local_events` and `use_tool_id` raise no audit event; **`register_callback` raises `sys.monitoring.register_callback` before its exchange** (`critic6/a1_audit_monitoring.py`, both versions). So:
- `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take` and `_set_local` are one-call steps. Each is atomic with respect to everything else in the process, instruments included, with one exception: a **MemoryError** raised by an allocation inside the call (`set_events` and `set_local_events` allocate per-code monitoring data; the capture's `setitem` can resize a dict) ends the step early, and the step's earlier effects stand (N2). In `_unwind_off` that can leave S set with no anchor after the pop, until the next close that leaves no anchor or the next reconciliation (U3). In `_unwind_on` the exception propagates out of `_commit`, so the body never runs (U1 is unaffected). This matters only when the process is out of memory;
- **`_register` is not a one-call step** (revision 6, B1). It is one statement with one consuming call, but a Python audit hook runs inside each of its five `register_callback` calls, before the exchange. See "The registration" below.

`rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py` and `rev6/t1d2_discriminating.py` show the premise for the one-call steps on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12:
- *Per-event sweeps (part A).* One trial per event that an INSTRUCTION tool, a CALL + C_RETURN tool, a BRANCH tool, a pure-Python `sys.setprofile` function and opcode tracing deliver inside the clearing function. Each trial registers an anchor at that event. The one-call step never clears after an anchor registered while the event was set: 0 violations in 48 / 32 / 0 / 3 / 48 trials per version. The BRANCH row fires no event inside the one-call form on either version, so its 0 is vacuous and it is not evidence (revision 7, M1 item 5); G_ATOM's frozen thresholds exclude it. The two-statement control (`A.pop(key)`, then `if not A: set_events(t, 0)`) violates under every one of the five, in 5–6 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 5–6 trials.
- *What instruments see (part B).* During a one-call step, a profile function sees one `c_call`, the consumer's, and a CALL tool sees only the pipeline's constructors. Neither sees `get_tool`, `get_events`, `set_events` or `pop`.
- *Audit events (part C).* None from `_unwind_off` and `_unwind_on`, the only steps part C ran. Revision 5 wrote "none, from any one-call step"; `_take`, `_register` and `_set_local` were never probed, and `_register` raises five (B1). G_ATOM now probes every step in every gate outcome of its frozen case matrix (`rev6/atom6.py`; revision 7's matrix and hookup criteria: `rev7/atom7.py`).
- *Gc and signals (part D2, revision 6; count-bounded in revision 8, G_ATOM).* Revision 5's part D did not discriminate: its two-statement control also showed 0 violations (the sixth critic's M5), because its finalizer and handler registered an anchor only while `'closer'` was in the registry, which is false exactly in the control's window, and because the control has no eval-breaker check between its test and its write. Part D2 (`rev6/t1d2_discriminating.py`) marks the close with a flag instead, and adds a control whose test and write are separated by a Python-level call. A cyclic-gc finalizer and a 20 µs SIGALRM handler, each registering an anchor, at gc threshold 1, 3 s per form: the one-call step 0 violations in 8,701 / 9,162 closes (3.12.3 / 3.13.12); the Python-call control 2,303 / 3,336; revision 5's two-statement control 0 / 0, which is why part D proved nothing.
- *Threads (part E; count-bounded in revision 8, G_ATOM).* A pure-Python profiler that yields the GIL at every C call in the close path, over 4 threads: 0 of about 71,000 body samples found the event clear. The two-statement control found it clear in 165 and 195.

**The registration (revision 6, B1; revision 7 counts inside it).** `_register(t)` gates each `register_callback` on `get_tool(t) is _TOOL_NAME`, evaluated immediately before that call, and after the last exchange reads the owner once more, still inside the consuming call. Let n (0 to 5) be the number of exchanges made. Revision 7 (B1): each exchange's previous callback is compared by identity with the callback styxx registered for that event (`_is_not`). For each one that differs (another party's callback, or None) `_LOST_APPEND` runs in C, before the pipeline pulls the next gate, so before the next exchange's audit hook can run. **Revision 8 (M1): the exchanges are read through `_tee`.** Branch `a` feeds `_compress`, which yields the previous callback itself whenever branch `b`'s comparison `_is_not(prev, cb_i)` is true. `_is_not(prev, None)` turns it into a bool, and `_LOST_APPEND` appends that bool. The value is never read; `len(_LOST)` is. The tee's buffer keeps every previous callback referenced until the pipeline is released: after `_list` returns, when both branches are dropped. Revision 7's pipeline did not do this. There, `map` dropped the previous callback right after `_is_not` returned, before `_filter` passed the True on and before `_LOST_APPEND` ran. If the slot held the only reference to an outside party's replacement, its deallocation ran Python code at that point, with the slot repaired and the count not yet made: a `__del__`, a `weakref.finalize`, or a finalizer of something the callable's closure held. If that finalizer switched greenlets, another trace's exit could steal the mutex (the owner's frames are off the chain) and find `_LOST` unchanged, and that trace's loss was silent again (the eighth critic's M1; `critic8/c1_del_between_exchange_and_count.py`, both versions). Under revision 8 every such finalizer runs after the consuming call has returned, with every count made. In `rev8/c1_on_mech8.py` the finalizer already sees the post-count `len(_LOST)`, and the greenlet shape notes the other trace (X158d). `rev8/out_c7_rerun.txt` shows the critic's sketch giving the same result. `r` is `[None] * k + [owner]`, where k is the number of such counts. Revision 6 returned `[prev_1, …, prev_n, owner]` and left the count to its caller. The audit hook of exchange i runs after gate i and before exchange i. Between exchange i, its comparison and count, and gate i+1, and between the last exchange and the owner read, CPython runs only C code. Revision 7 said "nothing runs" there and its pipeline did not ensure it. From revision 8, the only objects whose last reference the pipeline drops inside the call are bools and None, and it releases the previous callbacks only after the call. Hence, whatever the audit hooks do:
- exchanges 1 to n−1 landed on an id that carried `_TOOL_NAME` at that moment;
- exchange n landed on the id as `owner` holds it: on styxx's id if `owner is _TOOL_NAME`, on an unowned id if `owner` is None (nobody's callbacks are replaced), and otherwise on the id of the tool that owns it now, whose callback for `_EVENTS5[n-1]` styxx then replaced with its own. styxx does not restore it: a restoring `register_callback` would run an audit hook before its own exchange, and could overwrite a callback that tool registered meanwhile;
- `r` is *complete* iff `r[-1] is _TOOL_NAME`, and *incomplete* otherwise. Completeness implies n = 5. If some gate k fails, the gates after it and the owner read run back to back in C with nothing between them (the seventh critic's reading of the pipeline, confirmed on both versions), so the owner read sees the same non-styxx owner that failed gate k;
- every repair of a slot that did not hold styxx's callback is counted in `_LOST` in the same C call as the exchange that made it (revision 7). A fault, an audit hook, a thread switch or a greenlet switch can land between two exchanges, but never between an exchange and its count. From revision 8, a replaced callback's finalizer cannot land there either, because it runs only after the call. Revision 7's pipeline let one run between them (the eighth critic's M1; G_ATOM's finalizer case; X158d). So no repair is ever left uncounted (`rev7/m7_modelcheck.py`: `cb2:` configurations with a fault, a greenlet switch and a re-entered exit; X158b on CPython; revision 8: `rev8/m8_modelcheck.py` mutant `count_after_release`). An exchange that lands on another tool's id, whose previous callback is that tool's, is counted too; that is harmless, because the registration is then incomplete and noted anyway (F21).

Every caller acts on `r`: E4 uses the id only if `r` is complete; the reclaim counts a complete `r`; X5 notes MONITOR_LOST unless `r` is complete, and reads `len(_LOST)` after the registration, so its own in-call count notes its own trace too. So a split is noted by the exit that made it, and by every other trace live at that moment: while another tool holds the id, no exit's registration on it is complete, and a later complete registration needs a reclaim of that id (after its owner frees it), a re-take, or a rebinding to the other id, each of which appends to `_LOST`. A split at a first acquisition or an adoption (`_TOOL[0]` None) has no live trace to note it. The report there only moves E4 to the other id, and the one replaced callback of the other tool is surfaced nowhere (X154c; revision 7, N3). A count at the split itself would therefore change no record. Revision 6 has none (the model check's REGSILENT property checks exactly this, with no such count). Revision 7's in-call count makes one when the exchange that landed on the other tool's id found that tool's callback, and it is redundant there. At a first acquisition, a rebinding or an adoption onto a fresh id, every previous callback is None, so the registration counts five. No trace is live at a first acquisition or an adoption, and a rebinding counts anyway, so this changes no record. The rebinding's own count stays necessary: a rebinding onto an id that still carries styxx's own callbacks from an earlier use counts nothing in-call (X137h, revision 7 form). The residual, disclosed under L-MONITOR: when an audit hook on `sys.monitoring.register_callback` yields the GIL (a thread switch), switches greenlets, or runs code that frees styxx's id, and another tool takes the id before that exchange, styxx replaces that tool's callback for one event and notes it; it does not restore it. A hostile tool that takes the id under styxx's own name object is taken for styxx (L-MONITOR). A hook that raises leaves the first i−1 exchanges made and the rest not made; the exception propagates from `_register` (E4: the enter fails; M3 step 0: the transaction fails; X5: `__exit__` propagates it, M5). On styxx's own id the callbacks not re-registered are the ones already there, which are styxx's own unless a party replaced them. `rev6/r6_b1_register.py` runs the critic's shape at each of the five hooks on both versions: revision 5's form replaced 1–3 of the foreign tool's callbacks and reported nothing; revision 6's replaced at most one (at hooks 1, 3 and 5; at hooks 2 and 4 the foreign tool had no callback for that event), and every trial reported the split with the foreign owner. A raising hook at exchange 3 left two callbacks re-registered under both forms, and at X5 it propagated out of `__exit__` with the exit uncommitted and the mutex owner dead; the next trace passed.

Revision 5 called `_register` a one-call step. Registration cannot be made atomic from Python: the audit event is raised inside `register_callback` itself, before the exchange, and styxx cannot remove it. Registration is kept to where it is needed: the first acquisition of an id, a reclaim, a rebinding, and X5's test, which is the only way to learn a callback (sys.monitoring has no getter). Registering once at import and never again was considered: it would leave a replaced callback undetected and unrepaired (X5's test is the detector), and a reclaim after another tool's use of the id needs a registration anyway.

G_HYG checks the shape statically and G_ATOM dynamically. `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take`, `_register` and `_set_local` each consist of one statement binding their locals from `_TOOL[0]` and `_MON[0]`, and then the one statement. `_register` has `a, b = _tee(...)` between the two (revision 8). Each pipeline names only the M1 one-call vocabulary, the step's locals and parameters, `o.frame`, `_ANCHORS`, `_ANCHORS.get`, `_ANCHORS.pop` and literal tuples (G_HYG gives the exact list). The mutation audit's revision-5 and revision-6 rows name the witnesses for splitting any of them.

`_run_async` has the same shape, with `_commit(o)` and then `result = await afn(*args, **kwargs)` as its try body, and the same two statements in its `finally`. That `await` compiles to a SEND loop. Its throw-then-return path is taken when a thrown exception is handled by the awaited object, which then returns. On that path, the closing jump after CLEANUP_THROW lies outside the try's exception-table range on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12. On 3.12 it is a JUMP_BACKWARD, which checks the eval breaker, so a signal landing there skips the `finally` (`rev1/p19_run_async_throw_path.py`). On 3.13 it is JUMP_BACKWARD_NO_INTERRUPT, which only an injected fault reaches. No count, note, refusal or termination depends on that `finally`. The opening's anchor frame is dead, so it is on no live chain, and exit's X3 or a prune finalises the opening as `('open', OPEN_AT_EXIT)`, inside I6. Until then the dead anchor keeps PY_UNWIND set, which is L-DELIVERY scope only (U4 below), and the opening keeps its dead anchor frame (M1). `_run`'s try body, `_commit(o)` and the call, has no loop. Revision 5 has no loop in any function that `_run`'s try body or `finally` calls: the one-call steps iterate in C, not at a bytecode back-edge. Revision 4's `_await_clearers` loop is gone. `rev5/spec_rev5_eventpath.py` finds no backward jump in `_run`, `_commit`, `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_detach`, `_take`, `_register`, `_set_local` or `_named`. It then faults every instruction of `_commit` and `_unwind_on`. In every trial `_run`'s `finally` ran and left no anchor and no event (24 and 113–117 trials per version). No signal position skipped `_run`'s `finally` (p10).

**The unwind scope (revision 2, M4; restated in revision 3; made atomic in revision 5).** The global PY_UNWIND is set only while some anchor is registered in this copy, so only while some section is open anywhere in the process. Revision 1 set it while any mint existed, which included a whole trace, zombies, and a freed id after exit. The protocol has no lock and no wait:
- the opener, inside `_run`'s `try`, stores its anchor, re-tests its claim (step 8), then runs `_unwind_on(o)`: one call that sets the event iff the id is named and o's anchor is still registered. Then it arms;
- every clear is `_unwind_off(key)`: one call that pops the key and clears iff the id is named and no anchor is left. The closer and every other detacher call it with the anchor frame, and the reconciliation calls it last with None.

*Why no clear can land under a registered anchor.* A machinery clear happens only inside an `_unwind_off` call, and only if that same call finds `_ANCHORS` empty. Nothing runs inside that call. So at the instant of any machinery clear, no anchor is registered. An opener O stores its anchor before its `_unwind_on`. From O's `_unwind_on` until O's pop, S is set, unless a party outside the machinery changes it. The argument uses no order between threads, no liveness test and no wait. It holds whatever Python code runs between two machinery steps, on any thread, O's own included. That covers a signal handler or finalizer that opens a section, a profiler, a CALL or INSTRUCTION tool, a greenlet switch, and a `run_async` section left suspended and resumed elsewhere. Revision 4's two exclusions for code on O's own thread (R19) and for greenlet switches are therefore gone. Revision 3 relied on `_ANCHORS or set_events(_TOOL[0], 0)` having no eval-breaker check between the test and the call. That fails under any tool that runs Python code at an instruction inside the expression, including tools this spec says coexist (the fourth critic's MF1; `critic4/a1_what_splits_test_and_clear.py`):
- a `sys.setprofile` or `threading.setprofile_all_threads` function (V48's shape), and cProfile with a Python timer;
- a sys.monitoring CALL callback, local or global;
- a sys.monitoring INSTRUCTION callback, the id-5 injector included, and a settrace function with `f_trace_opcodes` set;
- a BRANCH callback on 3.12.3 (not on 3.13.12).

Revision 4 tolerated them by making the opener wait for announced clearers. The wait closed cycles through user code and through sections opened inside a clearer's window (the fifth critic's MF1), and its same-thread exclusion lost calls silently (MF2). A one-call step is not split by any of these tools (`rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py`), so revision 5 needs neither the announcement nor the wait.

*The invariant, with its windows.* Write S for "styxx's id has PY_UNWIND set" and A for "some anchor is registered".
- **U1. An armed section's body never runs with S clear while its core is crediting and alive.** This holds on every interleaving, fault-free or not, whatever Python code runs between two machinery steps, on any thread, this one included. The one exclusion is closed: a party outside the machinery that clears S, frees styxx's id, or changes its callbacks or local events (L-MONITOR). The body starts after the opener's `_unwind_on`, and by the argument above no machinery clear lands while its anchor is registered. The anchor stays registered until its own close, or until a detacher pops it: exit's X3 after the credit stop, or a reconciliation's prune of a dead core. After X3 nothing counts. After a prune the core is dead, and its record is never read again (M3). A fault in the machinery never clears S: faults only cut a step sequence short, and a fault cannot land inside a one-call step, other than a MemoryError from an allocation inside it (N2, "One-call steps"). `_register` is not a one-call step, and a fault from an audit hook can land inside it (revision 6, B1). `_register` never writes S: from revision 8 it binds no `set_events` (G_HYG's index clause), and G_ATOM's `_register` cases with S set check it (the eighth critic's M3). The model check takes the atomicity of `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take` and `_set_local` as an axiom, and G_ATOM tests it on each listed patch level. **Revision 8 (M1): the model also takes as an axiom that a registration's exchange and its in-call count are one step.** No Python code runs between them on CPython: the tee form holds the previous callback until the call returns, and G_ATOM's finalizer case and X158d test this. Revision 7's CPython form broke the axiom. `rev8/m8_modelcheck.py`'s mutant `count_after_release` puts a step boundary between the exchange and its count, where a replaced callback's finalizer would run, and finds LOSTNOTE (Revision 8 section).
- **U2. A clear S with A set lasts only while an opener is between its anchor store and its `_unwind_on`,** on the opener's own thread, before its body starts, or after an outside party cleared S.
- **U3. A set S with no anchor never exists**, fault-free or after a fault, except after an outside party set S or freed styxx's id, or after a MemoryError inside `_unwind_off`'s `set_events` (N2). Then it lasts until the next reconciliation's `_unwind_off(None)` or the next close that leaves no anchor. S is set only by `_unwind_on`, in the same call as a test that o's anchor is registered. A becomes empty only by a pop inside `_unwind_off`, in the same call as the clear. Revision 4 had two windows here: a detacher between its pop and its clear, and an opener whose anchor X3 popped after step 8, which set S and kept it for its whole uncredited body. Both are gone. `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py` checks this as STALE in every state of every configuration.
- **U4. After faults**, S set with no live section lasts only while a *dead anchor* stays registered. That follows a fault in a close before its `_unwind_off` call (at `_detach`'s first instructions, or while `_unwind_off` builds its pipeline), or a `_run_async` `finally` skipped by #130279 on 3.12. It lasts until the owning trace's exit detaches it at X3, or a reconciliation prunes it once its core is dead (revision 4: this also reaches a core that already exited, X146d). This is L-DELIVERY scope only. The dead anchor's frame is on no live chain, so no count, note or refusal depends on it. Revision 4's other fault window, a fault between a pop and its clear, no longer exists. In `rev5/spec_rev5_eventpath.py`'s fault sweep, every trial in the close path left either nothing or a dead anchor with S set, never S set with no anchor.

  A fault anywhere in `_commit`, which is revision 2's MF2 case, leaves neither: the `finally` pops the anchor (`o.frame` is still set) and clears S if no anchor is left.
- **What is lost (MONITOR_LOST completeness, revision 5; revision 7 adds callbacks and local events).** Nothing is lost through the machinery, on any interleaving, fault-free or not (U1). A call lost through an outside party is noted in one of these places:
  - the section's close, or exit's X3, finds S clear under its armed, registered anchor;
  - the next `_unwind_on` anywhere in the process finds S clear, and notes every armed, registered opening in the same call in which it sets S;
  - the exit's MONITOR_LOST test finds `_LOST` changed since the trace's E4 (an id freed and re-taken, a rebinding or re-take, or, from revision 7, a replaced callback that another trace's exit repaired), or the id taken by another tool (an incomplete registration);
  - the exit's MONITOR_LOST test finds a replaced callback itself (and counts its repair, revision 7), or a held mint's local events changed. No machinery step repairs a mint's local events while any holder is live (only a rebinding or re-take sets them, and those count), so every holder of that mint finds them changed at its exit.

  The silent shapes, all outside parties that undo their own change before a machinery step reads it: a party that cleared S sets it again itself; a party that replaced a callback restores it before any exit registers; a party that cleared a mint's local events sets them again before every holder's exit. Revision 6 had a fourth, silent through the machinery itself: an exit's registration repaired a replaced callback for every trace, but only the exiting trace was noted (the seventh critic's B1, closed by the count; X158). The model check covers every place in this list with two cores from revision 7 (`cb2:` configurations). The notes are precise too. Every flag follows a state in which S was clear under an armed, registered anchor, which by U1 only an outside party can cause. `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py` checks both halves as LOSTNOTE and FALSEFLAG. Revision 4's close-time test alone was erased by any section that opened before the blinded close (the fifth critic's MF2; `critic5/c7_external_clear_masked.py`, X137d).
