What scoping costs:
- 2.5–3.2 µs more per section (`rev2/p4_unwind_scope.py`);
- each toggle bumps the interpreter's monitoring version, so every code object that then runs is re-instrumented once, about 0.05–0.10 µs per distinct function per section (p4);
- other tools' `DISABLE`d locations stay disabled: no location fired more than twice in 1,000 toggling sections, and a location firing twice is a line that spans two offsets (`rev2/p4b_disable_kept.py`).

### M8. `record()` is pure
- **Refusals first.**
  - Refuse **TRACE_INCOMPLETE** if `core.pid != os.getpid()` ("inherited across fork: the record belongs to the parent process").
  - If `'exited'` is not in marks: refuse **TRACE_INCOMPLETE** when `'exiting'` is present, or `'entering'` is present without `'active'`. Refuse **TRACE_ACTIVE** with "the tracer was never entered" when `'entering'` is absent (v5e's X91, unchanged). Otherwise refuse **TRACE_ACTIVE** with "the tracer is active: record() was called inside its with-block, or its `__exit__` never started, and then one call of `__exit__()` completes it".
- **Openings.** Each opening's `end, notes = o.fin.get('fin', ('open', ()))`, plus `o.lazy` and `core.lost_note` when set.
- **Counts.** Expanded through `core.names`. `uncredited` is summed across threads.

The schema is v5e's, with the `/3` id and the code sets of the Reason-codes section.

### M9. The at-fork handler
`os.register_at_fork(after_in_child=_forget_in_child)` is registered at import on every version; it is a no-op until a tracer has been constructed. Revision 2 (N5) orders the steps so that every step that runs no user code comes before any step that can:
1. replace `_GUARD['hint']` with a free token;
2. `_ANCHORS.clear()` (revision 5: `_CLEARING` no longer exists);
3. `set_events(tool, 0)` if the tool is ours;
4. for each mint m in a `list(_MINTED.values())` snapshot: `set_local_events(tool, m.code, 0)` if ours, and `m.pend.clear()`;
5. clear `_BY_FN` and `_MINTED`;
6. last, for each mint in the snapshot: if `m.fn.__code__ is m.code`, restore `m.original`.

Step 6 is the only one that runs user code: a `__code__` write raises the `object.__setattr__` audit event. CPython ignores an exception raised by an at-fork handler, so a raising audit hook aborts the handler there. Revision 1 ran the restores first, and such an abort left the child with its anchors, its registered mints and the global PY_UNWIND for its whole life. In revision 2 the child keeps only the minted code still installed on the remaining functions (`rev2/p8_atfork_abort.py`, both versions). That code has no events and runs as the original would, since M_T is an equal copy. A child tracer that later declares such a function treats M_T as its original, and restores M_T at the end.

The pass-through no longer depends on this handler. `run()`, `__exit__`, `record()` and reconciliation compare `core.pid` with `os.getpid()`, so they are correct even if the handler never ran or was aborted at any step. G_FI's fork scenario sweeps this handler (Companion gates).

The tool id is kept (monotone). Inherited cores have another pid: `run()` passes through, `__exit__` is a no-op, and `record()` refuses.

### M10. The introspection interface (frozen with the prereg; the only private surface the exam may read, and, for G_ATOM only, the G_ATOM interface)
- **`_v5_state()`** returns a fresh dict (its fields are the vocabulary of C1–C9, G_FI):
  - `"mints"`: a list of `{"target", "holders": int, "installed": bool, "local_events": int, "pending": int}`, sorted by the tuple `(target, holders, installed, local_events, pending)`. `target` is F_T's `module:qualname` (revision 4, N6: snapshot equality needs a fixed order);
  - `"anchors"`: int;
  - `"guard"`: `"free" | "held" | "dead"`;
  - `"cut"`: int;
  - `"cut_current"`: bool, `_cut_ok()` (both watched bindings are plain functions whose code is in `_CUT`; revision 2);
  - `"tool"`: int or None;
  - `"tool_ours"`: bool;
  - `"global_events"`: int;
  - `"pid"`: int.
- **`_v5_faultpoints()`** returns `{qualname: fn.__code__}`, reading each function's `__code__` **at call time** (revision 2, M6), for injectors and the exam's lower-id fault tool. While a self-trace has minted a machinery function, that is the self-trace's M_T, which is the code that actually runs. Code captured at import never fires then (`rev2/p6_faultpoint_identity.py`). Its key set is frozen here (revision 1; revision 2 adds `_cut_refresh`, `_reclaim`, `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off` and `_ours`; revision 3 adds `_commit`; revision 4 adds `_named`; revision 5 adds `_take`, `_register` and `_set_local` and removes `_await_clearers` and the `"clearing"` field of `_v5_state()`), so exam cases can name machinery functions before the implementation exists:
  - `coverage_trace`;
  - the facade methods `_CoverageTracer.__enter__`, `__exit__`, `run`, `run_async` and `record`;
  - the module-level functions `_enter`, `_exit`, `_enter_txn`, `_exit_txn`, `_resolve_target`, `_own_dict`, `_cache_callee`, `_provenance`, `_ensure_tool`, `_mint`, `_retire`, `_reconcile`, `_prune`, `_locked`, `_acquire`, `_release`, `_txn`, `_alive`, `_open`, `_commit`, `_detach`, `_run`, `_run_async`, `_lazy_text`, `_cut_ok`, `_cut_refresh`, `_reclaim`, `_take`, `_register`, `_set_local`, `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_ours`, `_named`, `_clone_alive`, `_on_entry`, `_on_exit`, `_on_unwind`, `_outcome`, `_publish`, `_forget_in_child`, `_v5_state` and `_v5_faultpoints`.

  The v5 machinery is exactly these functions, plus the M1 names and the scoring functions of M11. It has no nested function, lambda, generator expression or other class; the facade and the plain-data classes `_Mint`, `_Core`, `_Opening` and `_Txn` have no other methods. Revision 4 (MF4) makes this mechanical: no region code object has a code object in its `co_consts`. On 3.12 and 3.13 a generator expression compiles to its own code object, which the injector, X140's back-edge list and G_COVER's line set would not reach. List, set and dict comprehensions are inlined (PEP 709) and are allowed. G_HYG checks this.

Neither function changes state.

- **The G_ATOM interface (revision 7, M1; frozen with the prereg, read by G_ATOM only).** G_ATOM has to put each step function into each of its gate outcomes, and some of those outcomes need an opening that is not registered, several armed openings, a registered key that no section owns, and the id given back to styxx after a case in which another tool held it. The public API cannot build these. Revision 6 said G_ATOM needed only `_v5_faultpoints()`, the public API and `sys.monitoring`; its own prototype wrote `_ANCHORS` and built openings directly (the seventh critic's M1 item 1). So these private names are frozen as a published test interface, like `_v5_state()`. The implementation must provide them with exactly these meanings, and no exam case may read them:
  - `_ANCHORS` (read and write: G_ATOM clears it and stores keys before a case and clears it after);
  - `_TOOL` (read: `_TOOL[0]` is the id the step acts on);
  - `_TOOL_NAME` (read: to give the id back with `use_tool_id(t, _TOOL_NAME)` after a case in which another tool held it; `_named` is an identity test, so no other object will do);
  - `_CONSUME` and `_list` (read: the consumer whose CALL opens the measured interval, compared by identity);
  - `_Opening` and `_Core` (read: G_ATOM builds objects with `_Opening.__new__(_Opening)` and `_Core.__new__(_Core)`, assigns only the slots the steps read, `core`, `frame` and `armed` on an opening and `flags` on a core, and checks the four member descriptors; no constructor signature is frozen). Revision 8 (N7) fixes what an opening's `frame` slot holds: a fresh `object()` per opening. It is hashable by identity, owned by no section and on no stack, so it serves as an anchor key as a frame does;
  - `_EVENTS5`, `_CALLBACKS5` and `_LOCAL` (read: to restore styxx's callbacks after a case and to pass local event masks);
  - `_LOST` (read: `len(_LOST)` is part of the observable state, because `_register` counts inside its call).

  Anything else G_ATOM needs it takes from `_v5_faultpoints()` (the five step functions' code), the public API (one `with coverage_trace(EXP): pass` binds `_MON`, takes the id and registers the callbacks before the first case) and `sys.monitoring` (another tool taking the id, events and callbacks read and written directly). `rev7/steps7.py` and `rev7/atom7.py` exercise exactly this interface, and so do revision 8's `rev8/steps8.py` and `rev8/atom8.py`. Revision 8's CALL criterion compares callees with the builtins `map`, `filter`, `itertools.chain`, `itertools.compress`, `itertools.repeat`, `itertools.tee` and `itertools.chain.from_iterable` themselves, which the binding check guarantees the vocabulary names are (M0), so the interface does not grow.

### M11. Scoring and check_metrics
- **`_check_coverage` step 1 (NO_TRACE).** Accepts a result iff `issubclass(type(result), dict)`, and reads it only with `dict.get`. The message is split three ways: "the result is a `<type>`, not a dict"; "no 'coverage_trace' key"; "'coverage_trace' is a `<type>`, not an exact dict (e.g. loaded with object_pairs_hook)".
- **Step 2 (WRONG_TRACER).** `type(t) is not str or t != _TRACER_ID`.
- **Step 3 (BAD_TRACE).** Every clause tests the exact type before any hash, membership or comparison. `end` is tested with `type(end) is not str or end not in _ENDS`; the same applies to `gates_sha256` (exact str), every dict key, and every note or problem string before its regex. The note and problem code sets are the /3 sets.
- **Steps 4–9 unchanged in order.** STALE_TRACE, TARGET_SET, BAD_COUNT, recorded problem, SECTION_ABSENT, NOT_EXERCISED. Coverage is checked for **every declaring gate, whatever its bar** (X122). The recorded-problem step has one site per recorded code (G7), with CUT_MOVED, REENTRANT and MACHINERY_BUSY added and FOREIGN_PROFILER removed.
- **NOT_EXERCISED.** The fixed sentence reads: "work on other threads, pools, executors, child processes or asyncio tasks is credited only to a section that work opens itself; generator and coroutine objects created before the trace are never credited; a call counts only when it returns, yields or raises from inside its body while the tracer is active".
- **score() on metrics.** The metric guard wraps `float(_v)`: an OverflowError becomes `GateSpecError("gate G: metric … is too large for a float")`. This is a v3-path error and carries no [V5] code.
- **check_metrics.** It never raises for a JSON-shaped result: dicts, lists, str, bool, None, floats and ints of any size.
  - `smoke = dict.get(result, 'smoke')` if the result is a dict subclass. It is truthy only for exact bool, int, float or str with a truthy value, so no user `__bool__` runs.
  - Usability is `issubclass(type(v), (int, float)) and type(v) is not bool and _finite(v)`. `_finite` catches **OverflowError only**, so asynchronous exceptions still propagate. The note reads "int too large for a float".
  - `present` uses the NO_TRACE test.
  - The coverage note is "smoke run" only when the trace is absent. Otherwise it is `str(e)` with " (smoke run: score(smoke=True) does not read coverage)" appended when smoke, so it always starts with the refusal's `[V5:CODE]`.
  - For in-process results carrying user objects, it can run two kinds of user method: a `__float__` override on an int or float subclass, and the `__eq__` of a str-subclass key stored in a user dict whose hash collides with a key it looks up (`rev1/p2_dict_collide.py`). The second is contrived: a JSON-loaded result has exact-str keys.

### Cost
The figures below come from the designs' prototypes. The implementation re-measures them in H4, and they are reported, not gated.

| operation | figure |
|---|---|
| non-target code, anywhere | about ×1.0 (local events only: D1 t_cost) |
| per hit | Entry walk plus confirmation, plus `_cut_ok()` on two bindings and one `_get_running_loop()`. In the event-path model a hit of a trivial target costs about 4.2–4.3 µs at depth 5 and 12.1 µs at depth 60, which is ×116–×117 and ×300–×356 an untraced trivial call (`rev2/p11_cost.py`, 3.12.3 and 3.13.12; the second critic measured 3.6–12 µs). That is far above D1's "×8 at depth 11", which was relative to a non-trivial target; for small hot targets the per-hit figure is what matters. `_cut_ok()` costs about 0.24 µs; revision 2's second binding adds 0.16–0.42 µs per hit in the model; `_get_running_loop()` costs 0.02–0.03 µs (critic2/t_cost.py, p11) |
| walk | linear in stack depth per hit, so a declared target recursing to depth n costs O(n²) (NOTE recursion-walk-quadratic: about 3 s at depth 8000 under v5e; to be re-measured) |
| global PY_UNWIND callback | Only while some section is open (revision 2). Inside a section, an exception unwinding through 1 frame costs ×1.43–×1.57 and through 6 frames ×2.01–×2.23, anywhere in the process; it grows with the frames unwound. Outside every section, ×0.99–×1.04: no callback (`rev2/p11_cost.py`; critic2/t_unwind_cost.py measured ×1.49–×2.17 under revision 1's always-on scope) |
| section open and close | 2.5–3.2 µs more than revision 1 for the PY_UNWIND toggle, plus one re-instrumentation of each distinct code object run in the section, about 0.05–0.10 µs each (`rev2/p4_unwind_scope.py`). The pass-through's `os.getpid()` adds about 0.14 µs (p8). Revision 3's `_commit` call and reordered `_unwind_off` add at most 1.3–2.4 µs per section in the model, whose test hooks inflate both paths (`rev3/b1_commit_cost.py`: 8.11 → 10.47 µs on 3.12.3, 6.09 → 7.36 µs on 3.13.12). Revision 4, uncontended: the clearer's announcement and withdrawal (`_txn()`, a store, a pop) add 0.46–0.56 µs to every close that clears, and the re-check with the `_CLEARING` test 0.04–0.05 µs to every open, while the open drops one `_unwind_on()` (0.22–0.23 µs): net +0.29 µs (3.13.12) and +0.38 µs (3.12.3) per section (`rev4/b4b_announce_cost.py`). In the model, whose test hooks and Python-level token class inflate the revision-4 path, a section costs 1.1–2.3 µs more than under revision 3 (`rev4/b4_cost.py`, two runs per version: 7.46–7.97 → 9.06–9.71 µs on 3.12.3, 6.30–6.47 → 7.73–7.81 µs on 3.13.12). Contended: with 4 threads opening and closing sections of one tracer in a tight loop at a 1 µs switch interval, 0.18–0.27% of opens waited in `_await_clearers`, about 0.7 ms on average and at most 3.2 ms. With a pure-Python profile function on every thread, 0.70–0.82% waited, 0.55–0.61 ms on average, at most 2.1 ms. Every call was counted and no MONITOR_LOST was noted (`rev4/b4c_contention.py`). Revision 5 replaces all of that. No open ever waits, so contention costs nothing extra. Each one-call step builds about ten iterator objects, 1.0–1.2 µs for `_unwind_on` and 1.8–2.9 µs for a pop with a clear, measured bare (`rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py` part F). In the model a section costs 1.6–3.2 µs more than under revision 4. With no other section open: 9.03–9.27 → 10.76–11.48 µs on 3.12.3, and 7.24–7.53 → 9.16–9.18 µs on 3.13.12. With another section open: 4.89–5.17 → 7.79–8.05 and 5.35–5.71 → 7.51–7.80 µs (`rev5/b5_cost.py`, two runs per version) |
| `coverage_trace()` | imports asyncio the first time it is called. The first call, and any E2 that meets a code new to the cut, scans `gc.get_referrers` once per new code: 1.6–2.1 ms with 100k extra tracked objects, 14–17 ms with 1M (`rev2/p2_shared_cut.py`). The third critic's re-run measured 4.0–4.5 ms and 20.1–21.5 ms on the same probe (N9): the figure depends on the machine and its load, and it is reported, not gated |
| enter or exit | reconciliation is linear in live mints; CLONE_ALIVE's `gc.get_referrers` scan runs only when the refcount gate fires (about 17–19 ms on a 1M-object heap under v5e) |

### What is removed from v5e
- `_LOCK`, `_ACTIVE`, `_STOP`, `_THREADS`, `_EPOCH` and the counters;
- the profile hook `_hook`, with its self-removal and last-close removal;
- every `sys.setprofile` call; styxx never calls `sys.setprofile`, `threading.setprofile`, `sys.settrace` or `threading.settrace` (G_HYG, revision 7);
- FOREIGN_PROFILER, PROFILER_LOST and THREAD_HOP;
- the `_own_dict` try/except;
- `isinstance` dispatch in resolution;
- the `__wrapped__`-based cache body;
- `weakref.finalize`;
- tracer id `/2`: committed v5e traces keep their verdicts as history and refuse WRONG_TRACER under v5f (X95b);
- the v5e exam as a v5f exam: v5f needs its own prereg and frozen runner.

---

## Exception-safety model

**Threat.** An exception can land at any bytecode of the machinery. That includes a function's entry (RESUME) and a loop back-edge. On 3.13, an exception at a back-edge escapes the enclosing `try` table without running `finally` or a with-block's exit (CPython #130279; round-4 found-after item). `rev1/p11_backedge_timer.py` measured this under SIGALRM on 3.13.12. The finally or with-exit was skipped in 400/400 trials for a `while` loop and 188/400 for a `for` loop whose body ends in an `if`. 3.11.15 and 3.12.3 skipped 0. On 3.12.3 the same skip appears at back-edges of code that carries INSTRUCTION, JUMP or BRANCH events (p12, p13), and at the throw-path back-edge of an `await` (p19). **Rule:** no machinery state may depend on a `finally` or with-exit running across a loop, `await` loops included. It also includes the machinery's own callbacks: a sys.monitoring callback is Python code with eval-breaker checks.

- **Sources:** signal-handler exceptions, KeyboardInterrupt, RecursionError, MemoryError, audit hooks, and a foreign tool raising at an event of one of our frames. That foreign tool can be a lower-id monitoring tool, or a settrace/setprofile function on the thread. Revision 5 (N2) lists every machinery site that raises an audit event, where a raising audit hook is such a source (revision 8's wider self-audit, W2, adds the sites it missed):
  - `object.__setattr__` at every `__code__` write (E4 and `_retire`);
  - `sys._getframe` in `_txn()`, at E0, at X1 and in every `_locked`, and in every callback (`sys._getframe(1)`). A hook that raises on it makes every hit raise out of its callback (L-DELIVERY);
  - (revision 8 self-audit, W2) `sys._getframe` in `_run` and `_run_async`, the argument of `_open` (M7), at every open;
  - (revision 8 self-audit, W2) `builtins.id` at every `id()` call, and `object.__getattr__` at every read of a frame's `f_code` (both raised on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12: `verify8/probe_open_audit.py`, 23 events in one `run()` on `rev8/mech8.py`). The machinery makes such calls in `_open`'s step-5 walk, in every callback (the `_MINTED` lookup, the hit's walk, the pending key and the publication's `id(code) in h.by_code` test), in `_alive`'s liveness test (so inside every reconciliation that tests a holder), and at the mint and retire keys inside E4 and X5. Revision 5's list named neither. Where they land: at open, all before `_open`'s append (steps 1–5), so a hook that raises there propagates out of `run()` (or the coroutine's first step) before any append or store, and fn does not run (V71); in a callback, a raising hook makes that hit raise out of its callback (L-DELIVERY), and one raised at publication loses that one credit, inside I6; inside a transaction, they are further sites of the kind "Audit hooks and slow owners" and I5 describe, which the model's lock-taking and greenlet configurations stand for, and G_FI faults every instruction that makes them;
  - `sys._current_frames` in `_alive`, called by `_acquire` on contention and by every reconciliation's holder test;
  - `gc.get_referrers` in E2's sharing scan and in CLONE_ALIVE;
  - (revision 6, B1) `sys.monitoring.register_callback`, five times inside every `_register`: at E4 (`_ensure_tool`: a first acquisition, an adoption or a rebinding), at M3 step 0 (`_reclaim`, in any transaction after a free), and at X5, which runs on **every exit**, inside the robust mutex. A hook that raises there cuts the registration short (M7, "The registration") and propagates out of the transaction (M5's heading). Revision 5's N2 row said this list named every site; it omitted this one.

  No one-call step raises an audit event: `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take` and `_set_local` call only `sys.monitoring` functions that raise none (`critic6/a1_audit_monitoring.py`), and G_ATOM checks every gate outcome of each (`rev6/atom6.py`). `_register` is not a one-call step. So revision 5 removed revision 4's two open- and close-path sites, `_txn()` in every clearing `_unwind_off` and `_alive` in every waiting open (the fifth critic's `critic5/c5_audit_events.py`). The close path raises no audit event. The open path raises audit events only before `_open`'s append: `_run`'s `sys._getframe()`, and step 5's `f_code` reads and `id()` calls. `_commit` and everything after the append raise none. (Revision 8 self-audit, W2: this said "the open and close paths still raise no audit event", which `_run`'s frame read and the walk contradict; V71.) `sys._current_frames` costs 3–3.8 µs per call at 51 threads (c5).
- **The GIL is assumed.** That is why free-threaded builds refuse.
- **Commits are single C operations:** dict setitem, `setdefault`, `pop` and `get` with str, int, frame, function, `_Core` or `_Txn` keys (identity or str hashing, so no Python `__hash__` or `__eq__` runs); `list.append`; `set.clear`; one STORE_ATTR or STORE_SUBSCR; one call of `get_tool`, `get_events`, `set_events`, `set_local_events` or `use_tool_id`; or, from revision 5, one one-call step (M7), which is one C call however many operations it makes inside. A `register_callback` call is *not* such a commit (revision 6): its audit hook runs Python code before its exchange, so `_register` is a sequence of five gated exchanges with Python code possible before each, and M7 states what it guarantees. The one comparison with a foreign object, another tool's name from `get_tool`, is an identity test (`_named`, revision 5), so no foreign `__eq__` runs. The machinery's correctness arguments never use the absence of an eval-breaker check between two bytecodes. They use the order of these operations on each thread, and the fact that nothing runs inside the C call of a one-call step, which calls no Python code and raises no audit event (revision 5; revision 6 restricts the claim to those steps).
- **No `try … finally`, no `with` statement and no threading lock** exist anywhere in the machinery, with one exception: `_run`/`_run_async`'s single `try … finally` around fn.
  - `_run`'s try body is `_commit(o)` and the call of fn, and contains no loop; `_commit` has none either (revision 3). Revision 5: nothing `_commit` or `_detach` calls has a loop either. Revision 4's `_await_clearers` loop is deleted, and a one-call step iterates in C, where there is no bytecode back-edge (`rev5/spec_rev5_eventpath.py`: no backward jump in any of them, both versions). The `finally` itself is `_detach(o, …)` and `o.frame = None`, with no loop.
  - `_run_async`'s try body is `_commit(o)` and one `await`, whose compiled SEND loop has a throw-path back-edge outside the try range (M7). No count, note, refusal or termination depends on that `finally` running. If it is skipped, the only machinery state left is a dead anchor, which exit's X3 or a reconciliation pops, and which keeps PY_UNWIND set until then (U4, L-DELIVERY scope).
  - The only `try … except` clauses are the two user-code sites (the import and PEP 562), which chain into UNRESOLVED, and exit's two `except GateSpecError` clauses around `_locked`, at X0 and X5.

### Invariants preserved by every prefix of every transition
- **I1: no over-credit.** A count is added to opening O for a frame F of M_T only if all three hold:
  - (a) at F's entry event, O's anchor was registered and met on F's live chain before the cut, the running loop was `O.loop`, O.core held F's mint, and `id(M_T)` was in `O.core.by_code`;
  - (b) F then returned, yielded, or unwound at an offset other than its entry offset;
  - (c) at publication, `id(M_T)` was still in `O.core.by_code`, which exit empties in its first store after the claim.

  A registered anchor whose frame died (an interrupted close, or a skipped `_run_async` `finally`) can never be on a live chain. A frame killed at its entry never publishes (X135, H8).
- **I2: mint integrity.** `fn.__code__ is m.code` only while m is registered with at least one holder that is active or has a live token. The exceptions are inside E4, X5 and `_retire`, and there the next reconciliation repairs the state: holder before register, register before events, events before install; restore before unregister, each step checked by identity.
- **I3: reconciled registries.** After any reconciliation:
  - every registered mint has only live holders;
  - no mint without a live holder stays registered;
  - a retired mint has no local events and no pending entries;
  - global PY_UNWIND is clear if no anchor is registered (M7, U3). Revision 5 makes this hold in every state, not only after a reconciliation, unless an outside party set the event or freed the id. Every reconciliation ends with `_unwind_off(None)`, which repairs that case. An anchor whose section's body may run always has the event set (U1). Revision 2 said "set only while some anchor is registered; a close or reconciliation repairs a stale set", and both halves were false (the third critic's MF1, MF2). Revision 3's version of this clause was false after an open that raced its trace's exit, which left `_ANCHORS[None]` registered for the rest of the process (the fourth critic's B1); revision 4 closes that (At open, steps 8 and 9);
  - (revision 4) no registered anchor belongs to a dead core (the prune of cores reached from anchors);
  - no freed, unowned tool id still carries styxx's events or callbacks: step 0 re-takes it (revision 2).
- **I4: single finalisation.** Each opening is finalised exactly once, by `o.fin.setdefault`. Every cleanup is idempotent and unconditional. After its core exits or is pruned, an opening holds no anchor, once its opener has left `_commit` (revision 4: an opener that stores after X3 or a prune detaches itself at step 8, and one that stored before is popped by X3 or the prune, which read the same `o.frame` key; the model check's END property, fault-free, on every pair and triple).
- **I5: no hang, and the only wait is the robust mutex (revision 5).** The mutex serializes transactions (enter and exit). A dead owner is stolen; a live owner on the same thread gives REENTRANT instead of a wait; a live owner on another thread is waited on for at most 10 s, by a clock bound at import (MACHINERY_BUSY). No threading lock exists, so a skipped release cannot hang anyone. Opens, closes and callbacks never wait for anything (V68, revision 7). Revision 4's opener wait is deleted.

  One cycle of waits can still form, and only through user code. The mutex owner runs user code inside its transaction that waits for a thread, and that thread is itself waiting for the mutex. Examples: an audit hook on a `__code__` write, or on `sys.monitoring.register_callback` (raised five times inside every exit's X5; revision 6, B1), or a finalizer, that takes a lock another thread holds around `cov.__exit__()` or `with coverage_trace(...)`. The waiter's bound breaks the cycle. After 10 s it raises MACHINERY_BUSY, its `with` releases the lock, and the owner proceeds (over-blocking #20).

  Revision 4's claim here, "a clearer never waits for anything, so no cycle of waits exists inside the machinery", was false. A section opened inside a clearer's window waited for other threads' clearers, and a clearer's window could run user code that waited for an opener's thread. Two such threads waited on each other, or on a user lock, until the bound (the fifth critic's MF1; `critic5/c8_nested_wait_cycle.py`, `critic5/c3_wait_cycle_user_lock.py`). `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py` models user locks, re-entrant sections and transactions, and gives the bounded waits a timeout step. Its HANG property is real: with a bound removed (`hang` mode) it finds revision 4's cycles as true deadlocks, and revision 5's mutex cycle likewise. With the bounds, revision 5 has no reachable state where live work exists and nothing can step, and no cycle except the mutex's (Revision 5 table).
- **I6: the envelope.** A fault inside the machinery can change a trace's record only within these bounds:
  - counts ≤ the fault-free counts;
  - each `end` ∈ {fault-free end, `"raised"`, `"open"`};
  - notes ⊆ fault-free notes ∪ {OPEN_AT_EXIT}. No fault, no instrument and no re-entrant code clears the event under a registered anchor (U1), so a fault cannot cause `UNWIND_LOST`. Revision 3 had to allow MONITOR_LOST here whenever an instrument runs Python code inside `_unwind_off`'s test-and-clear, the id-5 injector armed on its code included. Revision 4 removed that exception, and revision 5 removes the last way re-entrant code could clear under an anchor (R19);
  - problems ⊆ fault-free problems ∪ {REENTRANT, MACHINERY_BUSY when the fault itself enters or exits a tracer inside a transition};
  - or else `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE or TRACE_ACTIVE.

  A fault never causes any of these: over-credit; a hang; a mint left installed after the next reconciliation, unless its holder is a live zombie (L-ZOMBIE); or a poisoned later trace.

  A fault never swallows an exception, with one exception. An asynchronous exception delivered inside the PY_UNWIND callback, which exists only while some section is open (revision 2), replaces the exception being unwound; its `__context__` is None, so the original is lost (L-DELIVERY; `synth/p_deliver.py`, re-run in revision 1 on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 with the same result). The only other conversions are at the two documented user-code sites, the declared-module import and a PEP 562 `__getattr__`, each giving a chained UNRESOLVED.

### Per-transition prefixes
- **ENTER.**
  - Before E4: nothing global has changed.
  - Inside E4 (including an audit hook refusing the install, or refusing a `register_callback` inside `_register`, revision 6): the core is a holder without `'active'`, and its entering token dies with the exception. The next reconciliation prunes it and retires the mints it alone held. A registration cut short leaves the id named ours with some callbacks not re-registered and `_TOOL[0]` unchanged. If `_TOOL[0]` was None, the next enter adopts the id and registers again (Tool acquisition; revision 8 self-audit, W7: X154e). If it was set (a reclaim's registration), the callbacks not re-registered are the ones left on the freed id, which are styxx's own unless an intermediate owner replaced them; the next exit's X5 registration repairs them, and notes MONITOR_LOST and counts the repair (revision 7) if any was not styxx's. The re-take itself is not counted (F25; X157b, X157c).
  - Between E4 and E5: the same.
  - At the facade's return, after E5: a zombie.
- **EXIT.**
  - Before X1: a zombie. `record()` refuses TRACE_ACTIVE, and `__exit__` again completes it.
  - After X1: `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE. The exiting token dies, so the next reconciliation prunes the core, detaches its openings and retires its mints. Partial X3 or X5 work is completed idempotently. A signal at X3's loop back-edge on 3.13 is an "after X1" fault (X140).
- **OPEN.**
  - Before the append: nothing has changed.
  - After the append, before the `try` (the rest of `_open`, and `_run` up to its `try`): an unregistered, inert opening that exit finalises as `"open"`. The event is untouched.
  - Inside `_commit` (revision 3), at any point, including inside `_unwind_on` (a fault lands before or after its one call, never inside): the fault is inside `_run`'s `try`, so the `finally`'s `_detach` pops the anchor if it was stored (by `o.frame`, which only this `finally` releases, after the pop) and clears the event in the same call if no anchor is left. The opening ends `"raised"` and fn never runs. `o.armed` is still False, so no `UNWIND_LOST`. A second fault in that `finally` before its `_unwind_off` call leaves a dead anchor: until the trace's X3 or, if the trace has already exited (the open raced its exit), until the next reconciliation prunes the dead core it belongs to (X146d). Revision 2 ran these steps before the `try`. A fault after the first set left the event on with no anchor, and a fault after the commit left a dead anchor. Either kept PY_UNWIND on outside every section until the trace exited, and the next close did not clear it (`rev3/c7r3_stale_unwind_after_open_fault.py`: 33,552–51,880 lost handlers in 300,000 iterations after one fault, against 0).
- **CLOSE.**
  - Before the claim: exit claims it as `("open", OPEN_AT_EXIT)`.
  - After the claim: the cleanups are redone.
  - `_run_async`'s `finally` skipped (a signal at the `await`'s throw-path back-edge on 3.12; M7): the same as "before the claim". The anchor frame is dead.
  - After the claim, before `_unwind_off`'s call (revision 3 states it): a dead anchor. It keeps PY_UNWIND set until the trace's X3 or its pruning (U4). Scope only, no count.
  - After `_unwind_off`'s call (revision 5): the pop and the clear happened together, so the event is left as it should be. Revision 4's window between a pop and its clear is gone. Exit's X3 later re-detaches this opening if `o.frame = None` did not run. The anchor test in `_detach` keeps that from setting a false `UNWIND_LOST` (X143c).
  - After `_detach`, before `o.frame = None` (revision 4): the opening keeps its anchor frame until the core is dropped (M1). No registry is affected.
- **Callbacks.** Their only writes are `m.pend[...]` stores and pops, single-key increments and clone flags. A fault loses at most that one credit and can never add one: an entry killed before its store has no pending entry, and a pending entry killed at its entry offset never publishes. A pending entry whose confirmation never arrives stays until `_retire` clears it. It keeps its frame, and that frame's callers, alive until then (L-MONITOR).
- **Reconcile and retire.** Idempotent. A second fault inside them is repaired by the next transaction. The prune of cores reached from anchors is idempotent too: it pops only what belongs to a dead core, and `_detach` of an already-finalised opening repeats only cleanups.
- **Tool acquisition.** The callbacks are registered before `_TOOL[0]` is stored. A tool left named ours with missing callbacks (a fault, including a raising audit hook, inside `_register`) is adopted and re-registered at the next enter when `_TOOL[0]` is None (X154e, revision 8 self-audit, W7). When `_TOOL[0]` is set (the fault was in a reclaim's registration), the id is kept as it is, and the next exit's X5 registration repairs it (revision 7 corrects revision 6's "at the next enter", which held only for `_TOOL[0]` None; X157b).
  - Revision 5 claimed that no race with another tool can make styxx raise or write on a foreign id. Revision 6 (B1): no race can make styxx *raise*, and no race can make any write other than a `_register` exchange land on a foreign id. A `_register` exchange lands on another tool's id when an audit hook on `sys.monitoring.register_callback`, running inside that call before its exchange, lets another tool take the id; at most one exchange per registration does so, and the registration reports it (M7, "The registration"). Revision 4 had three such races:
    - `_ensure_tool` and `_reclaim` tested `get_tool(id) is None`, then called `use_tool_id`. Another tool taking the id between the two made `use_tool_id` raise ValueError. Now `_take` is one call (X155).
    - A `free_tool_id` landing between `_ours()` and `set_events` made that call raise ValueError out of `run()` or its close.
    - A free *and re-take* by another tool in that window made the call succeed on the foreign id. It cleared that tool's events or replaced its mask (the fifth critic's N1; `critic5/c4_toolid_retake_race.py`, both versions).

    Every write of events or local events is now a one-call step gated on the name (X154), and the one read on the close path, `get_events`, never raises. Revision 5 said the same of `register_callback`; revision 6 corrects it as above. What remains: the one-exchange registration residual (L-MONITOR), and a *hostile* tool that frees styxx's id and re-takes it under styxx's own name object, which it can read with `get_tool`. The identity test cannot tell that tool from styxx (L-MONITOR). No library does this.
- **At-fork.** It runs in the child's only thread, and every step is idempotent. Only its last step runs user code (M9, revision 2). The pass-through reads `os.getpid()`, so it holds whatever prefix of the handler ran.

### Residuals, disclosed
- **L-ZOMBIE.** A tracer whose exit never began stays active.
  - Causes: an exception at `__exit__`'s entry; a foreign tool raising at `__enter__`'s return event; or a harness's own `with` body ending in a loop on 3.13, where CPython #130279 skips `__exit__`.
  - `record()` refuses TRACE_ACTIVE.
  - What persists: its equal-code mints stay installed, with their local events. From revision 2 the process-wide PY_UNWIND callback persists only while one of its sections is still open. A zombie whose sections closed normally leaves no global callback. That covers the common case: the fault that made it a zombie happened after the harness's `cov.run` calls returned.
  - How long: until `__exit__()` is called, or until the facade becomes unreachable and some later enter or exit in the process reconciles. With no later transaction, that is until the process exits.
  - If a second fault leaves a stale anchor, the anchor frame's `f_back` chain can keep the facade reachable.
- **L-DELIVERY.** See Stated limits. Exceptions surfacing inside styxx callbacks take effect at that event.
- **Re-entry.** A finalizer, audit hook or signal handler that enters or exits a tracer inside another enter or exit on the same thread gets REENTRANT. In a finalizer this is printed as "Exception ignored" and that tracer fails.
- **Audit hooks and slow owners.** An audit hook that blocks inside a transaction holds the mutex, and other threads' transactions raise MACHINERY_BUSY after 10 s. Audit hooks run inside a transaction at `__code__` writes (E4's install and `_retire`'s restore), and, from revision 6's correction, at the five `sys.monitoring.register_callback` events of `_register` at X5 on **every exit**, at E4 and at a reclaim (B1). (Revision 8 self-audit, W5: this said "two sites". They also run at `_alive`'s `sys._current_frames`, `id()` and `f_code` reads in every reconciliation that tests a holder, which is the first step of every transaction, and at the `id()` of the mint and retire keys; see Sources.) If what the hook blocks on is a lock held by a thread that is itself waiting for the mutex, the two form a cycle. The waiter's bound breaks it after 10 s (I5; `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py`, `with L: exit(X) || exit(Y)+[lock]`: a true deadlock with the bound removed, MACHINERY_BUSY with it; the configuration's lock-taking nested program stands for either site). A hook that raises at `register_callback` inside X5 makes `__exit__` propagate it (M5).
- **Greenlets.** Modelled, and from revision 6 exercised on a real greenlet library in one shape (greenlet 3.5.6, `rev6/w6_greenlet.py`, X137f); from revision 8 in a second, a replaced callback's finalizer that switches inside X5 (X158d; revision 8 self-audit, W6). A greenlet switch made from inside a transaction (by a finalizer, audit hook or signal handler) makes the mutex owner look dead, so it is stolen and two transactions interleave. In `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py` (`greenlet: [exit(X), rec] || open(X)`) no property is violated when they do, because every reconciliation step is idempotent, and revision 6's model adds outside parties to that configuration with the same result (`rev6/m6_modelcheck.py`, `ext+greenlet: [exit(X), rec] || open(X)`). Only a transaction is affected. A switch cannot land inside a one-call step, so the event path has no greenlet exposure (`greenlet: [close(X), open(X)] || open(Y)`: revisions 5 and 6 clean; revision 4 has U1 violations and 2 end states whose loss is silent). A switch *can* land inside `_register`, from an audit hook on `register_callback` (revision 6, B1); the registration's per-exchange gate and owner read bound what follows (M7). A switch inside a reclaim, after its re-take and before its `_LOST` count, lets another greenlet's exit run its MONITOR_LOST test before the count; a call lost while the id was freed is then noted only by the close's ungated event read (F14, X137f), which revision 6 keeps for that reason.
- **R19 and the greenlet clearer (revision 4) are gone.** Revision 4 disclosed two shapes in which re-entrant code or a stack switch inside `_unwind_off`'s window let a clear land under a registered anchor:
  - a `run_async` section opened on the same thread inside the window and left suspended;
  - a greenlet switch inside the window.

  The fifth critic showed that R19 needs no second thread (`critic5/c2_r19_same_thread_resume.py`), and that its loss is silent once any other section opens before the blinded close (`critic5/c2b_r19_silent.py`). Revision 5's clear is one call, so there is no window. `rev5/w5_witnesses.py` opens the suspended section at every point of a close: `{t:1}`, no note, at every point. The loss reappears only under a split `_unwind_off` mutant.
- **Interpreter shutdown.** Exit during finalization runs inline (there is no helper thread). It is untested.

**Evidence and what must be re-run.** D1's crash sweep, run with its monitoring adapter, injected an exception at every machinery bytecode: 6337 points on 3.12 and 6283 on 3.13, 0 failures on D1's invariants C1–C8, with the C7 check using live facades. v5f's C1–C9 are defined under G_FI (revision 3, MF5); they restate D1's in terms of the public API and `_v5_state()`. D1's mutants M1–M8 were detected. That machine published at PY_START and gated on per-thread sets. The v5f machine must pass the same sweep, extended to two threads, `run_async`, generator targets and hopped coroutine sections (G_FI). Injection uses sys.monitoring INSTRUCTION events on the code objects from `_v5_faultpoints()`. The callbacks' own bytecode is not reachable by that injector while it runs as a callback, because events are not delivered inside callbacks (synth/p_syn1.py). Revision 2 (BF1, M8) reaches it another way: the frozen direct-call driver `cover_driver_v5f.py` calls the five callback-only functions outside any callback, as `FunctionType(code, vars(styxx.protocol))` built from `_v5_faultpoints()` code objects. Under the driver, a LINE tool, settrace and the INSTRUCTION injector all see their lines and back-edges (`rev2/p5_cover_driver.py`: 0 lines seen as real callbacks, 38 of the 49 body lines seen under a five-call driver, with back-edges in `_outcome` and `_publish` hit). The callbacks are also covered by X135/R16 (a lower-id tool raising at their events) and by the G_SIG floods.

## Version policy

| interpreter | tracing | scoring | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| CPython 3.9, 3.10 | **refused** (UNSUPPORTED_VERSION at `coverage_trace()` and `__enter__`) | works | There is no sys.monitoring. 3.10's `call_trampoline` runs FastToLocals/LocalsToFast around every Python profile callback. That reverts closure writes made by other threads and keeps deleted locals alive (round-4 BLOCKER), and no Python hook avoids it. D1 also found `importlib.import_module` hanging workers under SIGALRM on 3.10 (8/8). The "3.9 expected like 3.10" line is deleted. |
| CPython 3.11 | **refused** | works | There is no sys.monitoring. Any Python-level profile hook makes every C call on a sectioned thread skippable, and it leaked the harness's own lock in about 7% of SIGALRM timeouts (see Decisions). This is py310-class corruption of the traced program. Refusing it costs the lab's default interpreter; the P1 retro and the exam run on 3.12/3.13. |
| CPython 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (GIL builds; `_VERIFIED`) | **supported**, one mechanism | works | sys.monitoring local events on minted code. The global PY_UNWIND is set only while some section is open (M7, "The unwind scope"; revision 3 corrects revision 1's "while a mint exists" here, MF8). No profile or trace function is ever installed. These are the *verified interpreters*: the patch levels on which G_ATOM passed (M0). A build of the same patch level other than the one G_ATOM ran on is accepted but unverified (M0, N2). |
| every other CPython 3.12.x and 3.13.x (GIL builds) | **refused** (UNSUPPORTED_VERSION, revision 6) | works | Not a patch level on which the one-call premise was verified. A patch level joins `_VERIFIED` only with a passing G_ATOM run committed beside it (M0, Process gates). Revision 6 said results on other patch levels "are reported as unvalidated"; under M0 no tracing result can be produced there (revision 7, M2). |
| CPython 3.14+, free-threaded builds (`Py_GIL_DISABLED`), other implementations | **refused** | works | Refused until `Handle._run`, the frame model, sys.monitoring semantics (the RESUME eval-breaker order, PY_UNWIND offsets) and the GIL atomicity argument are re-validated. |

**One mechanism, no version-keyed cases.** Every exam case has the same expected outcome on the two verified interpreters, 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (G_XVER). No enumerated difference remains. D3's probes and the synthesizer's probes checked the three candidates below:

- **Eager tasks.** `eager_task_factory` exists on both versions, so X82 and X83 are unkeyed.
- **cProfile.** It runs on sys.monitoring on both versions and coexists (V33).
- **The RESUME eval-breaker order.** By D3's reading of its measurements, 3.12's INSTRUMENTED_RESUME checks the eval breaker *after* the instrumentation call, and 3.13 checks it before. So publish-at-entry is over-credited under signals on 3.12 only. D3 p3 measured 181 body-less credits in 8 s on 3.12 and 0 on 3.13. The difference is designed out by confirmation (0 on both, D3 p4).

A lower-id tool raising at PY_START makes publish-at-entry unsound on *both* versions. Judge 2's cr_det3 gave a false PASS on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12; that is X135.

**The refused interpreters in the exam.** On 3.10 and 3.11 the exam runs the refusal cases (X37) and every scoring-only case with a prebuilt `/3` trace. Their outcomes must be identical to those on the verified interpreters. Revision 8 (N8) pins them as the verified ones are pinned: CPython 3.10.20 and 3.11.15, the builds these probes ran on (`main, Mar 3 2026`, GCC 13.3.0, both). The prereg records each one's build identifier (`sys.version`, `platform.python_build()`, `platform.python_compiler()`), and the exam runs on exactly those patch levels. They are refused interpreters, so this pins the exam's inputs, not a support claim. No real unverified 3.12 or 3.13 patch level is required: X37b pins M0's patch-level refusal on a verified interpreter by replacing `sys.version_info` after import (revision 7, M2 and N6). Any other interpreter the exam machine has is not an exam interpreter.

**sys.monitoring coexistence.**
- **Tool ids.** styxx takes tool id 4, or 3 if 4 is taken, under the per-copy name `_TOOL_NAME`, and holds it until the process exits. Other tools asking for that id get CPython's ValueError. If ids 3 and 4 are both held under other names, enter refuses MONITOR_BUSY. That includes another loaded copy of `styxx.protocol`.
- **A freed id.** If a party calls `free_tool_id` on styxx's id, CPython 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 leave its events and callbacks in place (`rev1/p3_free.py`), so the trace keeps counting. `set_events` on the freed id raises ValueError, so nothing can clear it without re-taking it (`rev2/p3_free_reclaim.py`). Revision 2 (M3): while the id has no owner, the next transaction re-takes it under `_TOOL_NAME`, which clobbers nobody, and clears its events. That transaction is normally this trace's own exit. Exit notes MONITOR_LOST. In p3, after a free inside a section and a normal exit, revision 1 left 1,000 unwind callbacks for 1,000 unrelated raises, and revision 2 left 0 with the id's events cleared. If another tool takes the freed id first, styxx never touches it, and styxx's PY_UNWIND callback keeps firing there for the events styxx had set, until that tool changes them (p3: 1,000 of 1,000). That is disclosed under L-MONITOR and in the R2-B3 row.
- **Tools that coexist.** cProfile (PROFILER_ID 2), coverage.py's sysmon core (id 1) and ctrace core (settrace), pdb (settrace), and pure-Python setprofile/settrace tools. D3's t_coexist ran coverage.py 7.16 and pdb. yappi, pyinstrument and debugpy (id 0) are expected to coexist but were not executed. Several of these run Python code between two machinery bytecodes: a pure-Python profiler, cProfile with a Python timer, CALL and INSTRUCTION tools, opcode tracing, and BRANCH tools on 3.12 (M7). From revision 4 no machinery property depends on that not happening (U1). Revision 5 does not rely on a wait for this either: no such tool can run inside a one-call step (`rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py`). Revision 3 lost calls and noted MONITOR_LOST when such a tool ran on a closing thread (the fourth critic's MF1). Revision 4 made openers wait for the closing thread instead (the fifth critic's MF1).

## Reason codes

Status: **new**, **changed** (trigger or text), **same**, or **retired**. Every code is a literal at its own emission site (G7) and must survive the literal-census refusal gate (G_REF).

| code | status | when it fires | effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| DECL | same | parse: `exercises` is not a non-empty list of ASCII `module:qualname`, or has duplicates | raise |
| SECTION_DECL | same (now pinned: X07b, X07c) | parse: `section` without `exercises`, or not a non-empty ASCII str | raise |
| NOTHING_DECLARED | same | `coverage_trace(exp)` when no gate declares `exercises` | raise |
| UNSUPPORTED_VERSION | **new** | `coverage_trace()` or `__enter__` on anything but CPython 3.12.3 or 3.13.12 (`_VERIFIED`; revision 6: any other patch level too) GIL builds; also any `coverage_trace()` (revision 7: every one, not only the first) at which a `sys.monitoring` function to be bound, or already bound in `_MON`, or a one-call name as the module now binds it, is not the C object it names (revision 6, M1; revision 7, M3 and N1) | raise; nothing constructed, nothing bound; only the code prefix is spec-fixed |
| REENTRY | changed | `__enter__` whose atomic claim `marks.setdefault('entering', me)` loses: a second or concurrent entry, or a retry after a failed enter | raise; always appended to problems |
| CUT_UNAVAILABLE | **new** | construction or entry: `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once` is not a plain function (mocked or C-replaced); or (revision 2) either is bound to a function whose code is new to the cut and shareable: `<locals>` in its qualname, or another FunctionType among the code's gc referrers. The gc test sees a dead function not yet collected, and does not see one moved to the permanent generation by `gc.freeze()` (revision 3, N2). The stdlib codes themselves are new to the cut at the process's first `coverage_trace()`, so a clone of `Handle._run`'s code alive then makes every `coverage_trace()` refuse while it lives (MF4, #21) | raise; nothing minted |
| UNRESOLVED | changed | entry: the import raised; the `sys.modules` entry is not a module; the name is absent with no module `__getattr__`; the module `__getattr__` raised; **its two products differ**; a class lacks the name in its whole MRO | raise; chained `from e` where an exception caused it |
| INHERITED | changed | entry: a class step whose name exists only in some class of the MRO after the first (not only the direct bases) | raise; names the defining class |
| INSTANCE_PATH | changed | entry: a module after the colon; a step whose own dict is missing, lacks the name, or is served by a Python-level `__dict__` descriptor (never called) | raise |
| NOT_A_FUNCTION | changed | entry: not FunctionType, staticmethod/classmethod of one, or an admissible C cache wrapper. **Also:** a cache wrapper whose real callee is not its own-dict `__wrapped__`; whose callee is bound by name in the module or holder; with a sibling cache wrapper of the same callee there; two different declared objects reaching one F_T | raise; the message names the remedy (`declare <name>` or `'<module>:<name>.__wrapped__'`) |
| RESERVED_TARGET | **new** | entry: F_T is `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once`, or F_T's code is a cut code | raise |
| FOREIGN_DEFINITION | changed | entry: the declared module has no exact-str, non-`.pyc` `__file__`; a visited function link is incoherent (its `co_filename` is not its module's file); or no visited link has the module's globals | raise; names the innermost `module:qualname`, or `co_filename:qualname` when the globals' `__name__` is not an exact str; a `.pyc` `__file__` gets its own text |
| MONITOR_BUSY | **new** | entry: sys.monitoring tool ids 4 and 3 are both held under names other than this copy's `_TOOL_NAME`: other tools, or another loaded copy of `styxx.protocol`; revision 6: or an id's registration was split because another tool took it inside the registration, and the other id is unusable | raise; nothing minted |
| REENTRANT | **new** | enter or exit re-entered on the same thread from inside a transition (a finalizer, audit hook or signal handler) | enter: raise. Exit: recorded |
| MACHINERY_BUSY | **new** | another thread held the machinery (the robust mutex) for more than 10 s. Revision 5: an open never waits, so it never raises this. Revision 4's open-side case is deleted | enter: raise. Exit: recorded |
| CODE_SWAPPED | changed | entry: a mint with a live holder is no longer `T.__code__`. Exit: `T.__code__ is not M_T`, checked by **every** holder; the swap is left in place | entry: raise. Exit: recorded |
| TRACE_INACTIVE | changed | `run`/`run_async` on a tracer that is not active or is exiting (step 2), including an open that races exit or a prune: its opening was claimed, or exit began, by the time it stored its anchor (step 8) | raise + recorded: appended to `core.problems` before the raise. For an open racing exit the append can land after exit's X7; `record()` shows it once the racing thread has finished (revision 4, N5; X147) |
| UNDECLARED_SECTION | changed | open: no gate declares the section, **or the section is not a str** (never compared). Str subclasses are normalized first | raise + recorded |
| NESTED_SECTION | changed (text) | open: a registered opening of the same tracer is on the opener's chain before the cut (including an eager child's first step) | raise + recorded |
| CLONE_CALLED | changed | entry event: M_T running with foreign globals; recorded by **every** holder | recorded at exit |
| CLONE_ALIVE | changed | exit: an unexplained reference to M_T and either (a) gc finds another FunctionType holding it, or (b) the gc freeze count *rose* since the mint was made and references remain that visible referrers do not explain | recorded |
| CUT_MOVED | **new** | at an entry event of minted code, or at exit: `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once` is not a function whose code is in the cut | recorded at exit |
| TRACE_ACTIVE | changed (text) | `record()` on a tracer that was never entered (its own text), inside its with-block, or a zombie | raise |
| TRACE_INCOMPLETE | changed | `record()` after an exit that began but did not finish; after a failed enter; or in a process other than the one that entered it | raise |
| NO_TRACE | changed | score: the result is not a dict subclass; the key is absent; or the trace is not an exact dict (split message) | refuse |
| WRONG_TRACER | changed | score: the tracer is not exactly the str `/3` (every `/1` and `/2` trace refuses) | refuse |
| BAD_TRACE | changed | score: any schema clause fails, with the exact type tested before any hash or compare (`end`, `gates_sha256`, keys, codes); unknown or retired note or problem codes | refuse |
| STALE_TRACE, TARGET_SET, BAD_COUNT | same | as in v5e; order pinned by X109b and X112b | refuse |
| *(recorded code)* | changed set | score: `problems` is non-empty | every declaring gate refuses with the first problem's code |
| SECTION_ABSENT | same | score: no opening of the gate's *declared* section | refuse |
| NOT_EXERCISED | changed (text) | score: a declared target is missing from the union of its section's `calls` | refuse |
| note OPEN_AT_EXIT | changed | exit: the opening was still open; also "pruned: the trace's own exit did not run" | never refuses |
| note LAZY_RESULT | changed | `run()` got an exact generator, coroutine or async generator back; new text; "(its body had not started)" when unstarted | never refuses |
| note MONITOR_LOST | **new** | exit: our tool id was freed during the trace (re-taken if unowned, revision 2) or freed and taken; a held mint's local events were changed; the global PY_UNWIND was found cleared at a close while its section was open, or by another section's `_unwind_on` (revision 5); a callback on our id was replaced; revision 6: `_TOOL[0]` was rebound, or the exit's registration was not complete (the id was taken inside it); revision 7: another trace's exit repaired a replaced callback while this trace was live, or E4 re-took our id. A free whose re-take was cut short by a raising audit hook is not noted on a trace that lost nothing (F25) | never refuses; appended to every opening of the trace |
| FOREIGN_PROFILER | **retired** | styxx never touches profilers; every profiler coexists | a `/3` trace carrying it refuses BAD_TRACE |
| note PROFILER_LOST, note THREAD_HOP | **retired** | there is no hook to lose and no post-hop blind spot | BAD_TRACE if present |
| SIGNAL_TIMER (D1), UNSTABLE_ATTRIBUTE and GENERATOR_TARGET (D2), MACHINERY_BROKEN (D3) | never adopted | — | — |
| RETIRED earlier | carried | SHARED_CODE, NO_CODE, THREAD_OUTLIVES, HOOK_FAILED, PROFILER_REPLACED, EXIT_ORDER, SECTION_CONTEXT, ALIAS, SECTION_UNFINISHED, SECTION_FORM, CARRY_OUTSIDE, NESTED_CODE, SHARED_ROUTE | — |

## Per-finding disposition (all 58 confirmed round-4 findings)

Dispositions:
- **FIXED**: a mechanism change.
- **REFUSED_NOW**: the shape refuses with a coded, remedy-bearing message, disclosed as over-blocking.
- **SPEC_TEXT_FIX**: the behaviour is kept and the text is made true.
- **DISCLOSED_LIMIT**: a new or widened limit or residual.
- **EXAM_CASE_ONLY**: the rule already holds and the exam now pins it.
- **RULE_RETIRED**: the rule no longer exists in v5f; a regression case pins the successor behaviour.

### Blockers (6)
| # | key | disposition | v5f change | exam |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | pretrace-code-swap-stub-passes-provenance | FIXED | Coherence on every visited function link, F_T included. The declared module must have a source `__file__`. L-STUB and the J1-X1 row are rewritten. Residual R05b covers `'<'` or forged filenames (deliberate only). | X26b, X26c, X26d, X26e, X26f; R05b |
| B2 | hook-exception-leaves-lock-held | FIXED (structural) | No threading lock and no profile or trace function anywhere. Transitions exclude each other through the robust mutex (dead owners stolen, REENTRANT, MACHINERY_BUSY), and the callbacks never run at a C call. 3.11 is refused because its only mechanism re-creates the user-code form of this bug. | H6, H7, X138, X139, X140 |
| B3 | cache-wrapper-body-from-writable-wrapped | FIXED | F_T is the wrapper's real callee (`gc.get_referents`) and must be its own-dict `__wrapped__`, else NOT_A_FUNCTION naming the real callee. The provenance walk also steps to the real callee. Step 4, L-CACHE and "nothing is unwrapped" are corrected. | X24b, X24c |
| B4 | unstarted-generator-credited-py310-311 | FIXED | Confirmed-entry credit: PY_THROW is never used, and a frame killed at entry never publishes. 3.10 and 3.11 are refused. | X119, X135, H8 |
| B5 | stale-stop-after-mint-of-handle-run | FIXED | A monotone identity cut, captured before resolution. RESERVED_TARGET, CUT_UNAVAILABLE, CUT_MOVED. `_STOP` and its reset are gone. Spec lines 155/201 are superseded. Revision 1 has two changes. CUT_MOVED is checked at every entry event of minted code as well as at exit, so a transient rebind is caught. The loop boundary makes a loop started inside a section unable to credit it, uvloop included. Revision 2 has two changes. `BaseEventLoop._run_once`'s code joins the cut, and its binding joins `_cut_ok()`, which closes A1, A2 and revision 1's R17. E2 refuses a shareable binding code (M2). Residual R18 (deliberate only). | X34, X34b, X34c, X35, X35b, X35c, X65b, X65c, X65d, X65e, X65f, X65g, X141, X141b, X141c, V44, V61, V62, V63, V64; `cut_current` leftover invariant |

## Finding closure, rounds 1–3 (rows that change; every other v5e row stands)

No row moves from CLOSED to open. G_CLOSURE re-runs the 41-case round 1–3 battery against v5f on the verified interpreters (3.12.3 and 3.13.12).

| id | v5f status | how |
|---|---|---|
| R1-B1 shared code object | CLOSED_S | Unchanged (X40–X48). Generator objects created before the trace are under-credited, never over-credited (R14). |
| R1-B3 / R2-B2 / R3-B2 attribution | CLOSED_S | Unchanged anchor rule, plus the loop boundary: a loop started inside a section never credits it, uvloop included. The cut holds every `Handle._run` and `BaseEventLoop._run_once` code seen (revision 2). It is monotone and by identity, refuses shareable codes, and cannot be minted. CUT_MOVED is checked on both bindings at every hit. The one route left is the deliberate-only residual R18: re-entrant same-loop dispatch through code that is neither cut code (X34, X65b–X65g, X141b, X141c, V44, V63, V64). |
| R1-B4 C profiler crash or destroyed | CLOSED_S (structural, was: refusal) | styxx never calls `setprofile`, so no profiler is ever called, chained, replaced or removed (V33, V48, V57–V60). Among sys.monitoring tools, styxx adopts only an id that carries its own per-copy name, and never changes events or local events on an id that lost that name (X137, X142, X154). The one exception is a callback registration: at most one exchange per registration can land on an id that another tool took inside it, and it is counted, reported and noted (L-MONITOR; X154b). Revision 7's self-audit corrects this row, which said "never registers callbacks". |
| R1-D1 non-LIFO/reentry leaks | CLOSED_S | Atomic claims, idempotent detach, reconciliation (V15–V17, X32, X32d, V52). |
| R1-D2 resolution crashes and user code | CLOSED_P | Real-type branching with `is` comparisons (no tuple membership: V40b) and C-descriptor reads. User code runs only in the import and PEP 562, and any Exception there becomes a chained UNRESOLVED (V40, X17b, X131, X10–X13b). |
| R1-D3 / R2-D6 non-string trace keys, unhashable end | CLOSED_P | The exact type is tested before every hash or compare (X96–X108, X103b, X103c, X96d). |
| R2-B3 hook persists on a thread | CLOSED_S (structural), with one disclosed leak | There is no hook. The leftover check requires `sys.getprofile()` and `sys.gettrace()` unchanged on every case thread. A zombie's instrumentation also persists (L-ZOMBIE), but that is not this class. R2-B3 was a silent leak after a *completed* exit. A zombie exists only after a fault has propagated out of the harness's own `with`; `record()` refuses it, and it ends at `__exit__()` or at the next transaction once the facade is dropped. **Revision 2 (M3).** A party that frees styxx's id mid-trace could leave the global PY_UNWIND callback firing after a completed exit. Exit's reconciliation now re-takes an unowned freed id and clears its events (X137 free variant). **Disclosed leak:** if *another tool* takes the freed id before styxx's next transaction, styxx never touches it, and styxx's PY_UNWIND callback keeps firing on that id until the new owner changes its events or callbacks. MONITOR_LOST is noted on the trace in which it happened (`rev2/p3_free_reclaim.py`; L-MONITOR). **Revision 4 (B1).** Revision 3 reopened this class without a fault. An open racing its own trace's exit registered `_ANCHORS[None]`, which no later close could pop, so the process-wide PY_UNWIND callback outlived the completed exit for the rest of the process. That happened in 51–62 of 20,000 natural races per version, and revision 2 did the same with a frame key in 49–69. Closed by the owner-only release of `o.frame`, step 8's re-check after the store, and the reconciliation's prune of cores reached from anchors (At open, steps 8 and 9; M3). Witnesses: X146, X146b, X146c, X146d and X147; `rev4/m1_pairs_modelcheck.py`: no anchor left at any fault-free end state of any pair or triple. |
| R2-D4 RecursionError in hook | CLOSED_S | Callbacks are not dropped when they raise. A frame killed at its entry never publishes (V27, X135). |
| R3-B3 hook swallows signal exceptions | CLOSED_S | Callbacks have no handler (H2 with its mutant). |
| R3-D3 finalizer deadlock | CLOSED_S (structural, was: RLock) | No locks exist. The robust mutex refuses REENTRANT instead of waiting, and callbacks take nothing (H1 with its mutant, X139). |
| R3 nit: f_locals cost | CLOSED_S | No Python-level profile or trace function exists, so there is no trampoline. The only `f_locals` reads are `_alive`'s, in the mutex's liveness check on contention and in reconciliation's holder test. Revision 5 removes revision 4's third caller, the opener's wait, so no open or close ever reads `f_locals` (N5). |
| R3 nit: Ctrl-C drops a chained profiler | CLOSED_S | Nothing is chained. |
| R3 nit: PEP 562 | CLOSED_P | Two calls and identical products. FOREIGN_DEFINITION names the defining `module:qualname` (V10, V10b, X13b, X29b). |
| J1-X1 stub bound before the trace | CLOSED_P | `__globals__` identity plus code/file coherence on every visited link (X26–X30, X26b–f). The residual is L-STUB: an in-module stub, a wraps-stamped stub, or `'<'`/forged code (R05, R06, R05b). |
