## P1 retro under this spec
- **Harness and declaration unchanged.** `papers/first-afference/run_p1.py` is used unmodified, with `styxx/power_QUARANTINED.py.txt` loaded as `styxx.power` through SourceFileLoader. G4 declares the five `styxx.power` functions.
- **Runner change.** The only change is the v5e `cov.run(...)` line, and the interpreter is now a verified one (3.12.3 or 3.13.12).
- **Provenance.** All five pass coherence. Each function's `co_filename` is the `.txt` path, which is also the module's `__file__`, and `__globals__ is sys.modules["styxx.power"].__dict__`.
- **Expected record, identical to v5e.** `[{'calls': {'styxx.power:reachable': 12}, 'ambiguous': {}, 'end': 'returned', 'notes': []}]`, with empty `uncredited` and `problems`. Every one of the 12 calls returns, so all 12 are confirmed.
- **Verdict.** score() refuses NOT_EXERCISED, naming exactly the four unexercised functions (X118, G2). Afterwards `reachable.__code__` is restored and `_v5_state()["mints"] == []`.

---

## Over-blocking, disclosed

Each item is a refusal, or a missing credit, that a legitimate harness can meet.

1. **Off-stack work never counts.** This covers threads and grandchildren, thread pools, executors, `run_in_executor`, `asyncio.to_thread`, ThreadPool, library worker threads, asyncio child tasks and loop callbacks. **Remedy:** each job opens its own section. With `eager_task_factory`, only a child's first synchronous step runs on the parent's stack (X82). A child that opens its *own* section in that step refuses NESTED_SECTION for the whole trace (X83). **Remedy:** `await asyncio.sleep(0)` first (V35).
2. **Event loops must run outside the section.** `cov.run(name, asyncio.run, main())` counts nothing (X73). Every task step and callback of a loop started inside the section fails the loop boundary; with the stdlib loop it is also beyond the cut. This holds for uvloop and for any other loop that registers itself through `asyncio.events._set_running_loop` (revision 2, N9; a hand-driven scheduler that does not falls under L-WHERE), and for an eager task factory set through `loop_factory`: `asyncio.run` creates the main task before the loop runs, so the task is not started eagerly, and a `Handle._run` frame lies between it and the section (`rev1/p7_eager_loop_factory.py`, 3.12.3 and 3.13.12). A section opened inside a task of a running loop is credited normally, uvloop included (p9).
3. **Sections must be calls.** A generator or coroutine returned by fn gets LAZY_RESULT, and whatever of it runs after the close does not count.
4. **No same-tracer nesting on one stack above the cut or a loop boundary** (NESTED_SECTION). That includes an eager child's first step.
5. **A swallowed refusal refuses the whole trace.** This applies to any recorded code: REENTRY, TRACE_INACTIVE, UNDECLARED_SECTION, NESTED_SECTION, CLONE_CALLED, CLONE_ALIVE, CODE_SWAPPED, CUT_MOVED, REENTRANT, MACHINERY_BUSY. Every declaring gate scored against that trace refuses, including unrelated gates.
6. **Interpreters.** Tracing refuses UNSUPPORTED_VERSION on:
   - CPython 3.9, 3.10 and 3.11, including the lab's default 3.11, which P1-style harnesses use;
   - 3.14 and later;
   - free-threaded builds;
   - non-CPython implementations.

   - (revision 6, M5) every 3.12 and 3.13 patch level other than 3.12.3 and 3.13.12, until G_ATOM has passed on it and it is added to `_VERIFIED` (M0). This refuses most users' interpreters today; it is the price of not trusting an undocumented CPython property on a patch level where nobody has tested it;
   - (revision 6, M1) an interpreter whose `sys.monitoring` functions, or whose `map`, `filter`, `list`, `collections.deque`, `itertools` or `operator` objects styxx binds, were replaced by Python wrappers or subclasses before the first `coverage_trace()`: a monitoring shim, a debugger, or a test harness that logs `sys.monitoring` calls. From revision 7 (M3) this also covers a replacement of those `itertools`, `operator` or builtin objects installed later and then picked up by `importlib.reload(styxx.protocol)`, which re-binds them: every `coverage_trace()` after that reload refuses. **Remedy:** construct the first tracer before installing the wrapper, or install it after styxx's first `coverage_trace()`, and do not reload `styxx.protocol` while it is installed; the machinery never calls a wrapper installed on `sys.monitoring` after the first binding.
   - (revision 7, N2; a scope note, not a refusal) nothing refuses a different *build* of 3.12.3 or 3.13.12; such a build is accepted but its premise is unverified (M0).

   Scoring works on every version. **Remedy:** run the harness on CPython 3.12.3 or 3.13.12.
7. **sys.monitoring.**
   - Enter refuses MONITOR_BUSY when tool ids 4 and 3 are both held by other tools.
   - styxx holds its id from the first enter until the process exits, so another tool that later asks for that id gets CPython's ValueError.
   - A tool or harness that clears styxx's events or replaces its callbacks blinds the trace. The trace gets a MONITOR_LOST note, and possibly NOT_EXERCISED.
   - A tool that frees styxx's id does not blind the trace's local events: CPython 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 leave the events and callbacks in place (`rev1/p3_free.py`). The trace gets a MONITOR_LOST note. While the id has no owner, styxx's next transaction re-takes it and clears its events (revision 2). If another tool has taken it, styxx never touches it, and any events styxx had set there stay until the new owner changes them (L-MONITOR). A section opened after the free, before the re-take, gets no PY_UNWIND, so its calls that raise are not counted.
   - A second loaded copy of `styxx.protocol` needs the other id; a third refuses MONITOR_BUSY.
   - (Revision 6) If another tool takes styxx's id while one of styxx's callback registrations runs (an audit hook on `sys.monitoring.register_callback` yielded, switched greenlets or freed the id), styxx replaces that tool's callback for one event and does not restore it, and the traces live then note MONITOR_LOST (L-MONITOR). If styxx's id is taken while traces are live, the next enter rebinds to the other id, and every trace live at the rebinding notes MONITOR_LOST (E4).
8. **Declarable objects.** Declarable: a FunctionType; a staticmethod or classmethod of one; a C `lru_cache`/`cache` wrapper whose real callee is its own-dict `__wrapped__`, with that callee not bound by name in the module or holder and no sibling wrapper of it there. Refused:
   - class-based wrappers, `partial`, `property`/`cached_property`, bound methods, `singledispatchmethod`, builtins, C/Cython/numba callables, MagicMock;
   - attributes that come from a class through an instance; modules mid-path; inherited methods (declare the defining class);
   - a `sys.modules` entry that is not a ModuleType instance (UNRESOLVED);
   - a path step through an object whose `__dict__` is a Python-level descriptor (INSTANCE_PATH);
   - a cache wrapper whose `__wrapped__` was restamped (declare the function the wrapper calls);
   - a cache wrapper whose body is bound by name (declare that name);
   - two cache wrappers of one body in the same namespace, or two different declared objects reaching one body. **Remedy:** declare `'<module>:<name>.__wrapped__'`, which counts every execution of the body;
   - a PEP 562 attribute that returns a new object on each access, such as a deprecation shim (UNRESOLVED; declare the function it forwards to);
   - `asyncio.events:Handle._run`, and any function whose code is a recorded cut code (RESERVED_TARGET).
9. **FOREIGN_DEFINITION.**
   - Refused as in v5e: re-exports (declare the defining module); products of a no-`wraps` decorator or factory from another module; attrs- and namedtuple-generated methods whose globals are private; functions exec'd into private namespaces; autospec mocks and spies installed before the trace; PEP 562 lazy re-exports.
   - **New:** a declared module without an exact-str `__file__` (in-memory modules), or with a sourceless `.pyc`/`.pyo` `__file__`.
   - **New:** any function link the walk visits whose `co_filename`, after realpath and normcase, differs from its own module's `__file__`, unless it starts with `'<'`. That refuses, intentionally, a `__code__` swapped before the trace. It also refuses legitimate shapes:
     - modules whose code objects keep a stale filename, from loaders that do not fix `co_filename` (not measured);
     - a zip archive holding both `zmod.py` and a `zmod.pyc` compiled under a build path. On 3.13.12 zipimport loads the `.pyc`: `__file__` is `z2.zip/zmod2.pyc` and `co_filename` is the build path, so it refuses under (a) with the compiled-code message. On 3.12.3 the same archive loaded the source, whose names are coherent (`rev2/out_n2.txt`, from the second critic's `relp/z2.zip`). A zip holding only source is coherent on both versions (`relp/t_zip.py`). Revision 2 (N2) removes the rev-1 item "a relative `__file__` read after the working directory changed": the 3.12/3.13 path finder gives absolute paths, and the critic's `relp/t_rel.py` stays coherent after `chdir`;
     - a `__file__` rewritten by tooling;
     - a wrapper defined in an IPython/Jupyter cell, where `__main__` has no `__file__`, bound around a declared function.
10. **A wrapped target is the wrapper.** Calling the inner function directly does not exercise it.
11. **Cache hits do not count.** A body cached before the section opened reads as unexercised.
12. **`T.__code__` is a different, equal object during the trace.**
    - Code-identity tools see the change. Hot reload refuses CODE_SWAPPED, and `importlib.reload` during the trace under-counts.
    - Generator, coroutine and async-generator objects of T created before the trace, or in an earlier trace, keep T's original code, so their frames are never credited, even when resumed inside a section (R14). Create them inside the trace.
13. **A section open at trace exit** counts only calls confirmed before the exit's credit stop.
14. **Code run directly inside a trace, profile or sys.monitoring callback is never credited.** That includes code run by finalizers that CPython triggers inside such a callback.
15. **Child processes are invisible.** In a forked child, `run()` passes through: no section, no credit, no refusal. A tracer inherited across fork cannot exit in the child (a no-op), and its `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE there.
16. **Valid cases of v5c/v5d that v5e refused** are still refused: `thread_started_in_section`, `thread_pool_inside_section`, `x2_grandchild_thread_in_section`, `asyncio_task_in_one_section`. The shape of `preexisting_python_profiler_chained` now passes (V48).
17. **Cost.** See Mechanism. The walk is linear in stack depth per hit, so deep recursion of a declared target is quadratic. A global PY_UNWIND callback runs for every frame that unwinds by exception, anywhere in the process, while any section is open (revision 2; revision 1: while any mint was registered). Each section open and close costs about 3 µs more for toggling it. The first `coverage_trace()` imports asyncio and scans gc once per new cut code.
18. **Confirmation.** A call counts only if its frame returns, yields, or unwinds from inside its body while the tracer is active. Never counted:
    - calls still running at the credit stop;
    - frames that never return, yield or unwind, such as `os._exit()`, or a thread still running at interpreter shutdown;
    - `throw()` and `close()` resumptions, even when a handler in the body then runs;
    - a frame killed at its entry instruction.
19. **CLONE_ALIVE after a `gc.freeze()` during a mint's life.** At a holder's exit the gc freeze count may be above its value when the mint was made. For a mint this tracer joined, the mint can be older than this trace. In that case any reference to M_T that gc cannot see at exit refuses:
    - a frame of T executing on any thread at that moment, since an executing frame's code reference is invisible to gc (`rev1/p5_execref.py`);
    - a frozen generator, coroutine, traceback or frame of T.

    Frozen objects dying and `gc.unfreeze()` only lower the count, so they never trigger it (p4).
20. **Lifecycle refusals.**
    - REENTRY for a retry after a failed `__enter__`, and for the losing thread of a concurrent double entry. Create a new tracer.
    - REENTRANT when a finalizer, audit hook or signal handler enters or exits a tracer inside another enter or exit on the same thread.
    - MACHINERY_BUSY when another thread holds the machinery for more than 10 s. That includes a cycle through user code (revision 5, MF1). The machinery's owner runs user code inside its transaction that waits for a lock held by a thread which is itself entering or exiting a tracer: for example an audit hook or finalizer taking a lock that another thread holds around `with coverage_trace(...)` or `cov.__exit__()`. The waiting thread raises MACHINERY_BUSY after 10 s, which ends the cycle. The audit-hook sites inside a transaction include `__code__` writes and, as revision 6 corrects (B1), `sys.monitoring.register_callback`, which X5 raises five times on every exit: a hook on that event that takes such a lock closes the cycle at every exit, not only at mints and retires. (Revision 8 self-audit, W5: the reconciliation's holder test, `_alive`, also raises `sys._current_frames`, `builtins.id` and `object.__getattr__` inside every transaction that has a holder to test; Exception-safety, Sources.)
    - A section opened concurrently with the tracer's exit either refuses TRACE_INACTIVE, which is recorded and refuses the whole trace, or opens and counts only the calls confirmed before exit's credit stop, with end `"returned"`, `"raised"` or `"open"` (OPEN_AT_EXIT). Which one happens depends on timing. The TRACE_INACTIVE entry reaches the record only once the racing thread has finished, so a harness should join such threads before `record()`. Revision 4 pins the outcome set as X147. Revision 3 could also leave `_ANCHORS[None]` registered for the rest of the process on this race (B1), and could note a false MONITOR_LOST (MF2).
    - (Revision 5) Nothing waits at open. Revision 4's opener wait could stall an open for 10 s and then refuse MACHINERY_BUSY in three shapes. A clearer was held in its window by user code. Code run inside a clearer's window opened a section (a finalizer or signal handler; two threads doing this waited on each other). Or the clearer's window ran user code needing a lock that the opener's thread held (`with results_lock: cov.run(...)`). All three are gone (the fifth critic's MF1; `rev5/w5_witnesses.py`: c3 and c8 open in 0.0 s).
21. **The dispatch cut.**
    - CUT_MOVED: a harness or library that rebinds `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once`, or reassigns either's `__code__`, during a trace refuses every declaring gate. This happens if any hit of a declared target occurs while a binding is moved, or if one is still moved at exit (X141, X141b, X141c). A rebind and restore with no hit in between is accepted (V62). So is a rebind made before `__enter__` and kept through exit, because E2 adds its code to the cut (V61). nest_asyncio patches `_run_once` on the loop's own class, not on `BaseEventLoop`, so it is not a moved binding.
    - CUT_UNAVAILABLE: either binding that is not a plain Python function (mocked or C-replaced) at construction or entry refuses. Revision 2 (M2): so does either binding whose code is new to the cut and shareable, such as `Handle._run = logged(Handle._run)` with a generic decorator, or a module-level wrapper while another live function shares its code (X35b, X35c). Remedy: bind a dedicated module-level function. Revision 3 discloses four consequences:
      - **A real library (N1).** aiodebug 2.3.0's `log_slow_callbacks.enable()` binds `Handle._run` to `enable.<locals>.instrumented`, a closure that calls the original. While it is enabled, every `coverage_trace()` in the process refuses. For this shape the refusal buys no soundness: dispatch still passes the original `Handle._run` code and `_run_once`, both cut codes, so without the check the X65c shape is still `dispatched` (`critic3/c10_aiodebug_overblock.py`, both versions). The remedy text cannot be applied to a third-party patch. **Remedy:** call `log_slow_callbacks.enable()` after the trace exits, or disable it for the trace. The clause is kept, because a closure factory is exactly the shape that makes more functions with the code later.
      - **The gc test (N2).** A twin that `gc.freeze()` moved to the permanent generation is not seen, so its code is accepted into the cut, and the twin then stops attribution wherever it runs: an under-credit. A dead twin in an uncollected cycle is seen, so the refusal depends on gc timing (`critic3/c1_freeze_hides_twin.py`). Neither over-credits.
      - **A clone at the first construction (MF4).** The stdlib `Handle._run` and `_run_once` codes are themselves new to the cut at the process's first `coverage_trace()`. If a function sharing either code is alive then, such as `FunctionType(Handle._run.__code__, g)` built by a module imported at start-up, that code is "shareable" and never enters the cut, and every `coverage_trace()` in the process refuses CUT_UNAVAILABLE for as long as the clone lives (`critic3/c9_x34b_fixture_poisons_first_e2.py`). **Remedy:** create such a function only after the first `coverage_trace()` of the process, when the codes are already cut codes; X34b does this.
      - **Order dependence (MF4).** A code stays a cut code for the rest of the process, and E2 scans only codes new to the cut. So a wrapper whose code an earlier trace accepted is accepted again without the scan, even while a twin is alive, and rebinding to it during a later trace is not CUT_MOVED (`critic3/c8_cut_order_dependence.py`). Both effects are toward acceptance, never over-credit: the code is a cut code, so attribution stops at it. The exam's rebinding cases each use a fresh wrapper (harness rules, `rev3/c8r3_fresh_wrapper_per_case.py`).
    - **Cut poisoning that remains (revision 2, disclosed).** A function created *after* E2 that reuses a cut code, e.g. `FunctionType(W.__code__, g)` for a wrapper W bound earlier, stops attribution wherever it runs, for the rest of the process. The cut is monotone. The effect is an under-credit (NOT_EXERCISED with the call under `dispatched`), never an over-credit (R20, revision 7).
    - A signal handler or finalizer that runs directly in a re-entered `_run_once` frame, outside any `Handle._run`, is not credited to a section that re-entered it (revision 2; L-RUNTIME).
22. **Section types.** A non-str section refuses UNDECLARED_SECTION. A str subclass (StrEnum, `(str, Enum)`, numpy.str_) is normalized to its exact str value.

**Removed from v5e's list.**
- #6 foreign profilers: styxx calls neither `sys.setprofile` nor `sys.settrace`, so every such tool is expected to coexist. Coexistence was executed with D3's sys.monitoring adapter, not on the v5f machine, for cProfile, pdb, coverage.py's ctrace and sysmon cores, and a pure `setprofile` profiler (D3 t_coexist, p2_callback). The `profile` module, yappi, pyinstrument and debugpy were not executed. V33, V48, V50 and V51 pin coexistence on the implementation.
- #7 hook loss through `setprofile(None)`, RecursionError or signals: no hook exists.
- The per-event cost on non-target code.
- Over-block #16's `preexisting_python_profiler_chained`.

---

## Stated limits

- **Carried forward.** Exercised is not tested: one call satisfies a declaration. Transitive calls count. Only declared gates are checked. The trace is written by the runner, so forgery is an accepted residual under an honest-but-careless threat model. Hardcoded values (P1's G3) are out of scope.
- **L-WHERE (narrowed; revision 2 narrows it again).** Attribution certifies where code ran, not who asked for it. Work another gate supplies as data is credited to the section whose stack runs it:
  - queues drained inline or by a sectioned consumer;
  - `concurrent.futures` done-callbacks;
  - generators and coroutines *created during the trace* elsewhere and resumed there;
  - non-asyncio schedulers (trio, Twisted, a thread's own work queue, and any loop that does not register itself through `_set_running_loop`), which set no running asyncio loop;
  - eager tasks' first steps.

  Pinned by R01, R02, R09 and R10. Any asyncio loop, uvloop included, is no longer here. Work dispatched by a loop started inside a section fails the loop boundary and is never credited. v5e disclosed uvloop's Cython handles as a hole in this limit; revision 1 closes it (p9, X65d). Revision 2 closes revision 1's R17: its dispatch passes `BaseEventLoop._run_once`, now a cut code (X65g). The one asyncio route left is R18, deliberate only. A section opened in a task of a running loop re-enters that loop's dispatch through code that is neither cut code: a loop class whose own `_run_once` runs callbacks without `Handle._run`, or a self-restoring replacement of `BaseEventLoop._run_once` that does the same (`rev2/p1_runonce_cut.py`: credited on both versions).
- **L-RUNTIME (widened).** CPython-injected code counts for the section whose stack it lands on: signal handlers, `__del__` and weakref finalizers, gc callbacks, audit hooks, import-time module code (R08). Two exceptions follow from attribution (revision 2, N3). Such code landing while a loop started inside the section is running fails the loop boundary; v5e credited it. Such code landing in a re-entered `_run_once` frame, outside any `Handle._run`, meets a cut frame first (`rev2/p1_runonce_cut.py`: a SIGALRM handler in a re-entered `_run_once` is `dispatched` in revision 2, and was credited in revision 1). So does code a trace callback runs through `sys.call_tracing`, such as pdb's `debug` command; this is identical on 3.12 and 3.13 (R12). Code run directly inside any trace, profile or sys.monitoring callback is never credited.
- **L-CLONE.** During the trace, code reads M_T and then does one of these; each is credited as T:
  - builds a function with T's own globals and drops it before exit (R03);
  - runs `exec(M_T, T.__globals__)` (R04);
  - does `U.__code__ = M_T` and swaps it back before exit;
  - freezes a live clone while the gc freeze count ends exactly where it stood when the mint was made.

  Otherwise a live clone is caught by CLONE_ALIVE, foreign globals by CLONE_CALLED, and a swap left in place by CODE_SWAPPED.
- **L-STUB (widened and made true).** Provenance proves which globals the function was created with. It checks that every visited function's `co_filename` names its own module's file, or is a generated `'<…>'` filename. `co_filename` can be forged with `code.replace`, so this catches careless swaps; it does not prove where the code came from. Provenance does not prove that the function is the original binding. Credited:
  - a stub defined in the declared module (R05);
  - a `functools.wraps`-stamped stub (R06);
  - a stub whose code has a `'<…>'` filename, or whose `co_filename` was forged to the module's file, bound or swapped in before the trace (R05b; deliberate only).
- **L-CACHE (widened).** A cache wrapper's target is its real callee, the body. Every execution of the body counts, whatever route reaches it: a direct call, `__wrapped__`, a reference captured before decoration (R07), or a miss in a sibling cache wrapper of the same body held outside the module and holder namespaces (R13).
- **L-ASYNC-EXC (rewritten as the envelope I6).** A fault anywhere in the machinery can only do these:
  - lose counts;
  - make an opening read `"open"` or `"raised"` with OPEN_AT_EXIT;
  - add REENTRANT or MACHINERY_BUSY when the fault itself enters or exits a tracer;
  - make `record()` refuse TRACE_INCOMPLETE or TRACE_ACTIVE;
  - inside the PY_UNWIND callback, replace the exception being unwound (L-DELIVERY).

  An asynchronous Exception subclass landing in the declared module's import, or in its PEP 562 `__getattr__`, becomes a chained UNRESOLVED. An asynchronous exception during that import can also leave importlib's per-module lock inconsistent. That is CPython's behaviour: D1 observed it on 3.10 and 3.11. It was not measured on 3.12 or 3.13, where the lock's own wait loop is also exposed to #130279 on 3.13. **Remedy:** import declared modules before the trace. For a module already in `sys.modules` and not initializing, `import_module` takes no module lock.
- **L-DELIVERY (new; scope reduced in revision 2).** styxx's callbacks are Python code at these events:
  - while a mint is registered: every entry (PY_START/PY_RESUME), return and yield of a declared target;
  - while *some section is open* anywhere in the process (revision 2; revision 1: while any mint existed): every exception unwind of any Python frame, on every thread. M7 states the exceptions exactly (U3 and U4). From revision 5 there is only one: after a fault at a close's first instructions, or a `_run_async` `finally` skipped by #130279, a dead anchor keeps the event set. It lasts until that trace's exit, or until a reconciliation prunes it once the trace is dead. Revision 4 also kept the event set for a detacher's own window, for the body of a section whose anchor exit's X3 popped after its step 8, and after a fault between a pop and its clear. Revision 3 could also leave it set for the rest of the process after an open raced its own trace's exit (B1); revision 4 closes that.

  An asynchronous exception can therefore surface *inside a callback*, and it takes effect at that event:
  - **At a target's entry:** the body does not run, and the call is not counted.
  - **At a target's return or yield:** the completed call raises that exception in its caller instead of returning (synth/p_deliver.py), and the call is still counted, confirmed by the unwind at the return offset (synth/p_r16.py).
  - **At any frame's unwind:** it replaces the exception being unwound, and on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 its `__context__` is None, so the original exception is lost (synth/p_deliver.py). An `except` clause for the original exception then does not run.

  Near the recursion limit, a callback's own frame can raise RecursionError at those same points. No C call is ever skipped, because styxx enables no C-call event and installs no profile or trace function. So the lock-release hazard of hook-exception-leaves-lock-held cannot arise from styxx on 3.12/3.13. D1 measured 0 user-lock leaks in 51,075 and 52,456 SIGALRM timeouts with its PY_START/PY_RESUME-only adapter. G_SIG re-measures this with v5f's full event set.

  **What changes in the traced program (revision 2, M4).** This is a change of behaviour, and it is disclosed as one:
  - Untraced CPython has no eval-breaker check between a `raise` and the matching `except` clause. A pending asynchronous exception therefore takes effect only after the handler has started, and the handler's first instructions, up to its first eval-breaker check, always run. The measured consequence is below: 0 lost handlers untraced. With the unwind callback, it can take effect before the handler starts.
  - `finally` blocks and with-exits still run, for the replacing exception. A `with lock:` still releases.
  - An `except E:` handler for the exception in flight does not start, so its side effects are skipped.
  - Exception chaining is lost: the new exception's `__context__` is None.
  - CPython documents no guarantee that a handler starts before a pending asynchronous exception takes effect, but untraced programs can depend on it, and the second critic's probe does.

  Measured with that probe's method in `rev2/p4_unwind_scope.py`: a loop raising and catching ValueError in undeclared code, 1M iterations, under a 20 µs SIGALRM flood whose handler raises. It counts the handlers lost:

  | | 3.12.3 | 3.13.12 |
  |---|---|---|
  | untraced | 0 | 0 |
  | trace active, no section open: revision 1 | 171,683 | 121,297 |
  | trace active, no section open: revision 2 | **0** | **0** |
  | inside an open section: revision 2 | 168,512 | 120,255 |

  **Why this is accepted when 3.11's hook was not.** The 3.11 hook skipped C calls, including the `lock.release()` inside a `with` block's exit. That breaks code that the language's own `with`/`finally` rules make correct under asynchronous exceptions, and it reached every thread with a section. L-DELIVERY keeps every `with`/`finally` guarantee. It moves the point where an already-pending asynchronous exception takes effect to the start of a handler's matching. From revision 2 it does so only while a section is open.

  **G_SIG (frozen decision).** L-DELIVERY does not count as a "wrong result" under G_SIG. G_SIG's frozen harness nevertheless contains three L-DELIVERY cells, each with a fixed rule:
  - (a) an `except ValueError` flow in undeclared code, run while a trace is active and no section is open: gated at **0** lost handlers, the untraced value. Revision 3 (MF2) fixes the cell's shape so the gate does not depend on a fault window. The cell's trace opens and closes one section, fault-free, before the flood is armed, and opens none while it is armed. The flood's handler raises only while the flow runs, so no signal lands in the machinery during the cell. After a fault-free close no stale event exists (U3), and `_v5_state()["global_events"] == 0` is asserted before arming. Revision 2's cell would have been at the mercy of a signal landing in `_open` (the third critic's c7);
  - (b) the same flow inside an open section: measured and reported, not gated;
  - (c) `with lock:` and `try/finally` flows inside an open section: gated at 0 skipped cleanups and 0 leaks.

  Every other G_SIG harness flow uses only `with`/`finally` for cleanup and checks results only on iterations in which no asynchronous exception was delivered.

  **The alternative, not taken.** First-LINE confirmation would need no global event at all, at the cost of a LINE callback per line executed in declared functions. Revision 2 keeps PY_UNWIND and narrows it to open sections instead. That meets the measured case, unrelated code outside sections, at a cost of about 3 µs per section.
- **L-ZOMBIE (new).** A tracer whose exit never began stays active, and `record()` refuses TRACE_ACTIVE. The causes:
  - an exception at `__exit__`'s entry;
  - a foreign tool raising at `__enter__`'s return;
  - a harness `with` body ending in a loop on 3.13 hit by a signal at its back-edge (CPython #130279).

  Its equal-code mints stay installed. The process-wide PY_UNWIND callback stays only while one of its sections is still open (revision 2). That lasts until one of two things happens: `__exit__()` is called, or the facade becomes unreachable and some later enter or exit in the process reconciles. With neither, it lasts until the process exits. A double fault that leaves a stale anchor can keep the facade reachable through the anchor frame's callers.
- **L-MONITOR (new).** Any party can call `sys.monitoring.set_local_events`, `set_events` or `register_callback` on styxx's tool id. Doing so blinds the trace, and calls lost before exit are not counted. Revision 5 (MF2) states exactly when the loss is noted. A cleared global PY_UNWIND is noted in any of three ways:
  - the blinded section's close, or exit's X3, finds it clear under the section's armed, registered anchor;
  - any section opening anywhere in the process finds it clear and notes every armed, registered opening in the same call in which it sets it again (`_unwind_on`; X137c, X137d);
  - the exit's MONITOR_LOST test finds changed local events or callbacks, or the id freed and re-taken or taken by another tool, or (revision 7) `_LOST` changed because another trace's exit repaired a replaced callback while this trace was live.

  The loss is **silent** only when the party that changed something restores it before any of these reads: it clears PY_UNWIND and sets it again itself, or it clears a mint's local events or replaces a callback and restores it before exit. A repair by the machinery is not such a restore. An exit's registration that repairs a replaced callback counts the repair in `_LOST` (revision 7, B1), so every trace live then is noted. Revision 6 did not count it, and a live trace that lost calls through the replaced callback was silent after another trace's exit repaired it (X158). Revision 4 said "exit detects it". Its close-time test alone was erased by any other section's open (the fifth critic's MF2, `critic5/c7_external_clear_masked.py`).
  - **`free_tool_id` does not blind.** On 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 it only removes the name and leaves events and callbacks in place (`rev1/p3_free.py`), so counting continues. Exit notes MONITOR_LOST through the reclaim's count, or because the id is now another tool's. The exception (revision 7, F25): an audit hook that raises inside the reclaim's registration leaves the id re-taken and uncounted. A trace live then that lost nothing carries no note (X157b). A call lost while the id was freed is still noted, by its section's close, which reads the event without a name gate (X157c).
  - **An unowned freed id is re-taken (revision 2).** At the next transaction, normally the trace's own exit, styxx takes the id back under `_TOOL_NAME` and clears its events. Nobody owns it, so nobody is clobbered. Afterwards no styxx callback outlives the completed exit (`rev2/p3_free_reclaim.py`).
  - **An id another tool took is not written, with one bounded exception (revision 6 narrows revision 5's claim).** styxx changes no event or local event there, so it cannot clobber the new owner's events. Every such write is a one-call step gated on the name, so a tool that frees and re-takes the id between two of styxx's statements is never written to (the fifth critic's N1; X154). The exception is a callback registration (B1). `register_callback` raises the audit event `sys.monitoring.register_callback` inside the call, before its exchange. When an audit hook on that event yields the GIL, switches greenlets, or itself frees styxx's id, and another tool takes the id before that exchange, styxx replaces that tool's callback for that one event with its own. Each exchange is gated on the name just before its audit hook, so at most one exchange per registration lands so. The owner read right after it reports the other tool's name, and the registration is treated as incomplete: the exit that made it notes MONITOR_LOST, as does every trace live then (M7, "The registration"). At a first acquisition or an adoption no trace is live. The report there only moves E4 to the other id, and the replaced callback is surfaced nowhere (revision 7, N3; X154c). styxx does not restore the replaced callback. A hook that raises leaves a partial registration and propagates (M5). Registrations happen at a first acquisition, a reclaim, a rebinding and every exit's X5 (Exception-safety, Sources). A hostile tool that re-takes the id under styxx's own name object, read with `get_tool`, is taken for styxx, and styxx cannot tell it from itself (R21 pins it, revision 7). The cost falls the other way: events styxx had set on the freed id, including the global PY_UNWIND if a section was open at the free, keep calling styxx's callbacks there until the new owner changes them (p3: 1,000 callbacks for 1,000 unrelated raises after the exit). This is the one leak after a completed exit, and it is disclosed in the R2-B3 row.
  - **Races.** Revision 5: none raises. `_take` takes an unowned id in one call, and no write is attempted on an id styxx does not hold (the Tool acquisition prefix; X155). Revision 4 could raise ValueError out of `run()` or a transaction here.
  - **Pending entries.** A pending entry whose confirmation never arrives keeps its frame (and that frame's callers) alive until the mint is retired.
- **Counts are confirmed entries.** One per start or resumption whose frame then returned, yielded or unwound from inside its body. A two-yield generator consumed fully records 3; a one-await coroutine run by asyncio records 2. Counts are lower bounds.
- **Interrupted enter and exit** are reconciled at the next enter or exit (see the exception-safety model). The only exception is L-ZOMBIE.
- **Diagnostic buckets are partial.** `uncredited` counts confirmed, uncredited hits on every thread while the tracer is crediting and some section is open somewhere in the process (revision 2: a hit while no section is open stores nothing). It never sees code inside callbacks or unconfirmed calls, and it is never evidence.
- **Scope of testing.** CPython 3.12.3 and 3.13.12, GIL builds, and of those the builds G_ATOM ran on (revision 7, N2: another build of the same patch level is accepted by M0 but unverified); from revision 6, tracing refuses every other patch level (M0, `_VERIFIED`). Not covered: 3.14, free-threading, gevent, greenlet beyond the shapes of X137f and X158d, subinterpreters, and interpreter shutdown. Greenlet switches are otherwise covered by the model check only (`rev5/m5_modelcheck.py` to `rev8/m8_modelcheck.py`; revision 8 self-audit, W6: this said "X137f's one shape" and "the model check only" after X158d was added). A switch made from inside a transaction defeats the mutex's liveness test, so two transactions can interleave, and the model finds no property violated when they do. A switch cannot land inside a one-call step, so the event path has no greenlet exposure. Revision 4's clearer announcement, which a switch also defeated, is gone (revision 5, N5).

---

## Exam cases required

### Exam harness rules (frozen with the prereg before implementation)
- **Interpreters (revision 7, M2).** The full exam runs on exactly the verified interpreters, the `_VERIFIED` patch levels 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (GIL builds), and the prereg records each one's build identifier (M0, N2). The expected outcomes are identical on both (G_XVER). On any other 3.12 or 3.13 patch level every tracing case would refuse UNSUPPORTED_VERSION, so such an interpreter is not an exam interpreter; X37b pins that refusal on a verified interpreter. On 3.10.20 and 3.11.15 (revision 8, N8: pinned by patch level, with their builds recorded like the verified ones') the runner executes X37 and every scoring-only case, and those outcomes must equal the verified interpreters' ones. The scoring-only cases read prebuilt `/3` traces. `ref_v5f.py` generates them on 3.12.3 at freeze time, and they are committed in the freeze commit as `traces_v5f/*.json`, each hashed in the prereg.
- **How cases run.** There are three placements.
  - **Inside the self-trace.** Each case runs in its own thread inside the outer self-trace as `cov.run(<gate section>, case)`, with a 30 s join watchdog.
  - **On the main thread before the self-trace.**
    - Signal cases, hazard sweeps, H6–H10, X71c, X138–X140, V65, (revision 7) V68 and (revision 8) V70.
    - Every case that rebinds `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once`: X35, X35b, X35c, X65e–X65g, X141, X141b, X141c, V61, V62, V64, R18 and (revision 7) R20. Such a rebinding would otherwise be seen at the self-trace's own hits and refuse it CUT_MOVED.
    - Revision 2 (M6): every case that uses the id-5 injector on a machinery function, which is X92b, X131 and X132. Before the self-trace nothing is minted, so `_v5_faultpoints()` returns the code that runs, and the self-trace's own calls cannot be faulted.
    - Revision 3 (MF3): every case whose premise is that no other section is open anywhere in the process: X137c, X143, X143b, X143c, X144, X145 and the H10 cells. Inside the self-trace, the case's own `cov.run(<gate section>, case)` anchor stays registered for the whole case. `_ANCHORS` is process-global, so no `_unwind_off()` clears the event there, and a witness of the toggle protocol scores the same under the spec and under its mutant (`critic3/c6_x143_inside_selftrace.py`; `rev3/c6r3_x143_placement.py`, both versions: X143 gives spec `{'A': {'t': 1}}` and mutant `{}` before the self-trace, and `{'A': {'t': 1}}` for both inside it). A new case whose expected outcome depends on `_v5_state()["global_events"]` or on the event being cleared belongs in this list; the exam author lists them in the frozen runner. Revision 4 adds X146, X146b, X146c, X146d, X147 and X148, whose outcomes read `anchors` or `global_events`, or need exit or a reconciliation to run while no other section is open. Revision 5 retires X149, X149b, X150 and V66, with the opener's wait and `clearing`. It adds X137d, X138b, X143 and X145 in their sweep forms, X146e, X148's sweep form, X152 and X153. Revision 7 adds V67, whose premise is that no anchor is registered anywhere.
  - **In a fresh subprocess** of the same interpreter, running the exam's fixture path. These are the cases that need tool ids in a state the process-long self-trace cannot give: X36, X137's `free_tool_id` variant, X137b, X142 and X142b (revision 4), and X137e, X154 and X155 (revision 5), which free styxx's id or have another tool take it. Revision 6 adds X137f (a greenlet switch inside a reclaim), X154b, X154c, X137g and X137h (another tool takes styxx's id), X156, X156b and X37b (they replace `sys.monitoring.set_events`, `itertools.chain` or `sys.version_info` before the first `coverage_trace()`, which binds once per process), and X157 (an audit hook, which `sys.addaudithook` cannot remove). Revision 7 adds X158 and X158b (they replace a callback on styxx's id), X137h's revision-7 form, X154d, X157b and X157c (an audit hook, and a freed id), X156c and X156d (a replaced `itertools.chain` with a reload, and a replaced `collections.deque`), V69 (a replaced `sys.monitoring.set_events`) and R21 (a hostile re-take of styxx's id). Revision 8 adds X158c and X158d (a replaced callback), and its self-audit adds X158e (a reload), X137i (another tool takes styxx's id), X154e and V71 (an audit hook); revision 8 had not listed X158c and X158d here, although their rows say "in a fresh subprocess". **One subprocess per trial (revision 7, M4).** A case with several trials that each change tool ownership (X154b's five) runs each trial in its own fresh subprocess. Revision 6 said "in a fresh subprocess" for all five, and run in one process, trials 3–5 refuse MONITOR_BUSY, because each trial leaves the other tool holding styxx's id. Revision 3 adds the cases that fork (N10): V47 and G_FI's fork scenario. Their subprocess starts no thread before it forks. With the fork context, `ProcessPoolExecutor` launches all its workers before it starts its manager thread (3.12, 3.13), so every fork happens in a single-threaded process. V47 asserts that no multi-threaded-fork DeprecationWarning was raised. The subprocess reports its outcome as JSON.
- **Case hygiene.** A case that calls `gc.freeze()` calls `gc.unfreeze()` before it returns. The leftover check then requires `gc.get_freeze_count()` to be no higher than before the case. A case that rebinds `Handle._run` restores it. Revision 3 (MF4) adds two rules, because the cut is process-monotone and every rebinding case runs in one process:
  - *Fresh wrapper per case.* Every case that binds a function as `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once` uses a wrapper whose code object has never been bound, scanned or put in the cut in that process: a top-level `def` of its own in a fixture module, used by no other case, with no other function sharing its code except where the case's shape requires one (X35c's twin). This covers X35, X35b, X35c, X65e–X65g, X141, X141b, X141c, V61, V62, V64, R18 and R20. Without it, a code an earlier case put in the cut is accepted again without E2's scan. Then X35c is accepted and X141 has no CUT_MOVED (`critic3/c8_cut_order_dependence.py`); with fresh wrappers both keep their outcomes (`rev3/c8r3_fresh_wrapper_per_case.py`, both versions).
  - *X34b's clone.* X34b builds `FunctionType(Handle._run.__code__, vars(fx))` inside the case, after the process's first `coverage_trace()` (the self-trace's construction, or an earlier case's), and drops it (`del` and `gc.collect()`) before the case returns. No fixture module may hold such a clone at import. A clone alive at the first construction makes every later `coverage_trace()` in the process refuse CUT_UNAVAILABLE (`critic3/c9_x34b_fixture_poisons_first_e2.py`, #21).
- **A fresh tracer per case.** A case never reuses a tracer, a fixture wrapper, or a clone that another case created.
- **How cases pass.** A violation passes only if the refusal message *starts with* its expected `[V5:CODE]`. A valid case passes only with exactly the listed union counts, notes and ends. A residual case passes only with its documented outcome.
- **What the exam may read.** The public API, fixture modules, `_v5_state()` and `_v5_faultpoints()`, and no other private name of `styxx.protocol`. Machinery functions are named only by the frozen `_v5_faultpoints()` keys (M10). Private names of the standard library (`asyncio.events.Handle`, `loop._run_once`) may be used. The premise gate G_ATOM is a process gate, not an exam case. It alone may also read the frozen *G_ATOM interface* (M10, revision 7), and no exam case may.
- **Leftover checks after every case.** The snapshots are taken *outside* the case's self-trace section (revision 3, MF3). The "before" snapshot is taken on the harness thread before it starts the case thread that runs `cov.run(<gate section>, case)`. The "after" snapshot is taken after that thread has been joined, so that section has closed. For main-thread and subprocess placements, the snapshots are taken immediately before and after the case function. Inside the self-trace section, `anchors` counts the self-trace's own anchor, and `global_events` depends on it; X137c even leaves `global_events` 0 while that anchor is registered. With no section open at either snapshot, a fault-free run has `anchors == 0` and `global_events == 0` at both (U3). The checks:
  - `_v5_state()` equals the "before" snapshot, except the monotone fields `cut` and `tool`;
  - `cut_current` is True;
  - `guard` is `"free"`;
  - every fixture function's `__code__` is its original;
  - `sys.getprofile()` and `sys.gettrace()` are unchanged on the case thread;
  - `threading.getprofile()`/`gettrace()` are unchanged;
  - sys.monitoring tool ids other than styxx's are in their pre-case state. The harness compares a tool's name first by identity with the pre-case object, and by `==` only when `type(name) is str`. A name of any other type that is not the same object counts as changed. So the check itself runs no foreign `__eq__` (revision 5, N7: X142b's name is a str subclass whose `__eq__` counts its calls, and X142b reads that counter before the leftover check as well).
- **Threads named by ident (revision 5, MF3).** Any case that names a thread by its ident, for a filter, a hold or a state read, keeps that thread alive, parked on an Event, until every check of the case that uses the ident is done. It may also use the main thread. An ident is reused as soon as its thread ends, and a new thread started later often gets it. Revision 4's X149 failed a correct implementation under that reading (`critic5/c1_x149_ident_reuse.py`: `same_ident True` 20/20 when T1 was a new thread). Every case below states which thread performs each step and each read.
- **Instruction sweeps (revision 5).** An *instruction sweep* over a machinery function F on thread T is a family of trials. Trial k uses a sys.monitoring INSTRUCTION tool on id 5, with local events on `_v5_faultpoints()[F]` filtered to T's live ident. At the k-th instruction T executes in F, the tool's callback runs the case's *interference* to completion on another thread and waits for it, then returns. Trials run k = 1, 2, … until a trial in which T executes fewer than k instructions of F; that trial is not counted. Each trial uses fresh tracers. The case passes only if every counted trial gives the case's outcome. No timeout decides anything: the interference is a sequence of complete transitions that never waits for T. The sweep places the interference between every two consecutive instructions of F, which is where any instrument, signal handler, finalizer or thread switch could run code. So the sweep is the witness for every rule that says "this read and this write are one call" (the revision-5 row of the mutation audit). The trial count depends on F's bytecode, which differs between versions and implementations; the outcome does not. The injector rules below apply to the sweep's tool as well.
- **Fault tools.** The exam's own monitoring fault tool uses id 3, below styxx's 4. It takes id 3 only for the duration of each case that uses it and frees it afterwards. Before freeing id 3 or id 5, the harness sets the id's global events and every local event it set to 0, and registers None for every event it registered a callback for. Revision 5 adds this rule because `free_tool_id` leaves events and callbacks in place (`rev1/p3_free.py`). In revision 5's own probe, a sweep trial's callbacks outlived their free and fired in later trials, which hid violations until the probe unregistered them (`rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py`). The frozen injector uses id 5 (INSTRUCTION events on `_v5_faultpoints()` code). On 3.12.3, INSTRUCTION events themselves create the #130279 skip at the instrumented code's back-edges (p12). That is harmless here: the machinery has no `finally` or `with` across a loop other than M7's `await`, and nothing depends on that. Revision 4: the id-3 tool may also use local CALL and C_RETURN events on a machinery code object (X146b). Its callbacks run Python code inside the machinery. Revision 5 tolerates that everywhere, because no callback can run inside a one-call step (M7), so the tool is not an instrument the machinery must be protected from. Each case states the events it registers.
- **Injector rules (revision 2).** Each rule answers a measured failure mode:
  - *Code identity.* The injector instruments the code objects `_v5_faultpoints()` returns at the moment of arming (M10). They are read at call time.
  - *Thread filter.* It faults only events on the case's own thread. That thread is identified by `Thread.ident`, read after `start()` and while the thread is alive, and the injector is disarmed before `join()`. Idents are reused once a thread ends: in `rev2/p6_faultpoint_identity.py`, a filter keyed on a finished thread's ident matched a later thread.
  - *No settrace.* A settrace function active over a code object silences other tools' INSTRUCTION events on that code, and they stay silent after `settrace(None)` until `sys.monitoring.restart_events()` (`rev2/p5b_settrace_blinds_injector.py`, 3.12.3 and 3.13.12). So the injector never runs while a settrace or setprofile function is set, and it calls `restart_events()` before each trial. G_COVER has no settrace pass (revision 3, MF6).
  - *Self-check.* Each injector run first faults a frozen probe function at a known offset. If that trial does not fire, the run is void and counts as a failure of the instrument, not as clean.
  - *No other instruction-level tool (revision 3, N5).* While the injector is armed, no other sys.monitoring tool has LINE, INSTRUCTION, JUMP or BRANCH events on a machinery code object, unless it never returns `DISABLE` for machinery code. A LINE tool returning `DISABLE`, as coverage.py's sysmon core does, makes the injector miss 3 of 17 (3.12.3) and 3 of 15 (3.13.12) offsets on a code object's first execution; one returning None misses none (`critic3/c3_line_disable_blinds_injector.py`). G_COVER's LINE tool is built to this rule. V50, which runs coverage.py's sysmon core, uses no injector.
- **Environment (revision 7, N5: pinned by wheel hash).** The exam environment installs exactly these wheels from the prereg, each verified by sha256 before any case runs:
  - coverage.py 7.16.1, for V50: `coverage-7.16.1-cp312-cp312-manylinux1_x86_64.manylinux_2_28_x86_64.manylinux_2_5_x86_64.whl` sha256 `e82e10b9d290f60b63459cfb245a841aec347603997206296b93881463a93dcf`, and the cp313 wheel sha256 `6b3fd0f3435ebb7a7183b32a6062a8b755f08242ced1f3f22761d30b56b3c2a5`. Revision 6 wrote "7.16" here and "7.16.1" under G_COVER; 7.16.1 is meant.
  - greenlet 3.5.6, for X137f: `greenlet-3.5.6-cp312-cp312-manylinux_2_24_x86_64.manylinux_2_28_x86_64.whl` sha256 `975736b002ed080d124cf81a79cb7e05cb26d6b3f5c7a7b651c0fcce70353aa1`, and the cp313 wheel sha256 `f98e8215e172f567ce80eeaed9107fb4d32b6c44f26983d9b8334658136a205a`.

  These are the wheels `rev6/wheels31*` and `rev7/wheels` hold, which PyPI also publishes under these digests. D3's t_coexist ran coverage.py 7.16 (patch level not recorded), so V50's run on 7.16.1 is new evidence, not a re-run.

An interval SIGALRM (0.2 ms) is armed before `cov.__exit__()`. Its handler calls f once, the first time `_v5_state()["anchors"]` is below its value when exit began and above 2 (the witness's and G's anchors). By spec order that is inside X3, after X1 and X2, and G's on-stack opening, appended last, is still registered. A handler tripped from inside a monitoring callback cannot be used: code it runs raises no monitoring events (`rev1/p16_handler_events.py`). **Revision 2 (M7): a landing rule instead of hoping for one.** 100,000 suspended `run_async('G', …)` openings make X3 long, not 1,000. A trial *lands* iff the witness tracer's union has f, an observable. A trial that does not land is void and is repeated, up to 20 trials. X71c passes iff some trial lands and no landed trial credits f to G. It fails if no trial lands in 20, or if any landed trial credits G. SM1 applies the same rule to ref+W: a weakening is KILLED iff some landed trial credits G. In `rev2/p7_x71c_landing.py`, 1,000 openings landed in only 6 of 10 trials on 3.12.3, which is the critic's point. 10,000 and 100,000 landed in 10 of 10 on both versions, and the M10 mutant (credit stop after detach) credited G in every landed trial |

With the name test split from the write (`if _named(t): set_events(...)`), the trials in between clear or replace that tool's events or register styxx's callbacks on its id (`rev5/w5_witnesses.py` IS-GATE: 92/122, 31/65, 12/25 and 67/85 failing trials on 3.12.3; the spec fails none; `critic5/c4_toolid_retake_race.py`). Revision 6: the `_register` sweep witnesses only that its gates are inside its one statement. An INSTRUCTION tool cannot run code inside the C call, so this sweep never places the interference inside `register_callback`, where the audit hook runs; X154b does | every write is one call gated on the name (rev. 5) |

The case enters a tracer on f, runs `cov.run('A', f)`, installs an audit hook, and exits. The hook acts only on `sys.monitoring.register_callback` events raised on the case thread during that `__exit__`: on the k-th, it starts a thread that frees styxx's id (from `_v5_state()["tool"]`) and has another tool take it and register a callback of its own for each of the five events styxx uses, joins that thread, and returns; then it disarms itself. After `__exit__` returns, the case reads the other tool's callbacks by *exchange and restore*: for each event, `c = register_callback(t, e, None)` then `register_callback(t, e, c)`, with the hook disarmed (sys.monitoring has no callback getter; the ten audit events this raises are outside every trial). Revision 6 ran the five trials in one subprocess: each trial leaves the other tool holding styxx's id, so trial 2 rebinds to id 3 and trials 3–5 refuse MONITOR_BUSY (the seventh critic's M4) | every trial: `__exit__` returns; the trace PASS `{f:1}` with MONITOR_LOST; the other tool owns the id; its callbacks for the four events other than the k-th (in the order PY_START, PY_RESUME, PY_RETURN, PY_YIELD, PY_UNWIND) are its own, and its callback for the k-th event is styxx's. With revision 5's registration (one name gate before all five exchanges), trial k finds 6 − k of the other tool's callbacks replaced (`rev6/w6_witnesses.py`, both versions; `rev7/w7_witnesses.py`, one subprocess per trial, the same verdicts) | per-exchange gate in `_register` (rev. 6) |

T2 calls `f(True)`, so f's entry is stored as pending with outcome `u`: f is under no anchor of P on T2. `body` then returns, so section A closes, the last anchor, and PY_UNWIND is cleared. Main sets `go`. f raises on T2, and its unwind is not delivered, so the entry is stranded. T2 catches and drops the exception, and at once calls `f(None)`, which returns. No anchor is registered at that entry. Main joins T2, and P exits | P NOT_EXERCISED for f; `calls {}`, `unattributed {}`, `dispatched {}`; no MONITOR_LOST. With the pending entry keyed by `id(frame)` without holding the frame, the first frame dies, and the second reuses its id: 1000 of 1000 trials on both versions, gc on. Its PY_RETURN then pops and publishes the stale entry: `unattributed {f: 1}` (`rev8/a13_witness.py`, 20 processes × 50 trials per row, both versions; `critic8/c3_a13_frame_id_reuse.py`) | Decisions: the pending-entry key holds the frame (A13) |

The open path's audit events all precede `_open`'s append. With `_open` making an audited call after its append, the refused open leaves a second opening of A, finalised at exit with OPEN_AT_EXIT (`verify8/v71_getframe_at_open.py` on `rev8/mech8.py`, mutant `frame_after_append`, all three variants, both versions) | Exception-safety, Sources (the open path's audit sites) |

### New documented residuals (pinned outcome)
- **R05b.** A stub compiled with a `'<stub>'` filename is exec'd into the declared module's globals and bound before the trace. A variant installs stub code with `co_filename` forged to `mod.__file__` via `code.replace`. Outcome: PASS (deliberate only).
- **R12.** A settrace callback runs T on a section's stack through `sys.call_tracing` (pdb `debug`). Outcome: PASS, credited. The same callback calling T directly: not credited (NOT_EXERCISED).
- **R13.** A sibling cache wrapper of the declared wrapper's body, held in another module, is called. The declared wrapper is never called. Outcome: PASS.
- **R14.** A generator object of the declared generator function is created before the trace and resumed inside a section. Outcome: NOT_EXERCISED.
- **R16.** The id-3 tool raises at the target's PY_RETURN. Outcome: the caller sees that exception, and the call is counted `{f:1}` because its body ran (the L-DELIVERY shape).
- **R17 (retired in revision 2).** The shape is now violation case X65g: its dispatch passes a `BaseEventLoop._run_once` frame. That is a residual outcome changing before any prereg, so no prereg is affected.
- **R18 (revision 2).** Two variants. (a) A `SelectorEventLoop` subclass whose own `_run_once` runs ready callbacks through `map(operator.call, …)` (X65d's loop) runs `main`. In a task of it, section A calls `loop._run_once()`, which runs a job another task scheduled with `call_soon(f)`. (b) The stdlib loop, where the harness, after `__enter__`, rebinds `BaseEventLoop._run_once` to a module-level W that first restores the original binding and then runs the ready callbacks without `Handle._run`. Outcome of both: PASS, credited to A. No cut frame lies between f and A's anchor; the running loop is `A.loop`; and no hit or exit sees a moved binding (`rev2/p1_runonce_cut.py`, both versions; deliberate only; L-WHERE).
- **X157 (revision 6, B1 item 5; pinned residual).** In a fresh subprocess: a tracer on f runs `cov.run('A', f)`; then an audit hook is installed that raises `Denied(Exception)` at the second `sys.monitoring.register_callback` event on the case thread, once; then `cov.__exit__(None, None, None)`. Outcome: `Denied` propagates out of `__exit__`; `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE; `_v5_state()["guard"]` is `"dead"`; a second tracer on g then PASS `{g:1}` with no MONITOR_LOST, and after its exit `guard` is `"free"`, `anchors` 0 and `global_events` 0 (`rev6/r6_b1_register.py`, `exit-raise`, both versions, on the model).

- **R20 (revision 7, self-audit; over-blocking #21).** Main thread before the self-trace, with a fresh wrapper (harness rules). A module-level wrapper W is bound as `Handle._run` across one `coverage_trace()` and `__enter__` (its E2 puts W's code in the cut), then restored. After that, the case builds `FunctionType(W.__code__, g)`, where W's body calls its argument. A second tracer on f runs `cov.run('A', clone, f)`. Outcome: NOT_EXERCISED, with `dispatched {f:1}`. The clone is a cut frame between f and A's anchor: cut poisoning under-credits and never over-credits (`rev7/w7_audit.py` R20, both versions).
- **R21 (revision 7, self-audit; L-MONITOR's hostile tool).** In a fresh subprocess: a tracer on f runs `cov.run('A', f)`; then another tool reads styxx's name object with `get_tool`, frees styxx's id and re-takes it with that same object; then `cov.run('A', f)` again, and exit. Outcome: PASS `{f:2}` with no MONITOR_LOST. The identity test cannot tell the hostile tool from styxx, and CPython keeps the events and callbacks across the free (`rev7/w7_audit.py` R21, both versions).
- **R22 (revision 7, self-audit; "a `code.replace()` copy of M_T is never observed").** Inside the trace, `FunctionType(f.__code__.replace(), f.__globals__)`, a copy of M_T with T's own globals, is called inside section A. Outcome: NOT_EXERCISED for f; the copy is in no bucket (`dispatched {}`, `unattributed {}`); no problem is recorded. The copy carries no monitoring events (`rev7/w7_audit.py` R22, both versions). This is an under-credit of a deliberate shape, disclosed with L-CLONE.
- **X157b (revision 7, F25 and N4; pinned residual).** In a fresh subprocess: a tracer P on f runs `cov.run('A', f)`. An outside party frees styxx's id; nobody takes it. An audit hook is installed that raises `Denied(Exception)` at the 2nd `sys.monitoring.register_callback` event on the case thread, once. Then a tracer Q on g is entered. Outcome:
  - `Denied` propagates from Q's `__enter__`, and Q's `record()` refuses TRACE_INCOMPLETE;
  - `_v5_state()` then shows `tool_ours` True and `guard` `"dead"`;
  - P exits and PASS `{f:1}` with **no** MONITOR_LOST. The free was re-taken by a reclaim whose count the raise skipped, and P lost nothing (F25);
  - a tracer R on g then PASS `{g:1}` with no MONITOR_LOST, and after its exit `guard` is `"free"`, `anchors` 0 and `global_events` 0 (`rev7/w7_witnesses.py`, both versions).

A residual whose outcome changes is a spec change and needs a new prereg.

### Hazard sweeps (main thread, before the self-trace), each paired with a detection mutant
Every detection mutant below is an SM1 row. It is a frozen text patch against `ref_v5f.py`, run at freeze time, so the sweep's power is shown before the implementation exists. On the implementation the sweeps run unmutated (G_FI, G_SIG); mutants of the implementation come from SM2.

- **H1 (finalizers).** Cyclic garbage whose `__del__` either calls a target or enters and exits a tracer. Gen-0 thresholds 1–40 are swept at `_open`, `_detach`, `record`, the callbacks, `_enter` and `_exit`, with a 10 s watchdog. Expected: no hang, and no problem other than REENTRANT from the tracer-entering finalizer. **Mutant:** the robust mutex replaced by `threading.Lock`. The sweep must detect a hang.
- **H2 (SIGALRM).** `Timeout(Exception)` in a tight traced loop after a target call, 20 trials. Expected: 20/20 propagated and 20/20 PASS. **Mutant:** the callbacks wrapped in `except Exception`. Propagation must drop below 20/20.
- **H3 (KeyboardInterrupt).** Raised from a signal, 5/5. Expected: it propagates, and `_v5_state()` is clean after exit.
- **H4 (performance).** Reported, not gated: the Cost table re-measured.
- **H5 (gc cost).** No gc at resolution or close. The exit scan is refcount-gated.
- **H6 (no-hang fault sweep, the deterministic core of G_FI).** For every code object in `_v5_faultpoints()`, the id-5 injector raises at each executed instruction, one point per run. Expected: another thread completes a trace within 5 s, and invariants C1–C8 (defined under G_FI) hold. **Mutants** (SM1 rows, witnesses as listed there): M1 (`with RLock`) must hang; M2–M7 must be detected. M8 (per-thread counters) is retired with `_TH`.
- **H7 (user locks).** A `with lock:` loop inside a section under SIGALRM for 30 s per version. Expected: 0 harness locks left held and 0 hangs. **Mutant:** a setprofile-based event source (v5e's hook). It must leak more than 0.
- **H8 (body-less credit).** A SIGALRM flood with one section per call, each call counting its body runs. Expected: credited ≤ body runs, on both versions. **Mutant:** publish at entry. Detected on 3.12 (D3 p3: 181 in 8 s). On 3.13 the same mutant is killed by X135.
- **H9.** The back-edge cases X140 (deterministic) and X140f (flood), run on the main thread. **Mutant** (an SM1 row): `ref_v5f.py`'s X3 detach loop placed inside `with _M:`, where `_M` is a `threading.Lock` that `_exit_txn` also takes (v5e's shape). X140 must detect it on both versions: the exception raised at the back-edge offset skips the with-exit (p10), and another thread's exit then hangs.
- **H10 (L-DELIVERY; revision 2, M4).** G_SIG's three frozen L-DELIVERY cells (Stated limits): (a) outside every section with a trace active, 0 lost handlers, with the cell's shape fixed in revision 3 (no section is opened while the flood is armed, and `global_events == 0` is asserted before arming); (b) inside a section, reported; (c) `with`/`finally` inside a section, 0 skipped. **Mutant** (an SM1 row): PY_UNWIND set at mint time and cleared at retire (revision 1's scope). Cell (a) must detect it: `rev2/p4_unwind_scope.py` measured 171,683 (3.12.3) and 121,297 (3.13.12) lost handlers under that scope, against 0.

## The semantic-mutation gate (G_SEM), frozen before implementation, decided by execution

**Goal.** Show that the v5f exam detects *weakened* rules, not only deleted refusals. Round 4 found this was not so for v5e: 57/57 deletions were detected, but only 27/63 hand-aimed weakenings, and 29 of the survivors were witnessed exam holes. Equivalence is decided mechanically or by a written argument signed before the freeze, and never by a reviewer after it.

**Objectivity rule (revision 1).** Every quantity the gate reads is computed by a frozen script from frozen inputs: the catalog, the corpus, the seeds, N and the list of spec-fixed substrings. No step after the freeze asks a person to judge anything: not whether a mutant is equivalent, whether a line is reachable, which window a fault fell in, or which observables count.

### Frozen artifacts
The v5f prereg lists `FROZEN_*_SHA256` for each artifact below. All are written by the exam author, who is independent of the implementer (G_INDEP).
- **The exam runner.**
- **`ref_v5f.py`.** The reference model: an executable spec written **from this document's text alone**. The author attests they did not read `crashcons/v5f_core.py`, `monitor/v5f_region.py` or `narrow/proto`.
- **`rules_v5f.json`.** Every normative sentence of this spec as a rule atom with an id, including each crash-consistency ordering and claim (M1–M9, I1–I6).
- **`weakenings_v5f.py`** (the SM1 catalog). Each entry is an exact-once text patch against `ref_v5f.py` and carries: rule id, operator family, the NAMED witness case id, the witness's spec outcome, its weakened outcome, and the versions it applies to.
- **`opmut_v5f.py`.** The SM2 operator generator for the real implementation.
- **`diffprobe_v5f.py`.** The differential probe, its normalizer and its noise-mask procedure.
- **`corpus_v5f/`.** The differential-probe corpus, written by the exam author before the freeze. It holds:
  - every exam case program;
  - the fuzzer's programs for its frozen seeds;
  - the crash sweep's points;
  - the round 1–4 repros, rewritten by the exam author against the public API from `protocol_v5_redteam/round1_module/` through `round4/` and `round4/closure-audit/r1/held_battery.py`.

  Each file is hashed in the prereg. Nothing is added after the freeze.
- **`corpus_v5f/manifest.json`** (revision 2, M5). It marks every corpus entry with exactly one comparison class, assigned by a frozen rule from the entry's source and never by judgement:
  - **`envelope`**: every entry that injects a fault or depends on timing. That is every crash-sweep point; every exam case that uses the id-3 fault tool, the id-5 injector, a signal, `PyThreadState_SetAsyncExc`, a finalizer threshold or a thread race (X71c, X92b, X92c, X131–X133, X135, X138, X139, X140, X140f, X143, X143b, X143c, X144, X145, X146, X146b, X146c, X146d, X146e, X147, X148, X137d, X137e, X138b, X152, X153, X154, X155, V65, V68, R16, the H sweeps and every G_SIG cell; revision 5 retires X149, X149b, X150 and V66); and every fuzzer program whose grammar path includes a fault tool.
  - **`equality`**: every other entry.

  At freeze time the manifest is validated on `ref_v5f.py`. Each `equality` entry is run N = 5 times per version and must give identical spec-fixed observables. An entry that fails this is an exam-author error, fixed before the freeze by reclassifying it under the rule's own terms: it names a timing dependency the rule missed, and the rule is amended.
- **`cover_driver_v5f.py`** (revision 2, BF1). The direct-call coverage driver for the callback-only functions (G_COVER).
- **`traces_v5f/`.** The prebuilt `/3` traces for the scoring-only cases, generated by `ref_v5f.py` on 3.12.3 at freeze time.
- **`crash_sweep_v5f.py`.** The lab's `fault_injection_v5.py` and D1's `t_crash_sweep.py` merged and ported to the v5f interfaces. It holds the fault injector (sys.monitoring INSTRUCTION events, tool id 5, targets from `_v5_faultpoints()`), invariants C1–C9 exactly as G_FI defines them (revision 3), the background-tracer variant, and the two-thread, `run_async`, generator-target and hopped-coroutine scenarios.
- **`fuzz_v5f.py`.** The oracle fuzzer. Its grammar covers nested tracers, shared and cross-named sections, clones, swaps, generator `throw()`/`close()`, cross-thread coroutine sections, `gc.freeze`, raising targets and lower-id fault tools.
- **`sigflood_v5f.py`.** G_SIG: user-lock leaks, hangs, poison, wrong results, and credited ≤ body runs.
- **`refcensus_v5f.py`.** G_REF (refusal deletion by coded literal) and G_HYG (hygiene and back-edge lint).
- **`controls_v5f.py`.** The positive controls.
- **`sm1_result.json`.** Computed at freeze time on `ref_v5f.py`. It is included in the freeze commit.

### SM1: catalog power, computed at freeze time on the reference only
**Admission (mechanical).** A weakening W is *admitted* on a version iff all three hold:
1. its patch applies exactly once;
2. its named witness scores the spec outcome on unpatched `ref_v5f.py`;
3. under `ref_v5f.py` + W, the witness's observable outcome differs from the spec outcome on that version.

**Mutation mode (revision 4, N7).** The frozen runner's `--mutation CASE` runs exactly one case, in its frozen placement (inside a self-trace, on the main thread before one, or in its own subprocess), in a fresh process of the same interpreter started for that run, and reports that case's outcome. Every SM1 run is such a process: admission condition 2 (the witness on `ref_v5f.py`), condition 3 (the witness on ref+W) and the kill test. So is every SM2 run of instrument (a) on a mutant, which runs the whole exam in one fresh process per mutant, as `--mutation ALL`. No two runs share a process, so the process-monotone cut, the tool id and every fixture wrapper start fresh in each (the second critic's MF4 on rev 2; the fourth critic's N7).

**Every mutant run twice (revision 8, N9).** Every SM1 run (admission conditions 2 and 3, and the kill test) and every SM2 instrument-(a) run is made twice, each in its own fresh process, and the two outcomes are compared. A row counts as admitted, KILLED or UNWITNESSED, and an SM2 mutant as detected, only when both runs agree. A row or mutant whose two runs disagree is reported as NONREPRODUCIBLE and counts as not killed, which fails closed. This rule is fixed before the freeze, and applying it needs no judgement. The same rule applies to the model check's mutant verdicts that this text states: each rests on two runs with identical verdicts (`rev8/out_m8_mutants_run1.txt` and `_run2.txt`). The eighth critic saw one unexplained `NOT DETECTED` in a single run of `rev7/m7_modelcheck.py mutants` that seven later runs did not reproduce (its N9).

**Isolation (D2).** An admitted W counts as **KILLED** only if its **named** witness fails on ref+W in the exam's mutation mode, whether by a wrong outcome, a crash or a timeout. If only some other case fails, the row is **WITNESS_MISMATCH** and counts as not killed. The fix is a better-isolating witness, before the freeze.

**Unwitnessed rows.** A row failing condition 3 is **UNWITNESSED**. Before the freeze, the exam author must do one of two things for every UNWITNESSED row:
- write a witness that satisfies condition 3, then re-run admission; or
- file it as **EQUIVALENT_BY_SPEC**, with a written argument that no harness can observe the difference under this spec. The argument is signed by a reviewer who is neither the exam author nor the implementer.

After the freeze, nothing is reclassified. A surviving admitted row fails G_SEM, and the only remedy is a new prereg with a new exam.

**Worked example.** D1's M9, "claim after cleanup", is envelope-equivalent. Its faulted outputs stay inside I6. It is filed as EQUIVALENT_BY_SPEC with the I6 argument, and it is also the control that SM2's envelope comparison must *not* flag.

**Gate.** Both must hold:
- `ref_v5f.py` passes the exam on both verified interpreters;
- 100% of admitted rows are KILLED by their named witness on every version they are admitted for.

**Required catalog content:**
- every row of the Mutation-audit table above;
- all 63 round-4 census mutants (`protocol_v5_redteam/round4_exam_mutation/mutants.py`). Their classification is fixed here, by id, so nothing is decided by reading code:
  - **RETIRED (15).** Their site belongs to a v5e mechanism that "What is removed from v5e" deletes:
    - C01, C02, C04, C05, C06 and C08: the profile hook's close and hop handling, PROFILER_LOST, and `_THREADS` counts;
    - E04: the `_STOP` reset, replaced by the monotone cut;
    - E07: exit removing a foreign profiler;
    - H01, H02 and H03: the hook's self-removal and its `call`-event filter;
    - O03: install epochs;
    - O05 and O08: FOREIGN_PROFILER;
    - O06: the `_THREADS` count at open.

    Their successor behaviour is pinned by V37, V57–V60 and X65b instead.
  - **ADOPTED (1).** P03 "walk any type" is v5f's rule (Provenance (b)), so its patch has no site. The successor weakening, "walk only through functions", is a row (V38).
  - **RE-TARGETED (47).** Every other id. The exam author writes each as a patch against `ref_v5f.py` that weakens the same rule, with a named witness. P04 "no cycle detection" is expected UNWITNESSED: the walk is bounded at 16 hops and a revisited link repeats its own verdict, so the outcome cannot change. The exam author either finds a witness or files it EQUIVALENT_BY_SPEC with that argument, before the freeze. Cycle detection stays in the spec as a cost bound;
- the 29 round-4 exam-hole kill shapes, each as a row whose named witness is its v5f case;
- D1's crash-consistency weakenings, re-targeted to `ref_v5f.py`. A crash-sweep row's named witness is one invariant check of `crash_sweep_v5f.py`. The row is KILLED only if that invariant is among the sweep's failures; if only other invariants fail, it is WITNESS_MISMATCH:
  - M1 plain RLock → C3 (hang);
  - M2 no reconciliation → C7;
  - M3 register before append → C5 (anchors left);
  - M4 entering token always live → C7;
  - M5 exit claim not idempotent → C5 (openings left);
  - M6 install before register → C5 (code not restored);
  - M7 exiting token always live → C7;
  - M10 credit stop after detach → X71c;
  - M8 (per-thread counters) is RETIRED with `_TH`; M9 is filed as above;
- the confirmation weakenings, each with its named witness: publish at entry (X135); unwind-offset check deleted (X135); PY_THROW credited (X119); publication re-check deleted (X71); pending entry keyed without frame identity (V70, revision 8, N2: a deterministic witness, so the row is not UNWITNESSED and EQUIVALENT_BY_SPEC would be false);
- coherence weakenings: F_T exempt (X26b); intermediate links exempt (X26d); `'<'` skip widened to "no `__file__`" (X26e); sourceless accepted (X26f);
- freeze: "count > 0" or "count ≠ baseline" instead of "count > baseline" (V36b); visible accounting deleted (X58b);
- cut and loop: CUT_MOVED only at exit (X141b); E2's refresh deleted (V61); loop boundary deleted in attribution (X65d) or in the NESTED walk (V63); `is` chain replaced by tuple membership (V40b);
- revision 2: `_run_once` dropped from the cut (X65g); its binding dropped from `_cut_ok()` (X141c); cut replaced at each E2 (V64); either shareable-code clause deleted (X35b, X35c); PY_UNWIND back to mint scope (H10); `UNWIND_LOST` deleted (X137c); reclaim deleted (X137 free variant); at-fork restores moved first, or pass-through by a cached pid (G_FI fork scenario, C9);
- revision 3: `_commit` moved before `_run`'s try (X144); `o.armed` dropped from `_detach`'s check (X143b); the anchor test dropped from it (X143c); the reconciliation's final `_unwind_off` deleted (X143c in revision 3; X137 free variant from revision 5, where no pop leaves a stale event); `_unwind_off` in revision 2's order (X145 (a)); `_retire` step 6 as revision 2's test-then-clear (X145 (b)). Each was checked on `rev3/mech3.py`: the witness's outcome differs under the mutant, on both versions (`rev3/w1_rev3_witnesses.py`), and again on `rev4/mech4.py` for the revision-4 forms of the witnesses (`rev4/w4_rev4_witnesses.py`). SM1 admission re-checks it on `ref_v5f.py`;
- revision 4: every row of the revision-4 line of the Mutation-audit table that revision 5 keeps, each with the witness named there. The B1 rows and X148 were re-checked on `rev5/mech5.py` (`rev5/w5_witnesses.py`). The `_named` row rests on `critic4/a5_tool_name_eq.py`. The rows retired with revision 4's announcement and wait are not in the catalog;
- revision 5: every row of the revision-5 line of the Mutation-audit table, each with the witness named there. All except the `_Txn` and facade rows were checked on `rev5/mech5.py`: under the spec every sweep trial and every hold case gives the stated outcome, and under each single-rule mutant the named witness differs, on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (`rev5/w5_witnesses.py`, identical verdicts on both versions). The `_Txn` row rests on `rev5/p_txn_shared_succ.py`. The facade row rests on the fifth critic's reading (MF4); `mech5.py` passes its tracer object to `_run_async` and cannot show it. `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py mutants` also finds a property violation for each model-level mutant except two: `ensure_nocount`, whose only effect is a note on a loss-free free (X137e witnesses it), and `prune_credit_stop`, the deleted rule restored, which changes nothing;
- revision 6: every row of the revision-6 line of the Mutation-audit table, each with the witness named there, including F14's row (the name-gated close read, X137f). Checked on `rev6/mech6.py` (`rev6/w6_witnesses.py`, `rev6/w6_greenlet.py`, `rev6/r6_m1_builtin.py`, `rev6/p_first_reconcile.py`; identical verdicts on both versions): X154b, X154c, X137f, X137g, X137h and X156 give the stated outcome under the spec and differ under their single-rule mutants. X156b and X37b rest on the same binding and version code paths. `rev6/m6_modelcheck.py mutants` finds a property violation for `reg_rev5`, `rebind_count_none` and `blind_read_gated`; `rebind_no_local` (a loss that X5's local-events test notes) is not a property of the model;
- revision 7: every row of the revision-7 lines of the Mutation-audit table, each with the witness named there.
