Checked on `rev7/mech7.py` (`rev7/w7_witnesses.py`, one subprocess per trial; identical verdicts on both versions): X158, X158b, X137h (revision 7 form), X154d, X157c, X156c, X156d, X37b, and the self-audit's V67 and V68 give the stated outcome under the spec and differ under their single-rule mutants (`rev7/w7_audit.py` for the last two). V69's and X78f's variant rows rest on reading. X37b's mutant (a version read at import) is not implemented in `mech7.py`; its row rests on reading. `rev7/m7_modelcheck.py mutants` finds a property violation for `cbrep_nocount` and `count_after_register` (the `cb2:` configurations; the latter only with a fault) and for `blind_read_gated` in two new fault configurations; the Revision 7 table has the rest;
- revision 8: every row of the revision-8 line of the Mutation-audit table, each with the witness named there. Checked on `rev8/mech8.py` (identical verdicts on both versions): X158d (`rev8/c1_on_mech8.py`, mutant `reg_rev7`), X158c (`rev8/w8_new.py`, mutant `count_excl_none`) and V70 (`rev8/a13_witness.py`, mutant `pend_bare_id`) give the stated outcome under the spec and differ under their single-rule mutants. Revision 7's witness files re-run on `mech8.py` with byte-identical verdicts (`rev8/out_w8_*`). `rev8/m8_modelcheck.py mutants`, run twice (N9), finds LOSTNOTE for `count_after_release`; the Revision 8 section has the rest;
- revision 8 self-audit: every row of the self-audit line of the Mutation-audit table, each with the witness named there. Checked on `rev8/mech8.py` by the verifier's probes in `verify8/` (`/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/verify8/`, identical verdicts on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12): X137i (`pend_get`), X154e (`adopt_no_register`), V71 (`frame_after_append`) and X158e (`cbrep_nocount`) give the stated outcome under the spec and differ under their single-rule mutants;
- the hazard-sweep detection mutants H1, H2, H6 (M1–M7), H7, H8 and H9, with the sweep as the named witness;
- one weakening per operator family below, inside each rule-tagged region of `ref_v5f.py`;
- boundary rows on both sides of every quantitative rule: 16 hops accepted (V11c) and 17 refused (X30b); the 10 s busy bound, 11 s refused MACHINERY_BUSY (X138) and 7 s accepted (V65), both measured from the waiter's start (revision 2, M12). `_BUSY_SECONDS` is a float, which O7 (int ±1) never touches. So SM1 carries explicit rows for 0.5 s, ×0.5 and ×2, and SM2 gains operator O7b.

### SM2: operator mutants on the real implementation (the procedure is frozen; the mutants depend on the code)
**Region.** Defined by name, not by a marker the implementer places. It is the code objects of the frozen `_v5_faultpoints()` functions (M10), plus `Experiment._check_coverage`, `check_metrics`, `score` and `_check_trace_shape`, plus the module-level statements that bind the M1 names. G_HYG checks that `styxx/protocol.py` defines no function or class outside this set and the v4-era set. The v4-era set is listed by name in the prereg at freeze time, from the base commit.

**Operators,** applied mechanically at every applicable site. These are D1's O1–O14 plus D3's generic families:

| family | operators |
|---|---|
| deletion | O1 delete a statement; O13 refusal deletion by coded literal (any string constant or f-string part containing `[V5:` → its innermost enclosing statement becomes `pass`) |
| conditions | O2 negate a test; O3 drop one operand of and/or; IF: a test replaced by True / False; QUANT any↔all |
| comparisons | O4 `is`↔`==`, `is not`↔`!=`; CMP `==`↔`!=`, `<`↔`<=`, `>`↔`>=`; O6 `in`↔`not in` |
| types | O5 exact type ↔ `isinstance`/`issubclass`, both directions |
| constants | O7 every int constant ±1; O7b every float constant ×0.5 and ×2 (revision 2, M12: `_BUSY_SECONDS` and the 0.2 ms retry sleep are floats) |
| order | O8/SWAP: swap adjacent simple statements; O14 swap two sequential `if …: raise GateSpecError` blocks |
| claims | O9 `d.setdefault(k, v) is v` → unconditional store |
| scope | O10/ITER: `for x in seq` → `seq[:1]` / `seq[-1:]`; SLICE `[a:]` → `[a:a+1]`; O11 delete a `break` |
| immutability | O12 tuple rebuild → in-place mutation |

**TCE.** A mutant whose compiled module is byte-identical (bytecode, names, consts, recursively) to the original is equivalent by construction and is not run.

**Instruments,** in order, stopping at the first that detects the mutant:
- (a) the frozen exam in mutation mode;
- (b) the frozen crash sweep with the frozen stride (G_FI: every third point of the frozen enumeration);
- (c) the frozen fuzzer with frozen seeds and N;
- (d) the differential probe: the mutant against the unmutated implementation over the frozen `corpus_v5f/`.

**The normalizer compares only spec-fixed observables.** This list is closed:
- refusal codes, and exactly these message substrings:
  - the leading `[V5:CODE]`;
  - the NOT_EXERCISED fixed sentence (M11);
  - the `dispatched {…}, unattributed {…}` labels with their dict renderings;
  - the two NESTED_SECTION texts;
  - the LAZY_RESULT text and its "(its body had not started)" suffix;
  - the three NO_TRACE wordings;
  - "int too large for a float";
  - the TRACE_ACTIVE texts of M8;
  - for NOT_A_FUNCTION, FOREIGN_DEFINITION, INHERITED, INSTANCE_PATH and UNRESOLVED, the `module:qualname` (or `'<module>:<name>.__wrapped__'`) that this spec says the message names, as `ref_v5f.py` computes it for the same program;
- counts, ends, note codes and problem codes;
- `check_metrics` `present`/`usable` and each note's leading code;
- `_v5_state()` fields;
- fixture `__code__` identity;
- `sys.getprofile()`/`gettrace()` identity;
- styxx's tool ownership and the fixtures' local events.

Free-form message text, addresses, paths and timings are not compared. The **noise mask** is computed over the manifest's `equality` entries only. It masks a field iff the field differs between any two of exactly N = 5 unmutated runs on that version, run in the frozen corpus order with the frozen seeds. The mask is computed once, recorded and reported. If it masks any refusal code, count, end, note code or problem code, the baseline is unclean and G_SEM fails: the spec fixes those, so they must not vary.

**`envelope` entries** (revision 2, M5: "faulted" is now defined by the manifest) are never compared for equality. Each mutant output is checked against the entry's frozen acceptance predicate:
- for an injected fault, envelope I6 computed from the unmutated fault-free run of the same program;
- for a timing-dependent case, the outcome set or gate predicate the exam states for that case (X140f's three outcomes, X71c's landing rule, the G_SIG gates).

A mutant is flagged iff its output falls outside the predicate on some run. Nothing about an entry's class is decided after the freeze.

**Classification:**
- **KILLED:** (a), (b) or (c) detects it, or the mutant does not import;
- **EXAM_HOLE:** only (d) sees a spec-fixed difference;
- **UNDISTINGUISHED:** nothing does. It is counted and listed with its site and diff. No one judges whether it is equivalent, before or after the freeze, and it is not gated (judge 1). The shipping claim states the count and says that G_SEM certifies nothing about these mutants. Round 5 may attack them like any other code; a finding there is an ordinary red-team finding, not a reclassification. D3's "≤3%" cap is not adopted: both judges rejected it.

**Gate.** All of:
- EXAM_HOLE = 0 on both verified interpreters;
- a clean baseline (every instrument passes the unmutated implementation);
- at least one applied mutant per family;
- G_REF: 100% of O13 mutants KILLED, and the literal census finds 0 coded literals outside a mutable site.

KILLED, UNDISTINGUISHED and TCE counts are reported per family.

### SM3 (aux, carried): the independent second implementation
- It comes from a different author **and** model family than the implementer. Round 4 showed correlated failure: the N-version implementation reproduced all 6 blockers (`round4/blockers_against_nversion.json`).
- It passes the frozen exam, matches the oracle fuzzer, and gives identical normalized traces on both verified interpreters.
- Every round 1–4 blocker repro is replayed against both implementations, and none may reproduce.

### Positive controls (frozen; run before any v5f scoring; each must come out as stated, or G_SEM is void)
1. **SM1 on v5e.** SM1's admission and kill machinery is run on v5e's frozen exam with the 63 census mutants applied to v5e unchanged. For this control only, any failing case counts as a witness. There are no named witnesses, so SM1's admission conditions 2 and 3 are replaced by condition 1 alone, and the only classes are **KILLED** (some v5e exam case fails) and **SURVIVED** (none does). UNWITNESSED and WITNESS_MISMATCH do not arise (revision 2, N10). The control passes iff exactly the other 27 census mutants are KILLED and these 36 are SURVIVED, matching `semantic_mutation_census.json` (63 applied, 27 detected, 36 survived): B01, C01, C02, C06, D01, D02, E01, E04, E05, E07, E09, E11, H01, H12, H15, K01, O05, O07, O08, P01, P02, P03, P04, R01, R02, R03, S01, S02, S03, S07, S08, S10, S15, SC1, SC2, T01.
2. **SM2 on v5e.** SM2's generator, run on v5e, with instruments (a) and (d) only, must report EXAM_HOLE > 0 (revision 2, M11):
   - (a) is v5e's own frozen exam;
   - (d) is the differential probe over the corpus programs that use only the public API v5e shares with v5f.

   Instrument (b), the crash sweep, is excluded because control #3 requires it to find v5e unclean. Instrument (c), the fuzzer, is excluded because its oracle is `ref_v5f.py`, which v5e is not meant to match. SM2's clean-baseline condition applies to (a) and (d) only. v5e passes its own exam, and (d)'s mask is built from five unmutated v5e runs. On v5e the normalizer omits the `_v5_state()` fields and tool ownership, which v5e does not have, and compares every other listed observable. D3's 240-mutant sample let 83 survive, the witnessed holes among them.
3. **Crash sweep on v5e.** The frozen `crash_sweep_v5f.py` is run against v5e through `controls_v5f.py`'s v5e adapter. The adapter supplies the fault points (`_CoverageTracer.__enter__`, `__exit__`, `_open`, `_close`, `run`, `record`, `_hook` and `_resolve_target`) and a `_v5_state()`-shaped view of v5e's registries (`_MINTED`, `_BY_FN`, `_ANCHORS`, `_THREADS`, `_ACTIVE`) for C5 and C7. Fields v5e does not have (`cut`, `cut_current`, `tool`, `tool_ours`, `global_events`) read as their `S0` values. It must report at least one non-clean point on 3.12 and on 3.13. The committed results of `fault_injection_v5.py`, which the sweep extends, are cited for reference only: on 3.12, 2393 of 9369 points not clean, 132 of them HANG; on 3.13, 2041 of 8604 not clean, 127 HANG. D1's M1–M7 are SM1 rows (above), not a control.
4. **G_SIG on v5e.** G_SIG must show v5e leaking on 3.12 and 3.13 (D3 sig_sweep: 2521 and 2265 leaks), and H8's publish-at-entry mutant must fail on 3.12.
5. **G_REF blind shapes.** A planted blind-shape emission must be caught by G_HYG, for each of the 8 shapes in `mutation_gate_blindspots.json`.

### Companion gates
- **G_FI (crash sweep, frozen).** Required on both verified interpreters (3.12.3 and 3.13.12), on two threads: every fault point is **clean**. Clean is defined by outcome alone, so no window has to be mapped to implementation offsets. A point is clean iff C1–C8 below hold, and C9 as well in the fork scenario. Revision 2's clauses (a)–(e) are C3, C5 with C7, C8, C8's second reading, and C2.

  **The invariants C1–C9 (revision 3, MF5; frozen with this spec).** They are stated only in terms of the public API, `_v5_state()` (M10) and I1–I6. `crash_sweep_v5f.py` implements them as written here and reads no other private name. D1's `crashcons/t_crash_sweep.py` was their source; its private reads (`reconcile_now()`, `leftovers()`, `_TH`, `sys.getprofile()` as a hook check) have no counterpart here.

  *Vocabulary.*
  - The *scenario* is the frozen program run at a point. It constructs tracers only through `coverage_trace()`, catches nothing, and so stops at the first exception that reaches it. The *faulted tracers* are the tracers it constructed. Revision 4 (MF3): every scenario joins every thread it started before any tracer's `__exit__`, so no open or close in it runs concurrently with an exit, and its baseline record is deterministic. Revision 5 (MF5) goes further. Every scenario orders its machinery calls across threads with frozen barriers, so no `coverage_trace()`, enter, exit, `run()`/`run_async()` open or close, or `record()` on one thread overlaps a machinery call on another. Sections' bodies may overlap, which is how two sections are open at once. The order in which opens and closes happen across threads is fixed by the scenario, so each machinery call finds the same registry state in every run: the same anchors, the same event, the same mutex owner. So the instruction path of every call on the faulted thread, and with it the point set, is the same in every run. Revision 4's point set was not: whether the faulted thread ran `_await_clearers`'s loop, and how many times, depended on another thread's timing (the fifth critic's MF5; `rev4/out_b4c.txt`). Revision 5 has no such loop, and the mutex's wait loop is not reached in a sequential scenario. Races between concurrent transitions are covered by `m5_modelcheck` (Revision 5 table), the instruction-sweep witnesses and X147, not by G_FI.
  - The *faulted thread* (revision 4, MF3). Every scenario names the one thread the injector faults. The injector's filter admits that thread's live ident only. The single-thread scenarios name the scenario thread. The two-thread scenario is swept twice, once with its scenario thread named and once with its worker thread named. The `run_async`, generator-target, hopped-coroutine and background variants name the scenario thread. The fork scenario names the child's only thread.
  - A *fault point* (revision 4, MF3) is a triple `(key, offset, k)`. `key` is a `_v5_faultpoints()` key, `offset` an instruction offset in that code object (it has no nested code objects: G_HYG, MF4), and k ≥ 1. The injector raises at the k-th execution of that offset on the faulted thread in that run, counted from the moment it is armed. A scenario's point set is every triple with k ≤ K = 2 whose offset a *discovery run* executed at least k times on the faulted thread. The discovery run is the baseline run with the injector armed in counting mode, raising nothing. Revision 5 (MF5): the discovery run is made twice, at the start of the sweep, and the two point sets must be identical, or the G_FI run is void and counts as a failure of the instrument. At freeze time the same check is run on `ref_v5f.py`, whose point sets are committed with the freeze as a record, not as the set G_FI uses: the implementation's code differs. So an offset inside a loop gets its first two executions as separate points. The frozen enumeration order is scenario, faulted thread, key, offset, k. G_FI runs every point. The *frozen stride*, used by SM2's instrument (b) and G_COVER's pass 2 only, is 3: the points whose index in that order is divisible by 3.
  - The *baseline* is the same scenario run once per version, fault-free, at the start of the sweep. It gives each faulted tracer's baseline record, the scenario's result, and the snapshot `B = _v5_state()` taken after it.
  - A *probe cycle* runs on a new thread: `cov = coverage_trace(EXP_PROBE)`, then `with cov: cov.run('P', probe)`, then `score` of `cov.record()`. `EXP_PROBE` declares one function, in a probe module that nothing else uses. The cycle *completes* iff the thread finishes within 5 s and the score is PASS `{probe: 1}`. It contains two transactions, so it runs two reconciliations.
  - `S0 = _v5_state()` is taken before the scenario. In the background variant, the background thread is parked at a barrier outside its sections whenever a snapshot or check is taken; it runs only while the scenario runs.

  *When each check runs, in order:*
  1. The scenario runs with the injector armed, and the injector is disarmed. C2 and C6 are checked.
  2. `record()` of every faulted tracer is read. C1 and C8 are checked.
  3. A probe cycle runs while the faulted tracers are still referenced. C3 is checked.
  4. `_v5_state()` is read. C7 is checked.
  5. Each faulted tracer whose `record()` refused TRACE_ACTIVE gets one more `__exit__(None, None, None)`, and its `record()` is read again. C1 and C8 are checked on it (revision 2's clause (d)).
  6. Every reference to the faulted tracers is dropped (the scenario's result, the records, the tracers), then `gc.collect()` runs, then a second probe cycle. C5 is checked.
  7. In the background variant, at the end of the sweep, C4 is checked.

  *The invariants:*
  - **C1, no over-credit.** In every record read at steps 2 and 5, no `calls` count exceeds the baseline record's count for the same section and target. Each frozen sentinel gate declares a fixture function that the scenario calls only outside that gate's declared section. Every sentinel gate scores NOT_EXERCISED or a refusal, never PASS.
  - **C2, no swallowing.** One of these holds:
    - the scenario raised the injected exception object itself (`is`), out of the facade call during which it was injected;
    - `sys.unraisablehook`, installed by the sweep to record, received it (`exc_value is` the injected object). This is the path when the fault lands in code that CPython runs as a finalizer, including a suspended `run_async` section's `finally` under the GeneratorExit of `coro.close()` at deallocation. Revision 3 restores this alternative from D1's C2 (MF7);
    - `__enter__` raised GateSpecError UNRESOLVED whose `__cause__ is` the injected object;
    - (revision 4, MF3) the faulted thread was a worker thread the scenario started, and it ended with the injected object: the sweep's frozen `threading.excepthook`, installed to record, received it (`args.exc_value is` the injected object, `args.thread` the faulted thread). The scenario's own thread then sees no exception, and the worker's openings are finalised by exit's X3.

    A point whose fault never fired is UNREACHED, counts as clean and is reported.
  - **C3, no hang and no held exclusion.** Step 3's probe cycle completes.
  - **C4, containment (background variant).** A background tracer runs on another thread. It was entered before the sweep and stays active through it, declares the scenario's target (so it shares the mint), and opens its own sections calling that target, which returns rather than raises, so its counts do not depend on PY_UNWIND. At the end of the sweep its record has exactly one count per call it made, no problem, and no note. Revision 3 allowed MONITOR_LOST here when the injector was armed on `_unwind_off`'s code. Since the sweep covers every faultpoint code, that allowance was always granted (the fourth critic's N4). Revision 4 removes it, because no instrument can cause MONITOR_LOST any more (M7, I6). After the background tracer exits at the end of the sweep, `_v5_state()["mints"]` is `[]`: its retire drops any pending entries (C5's exclusion below).
  - **C5, nothing left once the faulted tracers are gone.** After step 6, `_v5_state()` equals `S0` except that `cut` may have grown, `tool` may have been set, and, in the background variant, the `pending` field of the background tracer's target mint is not compared. That field can differ fault-free (revision 4, MF6). A frame of the target entered while any anchor is registered anywhere stores a pending entry. If it raises after the last anchor closed, S is clear and nothing pops the entry until the mint retires, which the background tracer's hold prevents during the sweep (`critic4/a6_pending_left_fault_free.py`: 1 entry left with `anchors` 0 and `global_events` 0, both versions). C4 checks that it is gone once the background tracer exits. In particular:
    - `mints` lists only the background tracer's target, if any, with `holders` 1;
    - `anchors` is 0;
    - `guard` is `"free"`;
    - `global_events` is 0, so no stale event (I3 at quiescence);
    - `cut_current` is True.

    Also every fixture function's `__code__` is its original, except the background target's, which is its minted code. `sys.getprofile()` and `sys.gettrace()` are unchanged on the main thread.
  - **C6, nothing else escapes.** No exception other than the injected one leaves the scenario, and no thread the scenario started ends with any exception other than the injected one (which C2's fourth alternative then accounts for).
  - **C7, only live holders, while the faulted facades are alive.** At step 4, every entry of `_v5_state()["mints"]` has `holders` equal to the number of live tracers declaring that target. A live tracer is the background tracer, or a faulted tracer whose step-2 `record()` refused TRACE_ACTIVE with the "the tracer is active" text, which is a zombie (L-ZOMBIE). A faulted tracer whose `record()` returned a record, or refused TRACE_INCOMPLETE, holds nothing. No entry has `holders` 0, and `installed` is True for every entry.
  - **C8, the envelope.** Every record read at step 2 is within I6 against its tracer's baseline record, or `record()` refused TRACE_INCOMPLETE or TRACE_ACTIVE. Within I6 means:
    - counts at most the baseline's;
    - each `end` in {the baseline end, `"raised"`, `"open"`};
    - notes within the baseline notes plus OPEN_AT_EXIT (revision 4: no MONITOR_LOST allowance; I6);
    - problems equal to the baseline's, because no sweep scenario enters or exits a tracer from inside a transition.

    Every record read at step 5 meets the same test, and there TRACE_ACTIVE is a failure.
  - **C9, the forked child (fork scenario only).** Checked in the child and reported to the parent through a pipe:
    - `run()` passes through, returning fn's value with no exception;
    - after the child's next transaction, a probe cycle, `_v5_state()` shows `anchors` 0, `mints` `[]` and `global_events` 0;
    - every fixture function's `__code__` is its original, or is equal to it (`==`) and has no local events on `_v5_state()["tool"]`;
    - the parent's own trace ends with its baseline record.

  The SM1 crash rows (M1 → C3, M2/M4/M7 → C7, M3/M5/M6 → C5) name these definitions.

  **Scenarios added in revision 2:**
  - *Fork (N5).* A trace with a mint, an open section and a pending entry forks. In the child, the injector faults `_forget_in_child` at each of its instructions in turn, and an audit hook refusing `__code__` writes is a second variant. C9 is checked. The scenario runs in a fresh subprocess that starts no thread before the fork (N10).
  - *Callback-only functions (BF1, M8).* The injector faults the five callback-only functions at each instruction while the direct-call driver runs them. Clean means C1, C3, C6 and C8, and the driver's own trace counts ≤ its fault-free counts. A fault injected into a directly driven callback propagates to the driver, so C2 reads "the driver's call raised it".
  - *Injector rules.* The Exam harness rules apply: code read at arming, a thread filter on a live ident, no settrace, `restart_events()` before each trial, and a self-check. A trial that never fires is UNREACHED, counts as clean, and is reported.
- **G_SIG (signal floods, frozen, 60 s per cell on each verified interpreter).** Required: 0 user-lock leaks, 0 hangs, 0 poison, 0 wrong results, and credited ≤ body runs. Revision 2 (M4) freezes what "wrong result" means:
  - a skipped `with`/`finally` cleanup;
  - a result value that differs on an iteration in which no asynchronous exception was delivered;
  - a later trace whose record differs from its fault-free record.

  **Poison (revision 3, N8)** is that third bullet, checked one fixed way. After each cell, with the flood disarmed, the harness drops the cell's tracers, runs `gc.collect()`, and runs one probe cycle (G_FI's). The cell is poisoned if the probe cycle does not complete, or if `_v5_state()` after it differs from the snapshot taken before the cell in any field but `cut` and `tool` (C5's test). "0 poison" counts such cells. A poisoned cell is not also counted as a wrong result.

  An `except E:` handler that did not start because an asynchronous exception replaced E inside the unwind callback is L-DELIVERY, not a wrong result. It is measured in the three L-DELIVERY cells (H10), and cell (a), outside every section, is gated at 0.
- **G_HYG (static).** These must hold:
  - 0 coded emissions in a blind shape;
  - no `with` statement, no `threading` lock and no `try … finally` in the v5 region, except `_run`/`_run_async`'s single `try/finally`;
  - no loop in any `try` body (CPython #130279). The one allowed `await` is `_run_async`'s; its try body is `_commit(o)`, that `await`, and assignments (revision 3). `_run`'s try body is `_commit(o)`, the call of fn, and assignments. Each `finally` is exactly `_detach(o, (end, ()))` then `o.frame = None` (revision 4). Revision 5: no function reachable from a `try` body or a `finally` has a loop, other than `_run_async`'s `await`. The lint lists every backward jump in the code objects of `_commit`, `_detach`, `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take`, `_register`, `_set_local` and `_named`, and requires none (M7; `rev5/spec_rev5_eventpath.py`);
  - (revision 4, MF4) no region code object has a code object among its `co_consts`: no nested function, lambda, class or generator expression. List, set and dict comprehensions are inlined on 3.12 and 3.13 (PEP 709) and are allowed;
  - no `except` in the region other than the two chained user-code sites and `_exit`'s two `except GateSpecError` clauses around `_locked`. In the scoring functions, the only ones allowed are check_metrics' existing `except GateSpecError` report loops and `except OverflowError` (`_finite` and score's metric guard);
  - no `import` statement and no `importlib`/`__import__` call in the region, except `_resolve_target`'s single `importlib.import_module(module)` and `coverage_trace`'s `import asyncio`;
  - no type test by tuple membership (`type(x) in (…)`, `in _LAZY_TYPES`) in the region;
  - no `sys.monitoring` event other than PY_START, PY_RESUME, PY_RETURN, PY_YIELD and PY_UNWIND is named in the region;
  - the region's function and class names are exactly the frozen set (SM2 Region);
  - (revision 2) `_forget_in_child` writes `__code__` only in its last loop, after every registry and event step; `_detach`'s only anchor pop is inside its `_unwind_off(fr)` call (revision 5);
  - (revision 5, replacing revision 4's clause) `_commit`'s statements are, in order: the anchor store `_ANCHORS[o.frame] = o`; the step-8 test of `'fin' in o.fin` or `'exiting'` (with `_detach`, the TRACE_INACTIVE append and the raise); `_unwind_on(o)`; `o.armed = True`. `_open` stores no anchor and calls no `_unwind_on`. `_unwind_on` is called only from `_commit`;
  - (revision 5; rewritten in revision 6, M4, to match M1 and M7 exactly; revision 8, M1–M3) the step functions. `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take` and `_set_local` each consist of exactly two statements, and `_register` of exactly three (revision 8):
    1. one assignment whose targets are locals and whose values are `_TOOL[0]` and items `_MON[0][i]` with literal i (in `_take`, `_register` and `_set_local`, `t` is a parameter and only `_MON[0]` items are read). **Revision 8 (M3): each step binds exactly its frozen set, under the frozen names:** `_unwind_on` binds `t = _TOOL[0]`, `get_tool = _MON[0][0]`, `get_events = _MON[0][1]` and `set_events = _MON[0][2]`; `_unwind_off` binds `t`, `get_tool` and `set_events`; `_take` binds `get_tool` and `use_tool_id = _MON[0][5]`; `_register` binds `get_tool` and `register_callback = _MON[0][4]`; `_set_local` binds `get_tool` and `set_local_events = _MON[0][3]`. So `set_events` is callable only in `_unwind_on` and `_unwind_off`, and `_register` cannot write S. Revision 7 let statement 1 bind any `_MON[0][i]`, so a `_register` that cleared S passed this lint (the eighth critic's K13);
    2. in `_register` only (revision 8, M1): `a, b = _tee(P)`, one assignment to two locals whose value is one `_tee` call. P is the exchange pipeline `_map(register_callback, _compress(_repeat(t), <the per-exchange gates>), _EVENTS5, _CALLBACKS5)`. The statement builds, and reads and writes nothing;
    3. one statement whose outermost call is `_CONSUME(...)` (in `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take`, `_set_local`, as an expression statement) or `_list(...)` (in `_register`, as a `return`).

    The statements after statement 1 (the *pipeline statements*) may name only: the M1 one-call vocabulary (`_map`, `_chain`, `_chain.from_iterable`, `_compress`, `_filter`, `_is`, `_not`, `_and`, `_setitem`, `_ARMED`, `_FLAGS`, `_VALUES`, `_CONSUME`, `_LOST_KEY`, `_TRUE`, `_PYU1`, `_ZERO1`, `_NONE1`, `_NAME1`, `_repeat`, `_list`, `_EVENTS5`, `_CALLBACKS5`, `_TOOL_NAME`, from revision 7 `_is_not` and `_LOST_APPEND`, which only `_register` names, and from revision 8 `_tee`, which only `_register`'s statement 2 names); the locals bound by statement 1 (and, in `_register`, `a` and `b`); the function's parameters (`o`, `key`, `t`, `code`, `events`); the attribute `o.frame`; `_ANCHORS` and its attributes `get` and `pop`; and literal tuples of names in this list or of literals. Every call in them is a call of one of those names or of a `_MON[0]` local. No lambda, generator expression, comprehension, `def` or class appears in them. **Revision 8 (M2): no operator other than a call, those attribute reads and a tuple display appears in them.** That excludes a subscript, a comparison (`in`, `is`, `==` and the rest), a boolean, unary or arithmetic operator, a conditional expression, an assignment expression and a starred argument. Without this, a test built from allowed names by bytecode, such as `(o.frame in _ANCHORS,)` as a gate, passed the name list and ran before the consuming call: a test-then-act split that G_ATOM cannot see (the eighth critic's K12 shape, moved into the pipeline statement; control K12b). `_CALLBACKS5` is data: its functions are arguments of `register_callback` and operands of `_is_not`, and the lint checks that `_CALLBACKS5` appears only as an argument of `_map` in `_register`. The literal None may appear as `_filter`'s first argument (revision 7) and as `_repeat`'s argument (revision 8). Revision 5's version of this clause omitted `_repeat`, `_EVENTS5`, `_CALLBACKS5` and the `_MON[0]` locals, and so flagged its own reference code, and required `list` while `list` was read from builtins at call time (the sixth critic's M4 i–iii, v);
  - (revision 6, M4 iv) `_Opening` and `_Core` each have a class body consisting of `__slots__ = (<string literals>)` listing exactly their M1 fields and one `def __init__`; nothing else, so no `__getattribute__`, `__getattr__`, property or other Python-level descriptor exists (M1);
  - `set_events`, `set_local_events`, `register_callback` and `use_tool_id` are called nowhere but in the step functions, except `_forget_in_child` in the single-threaded child. Within the step functions, each is called only by the steps whose frozen set binds it (revision 8, M3, above). `_retire` has no step 6. `_reconcile` ends with the prune of cores reached from anchors and then `if _TOOL[0] is not None: _unwind_off(None)` (revision 6, F20), in that order. `_CLEARING`, `_await_clearers` and any wait outside `_acquire` do not exist;
  - **Scope (revision 6).** This lint checks the pipelines' *shape* only. It cannot see an audit event raised inside a C function, a wrapper bound at run time, or the type a C callable meets at run time. The companion dynamic gate G_ATOM checks those, on each listed patch level. **Revision 8 (M2): the division runs the other way too.** G_ATOM sees calls, not bytecode. A read or write of shared state done outside the consuming call by bytecode (a subscript, `CONTAINS_OP`, a method-wrapper call such as `_LOST.__iadd__`, a count placed after the call) is excluded by this lint's statement count, its statement-1 form and its operator ban, not by G_ATOM;
  - **G_HYG's step controls (revision 8, M2 and M3; frozen patches of `ref_v5f.py`, each of which the lint must reject, in the same run that passes the reference, or G_HYG fails).** Each is G_ATOM's patch of the same name (G_ATOM, "Controls"):
    - K11: `_register` counting after its consuming call through `_LOST.__iadd__` and an inlined comprehension (four statements);
    - K12: `_unwind_on` whose own-anchor gate is read by `in` and a subscript in statement 1 (statement 1 binds something other than `_TOOL[0]` and `_MON[0][i]`);
    - K12b: `_unwind_on` whose set is gated on `(o.frame in _ANCHORS,)` in the pipeline statement (the operator ban);
    - K13: `_register` binding `set_events` and clearing S, gated on the name, with its None swallowed by `_filter(None, …)` (the frozen index set);
    - K14: `_register` in revision 7's form, without `_tee` (two statements).

    `rev8/hyg8.py` implements the step clauses as written here. It passes `rev8/steps8.py` and rejects K11, K12, K12b, K13 and K14 by the clauses named (`rev8/out_hyg8.txt`, both versions). It also rejects K4–K7, K9 and K10 by the statement count or the name list. It does not reject K8, which changes an effect and not the shape, and is G_ATOM's;
  - (revision 4) `_detach` never assigns `o.frame`. `o.frame` is assigned only in `_open` and in `_run`'s and `_run_async`'s `finally`. `_detach`'s `UNWIND_LOST` test reads `o.armed`, then `get_events`, then `_ANCHORS.get(fr) is o`, in that order, and then calls `_unwind_off(fr)`, which is the only pop of an anchor in `_detach`;
  - (revision 5) `_TOOL_NAME` is tested only by identity: inside `_named`, whose test is `_MON[0][0](i) is _TOOL_NAME`, and in the one-call steps' `_is` gates;
  - (revision 5, N3) `_acquire` reads the clock only through `_monotonic` and sleeps only through `_sleep`; no function in the region reads `time.monotonic` or `time.sleep` at call time;
  - (revision 7, self-audit) no call of `sys.setprofile`, `sys.settrace`, `threading.setprofile`, `threading.settrace`, `threading.setprofile_all_threads` or `threading.settrace_all_threads`, and no read of those names, in the region. "What is removed from v5e" and R1-B4 claim this, and the leftover checks see only a lasting change, not a set-and-restore;
  - (revision 2; revision 6 names the list) exactly the names M1's reload paragraph lists are bound through `globals().get` (and `_TOOL_NAME` through `globals().get(...) or`); every other module-level name of the M1 block is a plain binding (N8; the sixth critic's M4 i, vi). `_MON` is assigned only in `coverage_trace()`'s binding, after the M0 builtin check.
- **G_COVER.** Every executable line of the region runs in at least one of the four passes below. No pragma exempts a line. A line that no pass reaches is dead code and must be deleted. Revision 3 drops revision 2's "or folded onto its guard's line" (N12; see "Line coverage only").
  - **Executable lines.** For each region code object, meaning the `_v5_faultpoints()` code objects read before any tracer exists plus the scoring functions of the SM2 region: `{line for _, _, line in code.co_lines() if line is not None} - {code.co_firstlineno}`. Revision 4 (MF4): G_HYG guarantees these code objects have no nested code objects, so no line is counted through an enclosing function's line.
  - **The measurement (revision 3, MF6).** One frozen LINE-event tool, `linecov_v5f.py`, written by the exam author and hashed in the prereg. It uses sys.monitoring tool id 0, which no exam tool or case uses, with global LINE events, because the self-trace mints some region functions and their minted copies must count. Its callback records `(co_filename, line)` for code whose `co_filename` is `styxx/protocol.py`'s path and returns None there. For all other code it returns `DISABLE`. So it never disables a location in machinery code (the injector rule of N5). Revision 3 also needed its LINE events not to split `_unwind_off`'s one-line test-and-clear; from revision 4 nothing depends on that (M7). A line counts iff this tool sees it in some pass. There is no settrace pass: settrace silences the id-5 injector (F1), so a crash sweep run under it never reaches the lines only a fault reaches.
  - **The four passes.** The tool is enabled at the start of every process each pass creates.
    1. **The exam's fast mode.** The frozen runner's `--fast` mode runs every exam case once, in its frozen placement (inside the self-trace, on the main thread before it, or in a fresh subprocess). It skips the hazard sweeps H1–H10, X140f and the G_SIG cells, and runs X71c under its landing rule. Each subprocess case (X36, both X137 subprocess variants, X142, V47) writes its lines to a file that the runner merges. The workers V47's pool forks are not measured; the fork pass-through lines are reached in pass 2's forked child.
    2. **The frozen crash sweep** at its frozen stride, with the background variant and the fork scenario; the forked child sends its lines through the scenario's pipe. This run's G_FI verdicts are discarded: G_FI's verdict comes from a run with no coverage tool (the injector rules).
    3. **The frozen fuzzer**, with its frozen seeds.
    4. **The frozen direct-call driver `cover_driver_v5f.py`**, counted for the named list below only.

    Lines that the spec requires and that run only in a subprocess are reached in pass 1's subprocess cases: `_reclaim`'s body (X137 free variant), MONITOR_BUSY's raise (X36), and `_ensure_tool`'s id-3 fallback (X142, X137b). Revision 5: `_take`'s and `_register`'s lines are reached at every first enter, and in pass 1's subprocess cases X137, X137e and X155. `_set_local`'s lines are reached at every mint and retire. The prune of cores reached from anchors is reached by X146d. The one-call steps have no branch in bytecode, so line coverage reaches all of each; which of their inner outcomes happen is gated by the sweep witnesses (X143, X145, X152–X155), not by G_COVER.
  - **Line coverage only (N12, accepted in writing).** G_COVER measures lines, not branches. A statement written on its guard's line, such as `if x: return`, counts as covered when the guard runs, whether or not the branch is taken. The machinery is written in that style throughout, so G_COVER shows only that no line of the region is dead. Which branches are taken is gated by the exam, SM1 and SM2, not by G_COVER. Branch coverage was not adopted. The only branch-level instrument on 3.12, BRANCH events, creates the #130279 skip (p13), and a gate on it would have to be read against that.
  - **The named list (revision 2, BF1), frozen here:** `_on_entry`, `_on_exit`, `_on_unwind`, `_outcome` and `_publish`, which run only inside sys.monitoring callbacks, and `_forget_in_child`, which runs only in a forked child's at-fork handler. The reason is measured: code inside a monitoring callback raises no events for any tool, and legacy settrace is suppressed there too. coverage.py 7.16.1 reports such lines as missing under both its sysmon and ctrace cores (critic2/`covt`; `rev2/p5_cover_driver.py`: 0 of 49 lines seen as real callbacks).
  - **The driver** is written by the exam author before the freeze, from M6's and M9's text, and hashed in the prereg. It builds `FunctionType(code, vars(styxx.protocol))` from the `_v5_faultpoints()` code object of each named function and calls it directly, outside any callback, inside real traces opened through the public API. Its frozen scenarios are one per branch of M6 and M9:
    - a non-minted code;
    - a CUT_MOVED binding;
    - foreign globals (CLONE_CALLED);
    - no registered anchor;
    - each outcome kind `c`, `a`, `d` and `u` (the `a` kind through V28b's manual hop);
    - two holders;
    - PY_RETURN;
    - PY_UNWIND at and past the entry offset;
    - a publication after the credit stop;
    - `_forget_in_child` in a spawned subprocess, as its last step.
  - **The driver's trampoline (revision 3, N7).** `_on_entry`, `_on_exit` and `_on_unwind` read `sys._getframe(1)` as the target's frame. So the driver calls each of them from one trampoline frame, a function built as `FunctionType(trampoline_code, vars(<target module>))` so that its `f_globals` is the target's module dict. The same trampoline invocation makes both the entry call and its exit or unwind call, because the pending entry is keyed by that frame. A call from any other frame records CLONE_CALLED into the real trace, which is the trace the scenario then scores, except in the CLONE_CALLED scenario itself. `rev2/p5_cover_driver.py` does this (`fake_target_frame`).
  - **Scope of the driver.** Lines of other functions executed by the driver do not count toward G_COVER. The driver cannot be used to cover anything else. `rev2/p5_cover_driver.py` shows a LINE tool seeing the callback functions' lines under a direct call, and not seeing them as real callbacks.
  - **After the freeze.** An implementation line in a named function that no frozen driver scenario reaches is dead code, like any other, and must be deleted. No exemption is granted after the freeze, and none is needed.
- **G_ATOM (revision 6, M4 and M5; rewritten in revision 7, M1; revision 8, M1–M4 and N3: the premise gate, dynamic, frozen and objective).** The one-call premise is a property of CPython, not of styxx's text, so it is gated by execution on every patch level in `_VERIFIED` (M0), on `ref_v5f.py` at the freeze and on the implementation. The frozen harness `atom_v5f.py` is written by the exam author and hashed in the prereg. `rev8/atom8.py` is its prototype (revision 7's was `rev7/atom7.py`). It runs against `rev8/steps8.py`, which holds M7's step functions as this text writes them, and against twelve single-step mutants of that file (K4–K14 and K12b, with K12 and K12b reported, not gated). It reads only the G_ATOM interface (M10) and `_v5_faultpoints()`. It calls each step as `FunctionType(code, vars(module))` with the code from `_v5_faultpoints()`, the same direct-call construction as G_COVER's driver. It prepares states through the G_ATOM interface, one enter and exit through the public API, and `sys.monitoring`. It runs in one fresh process per G_ATOM run and per control. Its output records the build identifier (`sys.version`, `platform.python_build()`, `platform.python_compiler()`; M0, N2). Nothing in it is decided by judgement: every case, expected effect, threshold and control is listed here.
  - **Instruments, armed around each step call.** A sys.monitoring tool on id 5 with global PY_START, and with CALL, C_RETURN and C_RAISE as local events on the step function's code. The step's CALL of its *consumer* opens the *interval*: the callable that `is _CONSUME`, or `is _list` for `_register`. The matching C_RETURN or C_RAISE closes it. An audit hook records every audit event raised on the step's thread. A `sys.setprofile` function records every `c_call` made from the step's own frame. The CALL events on the step's code are all recorded, not only the consumer's, each with whether the interval had already closed (revision 8, M2). The harness's own functions' code objects, and PY_START events on other threads, are excluded from the counts. Nothing else is.
  - **The case matrix (frozen, 67 cases; revision 7 had 64).**
    - `_unwind_on(o)`: 24 cases. The product of: the id named or held by another tool; o's anchor registered or not; the global event set or clear; and the other registered opening, none, one armed or one unarmed (each with its own core). o itself is unarmed, as it is at `_commit`'s call.
    - `_unwind_off(key)`: 24 cases. The product of: named or not; the key registered, a key not registered, or None; another anchor left after the pop or not; and the event set or clear.
    - `_take(t)`: 3 cases. The id unowned, held by another tool, or held by styxx.
    - `_set_local(t, code, events)`: 4 cases. Named or not, times events `_LOCAL` or 0, on a fixture function's code.
    - `_register(t)`: 12 cases. The id held by styxx with its five callbacks; held by styxx with its PY_UNWIND callback replaced by another party's (the B1 shape); the same two with the global PY_UNWIND **set** (revision 8, M3: revision 7's nine cases all began with S clear, so a `_register` that cleared S was invisible); held by styxx with its PY_START and PY_UNWIND callbacks replaced by two *finalizer callables* (revision 8, M1: instances of a harness class whose `__call__` returns None and whose `__del__` records whether the interval is open and `len(_LOST)`; the slots hold the only references); held by another tool; or unowned; and the *split* cases k = 1..5. In a split case an audit hook, at the k-th `sys.monitoring.register_callback` event inside the interval, runs a thread that frees the id, has another tool take it and register its own callbacks for the five events, and joins it. The harness's own interference is excluded from the step's audit count.
  - **Pass, per case (all must hold).**
    - (a) *Interval:* the consumer is called exactly once from the step's code, and that call returns or raises exactly once. The step's work is then inside the one call. A step that splits its work into a Python helper, or consumes with anything but the bound consumer, never opens the interval, and a vacuous 0-and-0 count fails here (the seventh critic's M1 item 3; `critic7/c3_gatom_vacuous.py`).
    - (b) *Inside:* no PY_START on the step's thread and no audit event. For `_register`: exactly one `sys.monitoring.register_callback` event per exchange made, where the number of exchanges made is the case's own (5, 0 or k; below), not read from the step (B1: expected, disclosed, not atomic). In the finalizer case (revision 8, M1), both finalizers run, and each runs after the interval has closed and sees `len(_LOST)` equal to its value before the step plus 2, both counts made. A finalizer that runs inside the interval, sees fewer counts, or never runs fails the case. The finalizer's own PY_START is harness code and is excluded above, so this is checked from the finalizer's record.
    - (c) *Outside:* between the step's start and its return, and outside the interval, no PY_START on the step's thread. The only `c_call`s the profile function sees from the step's frame are the consumer's, `itertools.chain.from_iterable`'s and, in `_register`, `itertools.tee`'s (revision 8). The latter two build lazy iterators and read nothing. **Revision 8 (M2):** every CALL event on the step's code is a call of `map`, `filter`, `itertools.chain`, `itertools.compress`, `itertools.repeat`, `itertools.tee`, `itertools.chain.from_iterable` or the consumer, and none comes after the interval has closed. So no Python function, C function, method descriptor or method-wrapper is called outside the one call. **What (c) does not see (revision 8, M2).** `sys.setprofile` reports `c_call` only for builtin functions and method descriptors, and CALL events report calls only. A subscript, a `CONTAINS_OP`, an attribute read or any other bytecode read or write of shared state outside the call is invisible to G_ATOM. G_HYG's step clauses exclude those statically (K12, K12b). Revision 7 said that (c) established "no read or write of shared state happens outside the one call". That overstated it: its K11 (a count after the call through `_LOST.__iadd__`) and K12 passed all 64 of revision 7's cases (`critic8/c2_gatom_bytecode_split.py`). K11 now fails the CALL criterion, and K12 remains G_HYG's.
    - (d) *State:* the observable state read at the consumer's CALL equals the state before the step. The state after the step equals the case's *expected effect*, which the harness computes from the rule of M7 for that gate outcome. The observable state is: the anchors registered (by identity), the id's owner, the id's global events, local events on the case's code, the flags of the prepared cores, and `len(_LOST)`. The expected effects:
      - `_unwind_on` sets the event and captures iff named and o registered, and captures (flags `UNWIND_LOST` on the core of every armed registered opening) iff the event was also clear;
      - `_unwind_off` pops the key and clears iff named and no anchor is left;
      - `_take` takes iff the id was unowned;
      - `_set_local` writes iff named;
      - `_register`:
        - held by styxx: 5 exchanges, returns `[_TOOL_NAME]`, and `len(_LOST)` is unchanged;
        - held by styxx with one callback replaced: 5 exchanges, returns `[None, _TOOL_NAME]`, `len(_LOST)` + 1, and styxx's five callbacks afterwards;
        - held by another tool or unowned: no exchange; returns `[owner]`;
        - split case k: k exchanges, returns `[None, <other>]` (the k-th exchange found the other tool's callback), `len(_LOST)` + 1, and afterwards exactly the other tool's k-th callback is styxx's;
        - held by styxx with S set, with or without the replaced callback (revision 8, M3): as the S-clear case, and the id's global events are still PY_UNWIND afterwards;
        - the finalizer case (revision 8, M1): 5 exchanges, returns `[None, None, _TOOL_NAME]`, `len(_LOST)` + 2, styxx's five callbacks afterwards, and the finalizer records required by (b).

        The number of exchanges is the case's, not read from the step, and criterion (b) compares the audit events with it. The callbacks are read by exchange and restore, after the step, with the hook disarmed.
    - Also, once per run: `_Opening.__dict__[n]` is a `types.MemberDescriptorType` for `armed`, `core` and `frame`, and so is `_Core.__dict__['flags']`. Revision 6 said "`armed`, `core` and `flags` on `_Opening`" in M1 and "`armed`, `core` and `frame` on `_Opening`, plus `_Core.flags`" here; `flags` is `_Core`'s (M1 item 4).
  - **Controls (frozen). Each must be detected in the same run, or G_ATOM fails** (revision 8, M4: revision 7 said "the run is void" and gave no rule for what follows a void run; see "No void run" below).
    - *Instrument controls*, on the harness's own pipelines:
      - K1: a pipeline calling a pure-Python pass-through of `set_events` (PY_START inside);
      - K2: an `attrgetter` over an object whose class has a Python `__getattribute__` (PY_START inside);
      - K3: a pipeline calling `sys._getframe` (an audit event inside).
    - *Hookup controls*: G_ATOM run in a fresh process on `ref_v5f.py` with one step replaced by a frozen text patch. The run must FAIL. These test the hookup to the implementation's steps, not the instruments:
      - K4: `_register` in revision 5's form, one name gate before all five exchanges, no owner read and no count. It fails all `_register` cases (9 of revision 7's matrix, 12 of revision 8's): in the split cases 6 − k of the other tool's callbacks are replaced, and its result has no owner element. So K4 tells revision 5's `_register` from revision 6's and 7's. Revision 6's K4 applied the one-call criterion, which both forms fail alike (M1 item 3);
      - K5: `_unwind_off` through a Python helper, the classic split (interval never opened, PY_START outside);
      - K6: `_unwind_off` consumed by a deque built at call time (interval never opened: the consumer is not `_CONSUME`);
      - K7: `_unwind_off` with the pop as its own statement before the consuming call (a C call outside the interval, and the state changed before the one call);
      - K8: `_unwind_on` whose set is not gated on o's own anchor (a wrong effect when o is not registered);
      - K9: `_take` with the unowned test as its own statement (`get_tool` called outside the interval);
      - K10: `_register` in revision 6's form, with the count made after the consuming call, as the seventh critic proposed (`list.extend` called outside the interval, and a wrong result);
      - K11 (revision 8, M2): `_register` counting after its consuming call through `_LOST.__iadd__` and an inlined list comprehension, the critic's shape (`critic8/c2`). It fails all 12 `_register` cases on the CALL criterion (c). Revision 7's criterion passed it;
      - K13 (revision 8, M3): `_register` binding `set_events` and clearing S when the id is named, its None swallowed by `_filter(None, …)` (`critic8/c5`). It fails the two S-set `_register` cases on (d);
      - K14 (revision 8, M1): `_register` in revision 7's form, without `_tee`. It fails the finalizer case: each finalizer runs inside the interval and sees `len(_LOST)` + 0 and + 1.

      **Static-only controls (revision 8, M2), run and reported, not gated here.** K12 (`_unwind_on` with its own-anchor gate read by `in` and a subscript in statement 1, `critic8/c2`) and K12b (the same read as a tuple display in the pipeline statement) pass all 67 cases on both versions, because G_ATOM cannot see bytecode. G_HYG must reject both (G_HYG's step controls).
  - **The frozen t1, D2 and R parts, with floors (revision 7, M1 item 5; revision 8, M4 and N3).** `rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py`'s parts A, B and E, `rev6/t1d2_discriminating.py`'s part D2 and (revision 8) part R are frozen with G_ATOM. Each has a floor on the work its one-call form must see, and a control that must violate. **Revision 8 (M4): every floor is a count, not a time.** Revision 7 ran part E for a fixed 3 s and part D2 for 3 s per form, and said a count below the floor voided the run. A machine about 30% slower, or a loaded host, would then void G_ATOM, and nothing said whether a void run could be repeated or how often. That was a decision after the freeze, in the gate everything else rests on. Now each timed part runs until its floor is reached and stops there. `T_MAX = 60 s` per form is a failure bound, not a measurement window: a form that has not reached its floor by `T_MAX` fails G_ATOM. The measured times to the floors are 1.6–2.3 s on both versions (`rev8/t8_floors.py`, `rev8/out_t8_floors.txt`), so `T_MAX` leaves a margin of about 25×. A control runs until its first violation. It fails G_ATOM if it reaches its own floor, or `T_MAX`, with none.
    - Part A, per instrument, for the one-call form: 0 violations, with at least 40 trials fired under INSTRUCTION, 25 under CALL+C_RETURN, 3 under the pure-Python `setprofile` function and 40 under opcode tracing (measured: 48, 32, 3 and 48 on both versions). Each of those four instruments' two-statement control must show at least 1 violation. The BRANCH row fires 0 events inside the one-call form on both versions. It is reported and is not evidence. Part A's floors count events, and it has no time bound to change.
    - Part B: the profile function sees exactly one `c_call` during the step, the consumer's.
    - Part E: over 4 threads, the one-call form runs until 50,000 body samples and must see 0 with the event clear (revision 8: measured 50,034–50,229 samples in 2.2–2.3 s; revision 7's fixed 3 s gave about 67,000–70,000). Its two-statement control runs until its first sample with the event clear, and must find one before 50,000 samples (measured: the first in 0.02 s).
    - Part D2: the one-call step runs until at least 5,000 closes and 5,000 interrupts and must show 0 violations (revision 8: measured 5,000 closes and 5,682–5,776 interrupts in 1.7–1.9 s). The Python-call control runs until its first violation, and must find one before 5,000 closes (measured: the first within 31 closes). Revision 5's two-statement control is reported and not gated, because it does not discriminate.
    - Part R (revision 8, N3; A14): the recursion sweep of `critic8/c4_a14_recursion_sweep.py`, frozen, run on the implementation's `_unwind_off`, `_unwind_on`, `_take` and `_set_local` through `FunctionType(code, vars(module))`. The recursion limit is set to 100,000. Each trial calls the step at C depth n, through one `next(map(...))` per level, from a state in which the step has an effect: for `_unwind_off`, the key registered as the last anchor with the event set; for `_unwind_on`, its own anchor registered, the event clear and one armed other opening; for `_take`, the id unowned; for `_set_local`, the id named and the local events different. A binary search over n in [0, 20,000] finds the largest n0 at which the step completes, and the sweep runs n0 − 30 … n0 + 30. It passes iff every trial either completes with the full effect or raises RecursionError with no effect, and both outcomes occur for every step. Its control is K7's `_unwind_off`, which must show a trial that raises RecursionError after its pop (measured: n0 = 4,998 on both versions; 31 full and 30 none per step; K7 has 1 partial trial; about 1 s per version; `rev8/r8_recursion_sweep.py`). A14's recursion claim is then gate-witnessed, not probe-only.
    - Revision 5's part D is dropped (the sixth critic's M5).
  - **No void run (revision 8, M4).** Every G_ATOM outcome is PASS or FAIL. A floor not reached by `T_MAX`, a control not detected, a hookup control that passes, or a case that fails is a FAIL, and nothing is re-run. Each G_ATOM run is committed with its output before its verdict is read. The verdict for a patch level on a given `ref_v5f.py` or implementation state is that state's first committed run. A later run belongs to a changed state (new code or a new build), and it is a new entry.
  - **What G_ATOM covers, and what it does not (revision 7, M1 item 5; revision 8, M2).** The implementation-facing part is the case matrix and part R. The matrix checks the implementation's own five steps for Python code, audit events and calls outside their one C call, under the instruments above, in every listed gate outcome. Revision 7 said "shared-state access" here. Bytecode-level reads and writes of shared state outside the call (a subscript, `in`, a method-wrapper call) are invisible to G_ATOM, and G_HYG's step clauses exclude them (K11, K12, K12b; the eighth critic's M2). Gc, signals and threads are tested only by the frozen t1 and D2 parts, which run reference pipelines built like the steps, not the implementation's steps. They test CPython's behaviour for that construction. So G_ATOM shows that the implementation's steps have the construction, and that CPython runs nothing inside that construction under these conditions. It does not show the second fact for conditions it does not create (M7, weakest points).
  - **Result on the prototype** (revision 8: `rev8/out_atom8.txt`, both versions): all 67 cases pass on `steps8.py`, with the slots correct. K1–K3 are detected. The hookup controls fail as required: K4 12 of 67 cases, K5, K6 and K7 24 each (every `_unwind_off` case), K8 3, K9 3, K10 12, K11 12, K13 2 and K14 1. K12 and K12b pass all 67 cases, as stated, and G_HYG rejects them (`rev8/out_hyg8.txt`). Parts E and D2 meet their count floors (`rev8/out_t8_floors.txt`), and part R passes with its control detected (`rev8/out_r8_recursion_sweep.txt`). Revision 7's result (`rev7/out_atom7_3.12.txt` and `_3.13.txt`) was all 64 cases passing on `steps7.py`, with K4 9 of 64 cases, K5, K6 and K7 24 each, K8 3, K9 3 and K10 9. The critic's c3 shapes are K5 and K6. `rev6/atom6.py`'s criterion passed them with the interval never opened (`critic7/out_c3.txt`). Revision 6's own prototype results (`rev6/out_atom6.txt`) are kept for its narrower criterion.
  - **Would have caught.** Revision 5's premise, "no one-call step raises an audit event", fails criterion (b) on `_register` itself (B1). A `set_events` wrapper bound before the first `coverage_trace()` (M1) is K1's shape; revision 6 refuses it at the binding, and X156 checks the refusal.

---

## Process gates for the v5f prereg

Carried, and adapted to the version policy:
- **G_EXAM_FROZEN.** Every `FROZEN_*_SHA256` in the prereg matches, read from the prereg as first committed. The freeze commit contains the runner, `ref_v5f.py`, the SM1 catalog, `sm1_result.json` and every gate script. It must be an ancestor of the first commit that carries the `/3` tracer id.
- **G0.** Violations refused with their own code: 1.0. The self-trace declares `Experiment._check_coverage`, `_resolve_target`, `_open`, `_exit` and `_run` (frozen names, M10) and must PASS (V34).
- **G1.** Valid cases exact: 1.0.
- **G2.** P1 retro exact on both verified interpreters (X118).
- **G3.** No v4/v5 disagreement over the pairable committed results.
- **G4.** Population ≥ 33.
- **G5.** Residual outcomes as documented: 1.0.

New:
- **G_INDEP.** The exam author, the implementer and the second-implementation author are three different people or agents, and the second implementation is from a different model family. `ref_v5f.py` is written from this spec alone, with a signed attestation. EQUIVALENT_BY_SPEC arguments are signed by a fourth reviewer.
- **G_V5E_DELTA.** At freeze time, the v5e cases ported to the `_v5_state()` interface are run against `ref_v5f.py`. Their outcome delta must equal the "v5e cases whose outcome changes" table exactly.
- **G_XVER.** Every case's outcome is identical on the two verified interpreters, 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (revision 7, M2: revision 6 said "3.12 and 3.13"). On 3.10.20 and 3.11.15 (revision 8, N8), X37 refuses UNSUPPORTED_VERSION and every scoring-only case matches.
- **G_SEM:** SM1 = 1.0 at the freeze; SM2 EXAM_HOLE = 0 after implementation; the positive controls as stated.
- **G_REF = 1.0** and **G_HYG = 0**, with G_HYG's step controls K11, K12, K12b, K13 and K14 rejected in the same run (revision 8).
- **G_ATOM (revision 6; revision 7 criteria; revision 8 matrix, floors and controls)** passes on every patch level in `_VERIFIED`, run on `ref_v5f.py` at the freeze and on the implementation before any scoring: all 67 cases pass, the t1, D2 and R parts meet their count floors within `T_MAX`, K1–K3 are detected in the same run, and K4–K11, K13 and K14 (patches of `ref_v5f.py`, each in its own process) fail, as they must, in the same run. Every outcome is PASS or FAIL; no run is void or repeated (revision 8, M4). A patch level is added to `_VERIFIED` only with a passing G_ATOM run committed beside it, naming its build.
- **G_COVER = 1.0**, with no pragmas; the callback-only functions are measured by the frozen direct-call driver (revision 2, BF1).
- **G_FI** and **G_SIG** as stated.
- **G_N (aux).** SM3.
- **G_CLOSURE.** The round 1–3 closure battery (`round4/closure-audit/r1/held_battery.py`, 41 rows) is re-targeted to the public API and holds 41/41 on both verified interpreters. Every round-4 blocker and defect repro no longer reproduces, or ends in its documented refusal.
- **G_RED.** A round-5 red team runs against the implementation before any shipping claim. The shipping claim names every UNDISTINGUISHED mutant (as uncertified, not as equivalent) and every residual.

---

## What round 5 should attack first

1. **The confirmation layer,** which is new to the winner.
   - Credit an entry whose body never ran: C-level resumption (`map(next, gens)`, itertools, the C Task step, `contextvars.Context.run`); `yield from` and `await` chains; a raising tool at PY_RESUME; PY_UNWIND offsets for frames that raise at the first instruction after RESUME.
   - Leak or strand pending entries: events cleared mid-frame; the tool freed; frames still running at retire.
   - Find 3.12.3 against 3.13.12 differences in PY_RESUME/PY_YIELD delivery.
2. **L-DELIVERY as possible program corruption.** A PY_UNWIND callback that raises replaces the in-flight exception, and on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 its `__context__` is None. The second critic answered round-5 question 2 with a probe: yes, an `except ValueError` flow in undeclared code loses its handler under a signal flood, at hundreds of thousands per 2M iterations. Revision 2 confines this to open sections: 0 outside them (`rev2/p4_unwind_scope.py`). It also freezes that G_SIG does not count it as a wrong result. Round 5 should attack what remains:
   - legitimate harnesses whose sections contain `except`-driven control flow under asynchronous exceptions (timeouts, KeyboardInterrupt handling);
   - the per-section toggle. Revision 5 replaces revision 4's announcement and wait with one-call steps (M7). Their soundness rests on one CPython property: no Python code, thread switch, signal handler, finalizer, audit hook, instrument callback or greenlet switch runs inside a C call that makes no Python-level call. Attack that premise. Look for a C function in a pipeline that calls back into Python: an `attrgetter` on a class with a Python `__getattribute__`, a dict whose keys hash in Python, a `sys.monitoring` call that raises an audit event on some patch level, gc run from an allocation inside the call, a debugger that preempts C code. Revision 6 refuses every patch level but 3.12.3 and 3.13.12, so attack those two, and attack G_ATOM itself: a patch level joins `_VERIFIED` only through it (revision 7 corrects revision 6's advice to check other patch levels, which the spec now refuses). Also attack the dead-anchor bound (U4): a fault at a close's first instruction keeps the event set until that trace exits or is pruned. Attack the outside-party residual too: a tool that clears styxx's event and sets it again itself, or that re-takes styxx's id under styxx's own name object;
   - the cost of re-instrumenting after each toggle in harnesses with many short sections.

   The named alternative is still first-LINE confirmation with local events only, at a per-line cost inside declared functions.
3. **The robust mutex and reconciliation.**
   - The exactness of `_alive`: a frame id reused by a new frame of the same code, and `f_locals` reads of another thread's frame on 3.12.
   - The cost of `sys._current_frames()` under contention, and slow owners against the 10 s bound.
   - Greenlets.
   - Pruning a holder whose entering token is live but slow.
   - `'exiting'` holders racing another thread's join, which must not yield a false CODE_SWAPPED.
4. **The coherence false-refusal surface.** Symlinked checkouts, zipimport, editable installs, `.pth` trees, pytest assertion rewriting, `importlib.reload` after the source moved, a relative `__file__` after `chdir`, frozen modules, Jupyter. Measure how many committed harnesses now refuse FOREIGN_DEFINITION.
5. **sys.monitoring interplay.** Tools calling `free_tool_id`, `restart_events`, `set_events` or `register_callback` on id 4 or 3. Id squatting before styxx's first enter. The MONITOR_LOST re-registration check itself. Per-patch-release instrumentation bugs (D1 hit a 3.12.3 settrace opcode bug).
6. **The crash-sweep scope.** The callbacks' own bytecode is not reachable by the INSTRUCTION injector while it runs as a callback. Revision 2 reaches it through the direct-call driver, which faults the same bytecode but not in its real calling context: a fault there propagates to the driver, not into a target frame. Attack the real context with lower-id tools and floods, and on two threads with hopped coroutine sections. Also check that no settrace/setprofile activity in the sweep process silences the injector (`rev2/p5b_settrace_blinds_injector.py`).
7. **CLONE_ALIVE's freeze clause.** A frozen clone with the count not above its mint value, after frozen objects died or after `gc.unfreeze()` then `gc.freeze()`. Over-blocks from executing T frames at exit.
8. **The SM2 noise mask and envelope.** A nondeterministic baseline field could mask a real regression. The mask must be minimal, and its size is reported.
9. **Over-blocking breadth.** Refusing 3.11 moves every lab harness. Count the committed preregs and harnesses affected, and check that every refusal message gives a working remedy.
10. **The loop boundary and R18.** Asyncio-compatible loops that do not call `_set_running_loop`. Greenlet-based loop bridges. Any way to re-enter a running loop's dispatch without passing a `Handle._run` or `BaseEventLoop._run_once` frame, other than R18's two shapes. Libraries that ship a loop class with its own `_run_once` and re-enter it. Shared-code poisoning of the cut by functions created after E2 (#21).

---

## Decisions this synthesis is least sure of

1. **Refusing CPython 3.11.** Judge 1 required it. Judge 2 left it to the panel, and this spec decides it on the py310-class argument. Refusing is the fail-closed choice, but it moves the lab's default interpreter, the P1 retro and the exam to 3.12/3.13. If the panel ranks L-SIGNAL-311 below py310, the alternative is D1's 3.11 setprofile adapter with SIGNAL_TIMER and f_lasti pairing for every target (judge 2's graft 1). That brings back a second adapter and a list of version-keyed cases.
2. **Confirmation grafted into D1's machine.** The pending entries hold their frames, and a global PY_UNWIND is set while any section is open (revision 2; revision 1: while any mint existed). Single-thread semantics were probed on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (synth/p_confirm_frame.py). No prototype of the combined machine has passed D1's crash sweep, G_SIG or the fuzzer. L-DELIVERY is also a new disclosed behaviour: while a section is open, an exception raised inside the unwind callback replaces the one being unwound. Round 5 may judge that program corruption. Revision 2 made it measurable and frozen: 0 lost handlers outside sections, reported inside, and not a G_SIG wrong result. The named alternative, first-LINE confirmation, costs a callback per line executed in declared functions. LINE events did not create the back-edge skip on 3.12.3 (`rev1/p13_backedge_line_branch.py`: 0/100), unlike JUMP, BRANCH and INSTRUCTION.
3. **One-call steps (revision 5).** Revision 5 removes revision 4's opener wait. It makes every read-decide-write on styxx's event, and every other write on its tool id, a single C call built from `itertools` and `operator`. The fifth critic's alternatives were to keep the wait with a fail-fast for nested opens, or to make losses detectable. Revision 5 prefers a mechanism in which no race outcome needs a wait. The one-call steps achieve that, and U1's exclusions shrink to outside parties. Two costs remain. One is readability: an implementer must build the pipelines exactly, and G_HYG pins their shape. The other is a premise about CPython that no specification of CPython states, though it is how 3.12 and 3.13 work: nothing runs inside a C call that calls no Python code. `rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py` tests the premise with every instrument this spec says coexists, with gc and with signals, on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12. The model-level cost is 1.6–3.2 µs more per section than revision 4 (`rev5/b5_cost.py`). Revision 4's wait cost 1.1–2.3 µs more per section in the model than revision 3 (`rev4/b4_cost.py`; the fifth critic's N5 noted that this item gave 1.3–1.7 µs). Revision 6: the premise was false for one step, `_register`, whose `register_callback` raises an audit event inside itself (B1). The premise now holds for four steps, is tested by the frozen gate G_ATOM on each patch level tracing accepts, and registration is bounded and reported instead of atomic. The premise is still the part of the design least under this spec's control.
4. **SM2's compromise.** SM2 compares only spec-fixed observables and does not gate UNDISTINGUISHED mutants. That makes EXAM_HOLE = 0 attainable by an exam frozen before the implementation. The cost is that a weakening whose only effect is on unfixed text, or that no frozen instrument observes, is reported rather than failed. The judges agreed on each half; they did not jointly test the combination.

---

## Revision 1: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_2026_09_25.md`. There is one row per item, in the critique's order; the last row is the CPython #130279 check requested alongside the critique.

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev1/`, abbreviated `rev1/`. They were run on CPython 3.12.3 and 3.13.12, and on 3.11.15 where marked. "Reading" means the resolution follows from the spec text and needed no execution.

No v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists yet. So no row claims a result *of the design as implemented*; exam cases and gates will measure those.

**Totals.** 42 rows:
- 40 resolved as the critique asked;
- 1 resolved with a rejected sub-claim: (b)4, the `loop_factory` part, which a probe shows is false;
- 1 partly rejected: (c)4, where the disclosure is fixed but the class claim is not accepted, with a note why.

| # | critique item | what changed (sections) | how it was verified |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a)1 | D15: `type(obj) in (staticmethod, classmethod)` runs a metaclass `__eq__`; so does `type(result) in _LAZY_TYPES` in `_run` | Resolution step 2 now uses `is` chains and forbids tuple-membership type tests. M7 `_run` uses an `is` chain. Also changed: delta table (Resolution), D15 and R1-D2 rows, a G_HYG lint clause, new case V40b, the mutation audit and SM1 | `rev1/p1_meta_eq.py` (3.11.15, 3.12.3, 3.13.12). **Found while resolving (not raised by the critique):**
- **F1. A settrace function silences other tools' INSTRUCTION events, and they stay off.** On 3.12.3 and 3.13.12, once a settrace function has run over a code object, tool 5's INSTRUCTION events on that code stop. They stay off after `sys.settrace(None)`, until `sys.monitoring.restart_events()` (`rev2/p5b_settrace_blinds_injector.py`). An injector sharing a process with ctrace or pdb could therefore run every trial without firing, and report clean. Fixed by the injector rules: no settrace or setprofile, `restart_events()` before each trial, a self-check trial, UNREACHED reporting, and G_COVER's settrace pass in its own process.
- **F2. Thread idents are reused.** A thread filter keyed on a finished thread's ident matched a later thread (the first version of `rev2/p6_faultpoint_identity.py`). The injector rule requires an ident read while the case thread is alive.
- **F3. Zip import differs by version** (N2 row). This affects FOREIGN_DEFINITION's outcome for the same archive, not any exam case.

**Kept from revision 1.** No machinery state depends on a `finally` or with-exit across a loop back-edge (CPython #130279). The new functions `_reclaim`, `_cut_refresh`, `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off` and `_ours` contain no `try`, and neither does the reordered `_forget_in_child`. `_detach`, still called from `_run`/`_run_async`'s single `finally`, gains no loop. `_cut_refresh`'s loop over two bindings and `_reclaim`'s callback registrations sit in no `try` body. G_HYG's lint is unchanged. The one new handler-free failure mode, a ValueError from another tool's id calls racing styxx's, is a fault within the model (the Tool acquisition prefix), not a caught exception.
