## Revision 3: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_rev2_2026_09_25.md` (verdict NOT_READY: 0 BLOCKER-for-freeze, 8 MUST-FIX, 12 NOTE). There is one row per item, in the critique's order, then the CPython #130279 check, then the findings made while resolving them.

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev3/`, abbreviated `rev3/`, with outputs in `rev3/out_*.txt`. Each was run on CPython 3.12.3 (`rt3/venv3.12`) and 3.13.12 (`rt3/venv3.13`) with the same outcome unless the row says otherwise. Most run against `rev3/mech3.py`, which is `rev2/mech2.py` copied, with revision 3's changes behind a switch (`REV3`) and each new rule behind a single-rule mutant switch (`MUT`). `critic3/` is the third critic's probe directory. "Reading" means the resolution follows from the spec text and needed no execution. As before, no v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists, so every result is of the model or of CPython itself.

**Totals.** 21 rows (8 MUST-FIX, 12 NOTE, #130279):
- 18 resolved as the critique asked;
- MF2 resolved with a changed fix. The critic's text fixes are all taken. Its "better" option, reordering `_open` to commit first, is replaced by moving the whole commit into `_run`'s `try`. That removes the fault windows without trading away X143's confirmation;
- MF7 resolved both ways the critic offered. The unraisable-hook alternative is restored, and MONITOR_LOST is added to I6, but only for an instrument that splits `_unwind_off`'s test-and-clear. Without one, the model shows it cannot arise;
- N1 is disclosed, with its remedy, and the clause is kept rather than narrowed.

| # | critique item | what changed (sections) | how it was verified |
|---|---|---|---|
| MF1 | `_retire` step 6 clears PY_UNWIND with no re-check, racing openers that do not take the mutex, so "in every interleaving" and I3 are false | **Fixed, and the invariant is restated with its windows.** `_retire` step 6 is now `_unwind_off()`. `_unwind_off` makes every call (`_ours()`, `get_events`) first. It then tests `_ANCHORS` and clears in one expression on one line, `if _ANCHORS or set_events(_TOOL[0], 0): return`. CPython 3.12/3.13 have no eval-breaker check between a load or test and the call after it, so no thread can open a section between the test and the clear. That closes the critic's race and also revision 2's own toggle window (a closer's clear landing after an opener's commit). "The unwind scope" now states U1–U4 in place of "in every interleaving". U1: a section's body never runs with the event clear. U2: a clear event with an anchor lasts only on the opener's own thread before its body. U3: a set event with no anchor lasts only inside one thread's own window. U4: the bounds after a fault. The one exception is an instruction-level instrument (the id-5 injector, opcode tracing) that runs Python code inside the expression. I3 is restated. Changed: M3 step 6, M7 (`_unwind_off`, "The unwind scope"), I3, G_HYG (the one-line form and the only `set_events` callers), the mutation audit, SM1. New case X145 (a) closer and (b) retire, witnessing `_unwind_off`'s order and step 6 | `rev3/q1_test_then_call_atomic.py`: the test-then-call, return-then-call and one-line `or` shapes saw a toggled dict non-empty at the call 0 times in 1.17–1.58M empty tests per shape; with one C call in between, 195–268 times. `rev3/c5r3_retire_step6_race.py` (the critic's c5 at every switch point of step 6): revision 2 `{}` with MONITOR_LOST; revision 3 `{'B': {'t': 1}}` at each point, with the event set while B is open. `rev3/m1_unwind_modelcheck.py`: all interleavings of two sections and a retire, with 0–2 faults. Revision 3 has 0 hits with the event clear in 1,223 / 4,300 / 7,216 states; revision 2 has 46 fault-free. `rev3/s1_unwind_stress.py` (real threads, a forced GIL hand-off at every protocol statement boundary with probability 0.3, a 1 µs switch interval, 18 runs per revision): revision 3 had 0 of 736,048 in-body samples with the event clear, counted all 368,024 raising calls, and noted no MONITOR_LOST. Revision 2 had 1,793 of 286,442 fault-free samples clear, lost 997 of 143,221 calls, and noted MONITOR_LOST in 6 of 6 fault-free runs. `rev3/w1_rev3_witnesses.py`: X145 (a) and (b) give `{t:1}` under the spec and `{}` with MONITOR_LOST under revision 2's order and step 6 |
| MF2 | A fault in `_open` between the first `_unwind_on()` and the anchor commit leaves PY_UNWIND on outside every section, and reconciliation does not repair it | **Fixed; the critic's text fixes are all taken; its "better" fix is replaced by a stronger one.** The anchor commit, both `_unwind_on()` calls and the race re-check move out of `_open` into `_commit(o)`, the first statement of `_run`'s and `_run_async`'s `try` body. Any fault in them is followed by the `finally`'s `_detach`, which pops the anchor if it was committed and runs `_unwind_off()`. So no fault in the open path leaves the event set or leaves a dead anchor. Revision 2's fault after the commit left a dead anchor that kept the event on until exit, which the critic did not list (F5 below). Every reconciliation now ends with `_unwind_off()`. U4 states the true bounds. After a fault in a close after its pop: until the next close that leaves no anchor, or the next transaction. After a fault at a close's first instructions, or a `_run_async` `finally` skipped by #130279: a dead anchor keeps the event until that trace's exit or pruning. H10 and G_SIG cell (a) now fix the cell's shape: no section is opened while the flood is armed, and `global_events == 0` is asserted before arming. Weighing the critic's "commit first, then set": it would still leave a dead anchor after a fault between the commit and the `try`, and it would drop the pre-commit set that X143 witnesses. Moving the commit into the `try` costs one Python call per section (at most 1.3–2.4 µs per section in the model, `rev3/b1_commit_cost.py`). If the 3.12 throw-path back-edge skips its `finally`, the only machinery state left is a dead anchor. No count, note, refusal or termination depends on it: its frame is on no live chain, and exit's X3 or reconciliation pops it. Its one effect is that PY_UNWIND stays set until then. **Found while resolving (not raised by the critique):**
- **F4. Revision 2 made false MONITOR_LOST notes after a single fault.** `_detach`'s check fired for any opening whose `o.frame` was set. That included an opening inert because of a fault right after the append, re-detached by exit's X3 with no section open. `rev3/c7r3_stale_unwind_after_open_fault.py`: revision 2 gives MONITOR_LOST after a fault at either of the first two open points. That is outside revision 2's I6, whose notes allowed only OPEN_AT_EXIT. Fixed by the `o.armed` and anchor-registered conditions (MF7).
- **F5. Revision 2's open path could leave a dead anchor.** A fault after the anchor commit and before the `try` (`open:committed`, `open:on2` in c7r3) left `anchors=1` with the event set. No close cleared it, only exit's X3, and it cost the same lost handlers as the critic's c7 case. Fixed by `_commit` inside the `try` (MF2).
- **F6. Exit's X3 could set a false `UNWIND_LOST`** on an opening whose close had been cut short after its pop: the event is legitimately off by then. Found by the stress, `rev3/s1_unwind_stress.py` in its first form. Fixed by the anchor-registered condition; witness X143c.
- **F7. A fault inside `_commit` after a concurrent clear set a false `UNWIND_LOST`** even with the anchor condition: the anchor is registered and the event clear, but the body never ran. Found by the model check. Fixed by `o.armed`; witness X143b.
- **F8. The fault injector itself can cause a lost confirmation.** While the id-5 injector is armed on `_unwind_off`, its callback runs Python code between the test and the clear, so another thread can commit a section there. That is the MONITOR_LOST exception in I6, C4 and C8. `rev3/m1_unwind_modelcheck.py`'s split variant reproduces it: 54 fault-free hits with the event clear, all inside the clearer's re-check window.

## Revision 4: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_rev3_2026_09_25.md` (verdict NOT_READY: 1 BLOCKER-for-freeze, 6 MUST-FIX, 7 NOTE). There is one row per item, in the critique's order, then the CPython #130279 check. The enumeration of concurrent transitions that the brief asked for follows the table, then the findings made while resolving the items.

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev4/`, abbreviated `rev4/`, with outputs in `rev4/out_*.txt`. Each was run on CPython 3.12.3 (`rt3/venv3.12`) and 3.13.12 (`rt3/venv3.13`) with the same outcome unless the row says otherwise. The model check `m1_pairs_modelcheck.py` is interpreter-independent and was run once. Most probes run against `rev4/mech4.py`, which is `rev3/mech3.py` copied, with revision 4's changes behind a switch (`REV4`) and each new rule behind a single-rule mutant (`MUT`). With `REV4` off it reproduces revision 3: revision 3's witness probe gives identical output on it (`rev4/out_w1_rev3_on_mech4.txt`). `critic4/` is the fourth critic's probe directory. "Reading" means the resolution follows from the spec text and needed no execution. As before, no v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists, so every result is of the model or of CPython itself.

**Totals.** 15 rows (B1, MF1–MF6, N1–N7, #130279):
- 12 resolved as the critique asked;
- B1 resolved with the critic's first fix, adapted. The anchor is popped by a key that no detacher nulls, as asked. But the key stays `o.frame`, which only the owning thread releases, instead of a second, never-nulled field. A never-nulled frame reference would keep every section's `_run` frame, with its arguments and result, alive for the trace's whole life. The re-check after the store and an anchor sweep in reconciliation are added;
- MF1 resolved by the robust option the critic preferred: nothing depends on the test and the clear being atomic any more. The exact instrument list is still stated, as information;
- MF6 resolved by the second of the critic's two options (exclude the background mint's `pending` from C5). The first, comparing with the after-state B, is not taken, because the background variant's B is itself timing-dependent.

Nothing in the critique is rejected.

| # | critique item | what changed (sections) | how it was verified |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | An open racing its own tracer's exit registers `_ANCHORS[None]` for the rest of the process: X3's `_detach` nulled `o.frame`, then `_commit` stored under it, and no later `_detach` could pop it | **Fixed, three parts.** (1) `_detach` never assigns `o.frame`; only the owner's `finally` releases it, after its own `_detach` (At open step 9; M7 `_run`). So every detacher (X3, a prune, step 8, the owner) pops by the anchor's own key, and `_commit` never stores under None. (2) `_commit` stores, then re-tests `'fin' in o.fin` (a detacher claimed o) or `'exiting'`, and if so detaches itself and refuses TRACE_INACTIVE (step 8). Every detacher claims before it pops, so either its pop follows the store or the claim precedes the re-test. The claim test covers a prune of a dead-facade core, where `'exiting'` is absent. The `'exiting'` test covers an opening appended after X3's snapshot. (3) Reconciliation sweeps registered anchors whose core is dead, with the credit stop first (M3). That bounds the two-fault case, where a fault after the store is followed by a fault before the `finally`'s pop, after the trace exited. Model: exit is now enumerated against its own tracer's open and close, and against a second exit and a pruning reconciliation (below). New witnesses X146 (PY_START of `_commit` held until exit returned), X146b (held after step 2, before the append), X146c (prune of a dead facade) and X146d (two faults, then the sweep), and X147, the #20 outcome set with natural timing. Its one `_unwind_on()` replaces revision 3's two. The append of a racing open can land after X7, so `record()` has it once the racing thread has finished. #20 says so, and X147 freezes the outcome set with both outcomes allowed and the join before `record()`. Their `finally` gains one statement, `o.frame = None`, with no loop. The new loop, in `_await_clearers`, is in its own frame, which has no `try`. Control, the same loop written inside the `try` body: the `finally` was skipped on both (on 3.12.3 because the injector's INSTRUCTION events create the skip, p12) |

### The concurrent-transition enumeration (the brief's systematic audit)

The last three critiques each found a race that the evidence did not exercise: `_retire` step 6 against an open (third critic), and an open or a close against its own tracer's exit (fourth critic). `rev4/m1_pairs_modelcheck.py` therefore enumerates every pair of concurrent transitions. The transitions are open, close, exit of the same tracer, exit of another tracer (retire and reconciliation), a second exit, and a reconciliation that prunes. Four triples are added where a pair alone cannot reach the interesting state. Each transition is modelled step by step from the spec's pseudocode, with a step boundary between every two C operations, which is finer than any eval-breaker placement. So the enumeration also covers any *interleaving* that such code causes on another thread. Revision 5 (N8): it does not cover what that code *does*: open a section, block, switch stacks, or clear the event. Revision 5's `m5_modelcheck.py` adds those (Revision 5 table). Each configuration is explored exhaustively with 0, 1 and 2 injected faults (triples: revision 3 fault-free only, revision 4 with 0 and 1). The properties are U1, A0, HANG, END (the fault-free end state: no anchor, no event, no announcement, the mutex free, every opening finalised, no `UNWIND_LOST`), REPAIR (faulted: after the owning trace's exit and one reconciliation, no anchor, no event, no announcement, no `UNWIND_LOST`) and P8 (step 8: no body runs whose opening was claimed, or whose exit had begun, when it stored its anchor). Visited states are stored as 64-bit hashes, so a state count is a count of distinct hashes; a collision could hide a state, with probability about n²/2⁶⁵, negligible at these sizes (revision 5, N8).

Each cell gives the violations found with no fault (`ff`), or if there are none, the first faulted budget that has any (`f`), then the states explored per fault budget (0/1/2 for pairs; 0, or 0/1, for triples). "ok" means no violation of any property at any budget. END_A and END_S count fault-free end states with an anchor or the event left; FLAG counts end states with a false `UNWIND_LOST`.

**What the enumeration shows.**
- **Revision 4: 0 violations of any property**, in all 15 configurations and all 41 runs (49.5 million state hashes), with up to two faults on pairs and one on triples. No hit ran with the event clear under a crediting core. No state had an armed, crediting, registered anchor with the event clear. Nothing hung. No fault-free end state left an anchor, the event, an announcement or the mutex held, or an unfinalised opening, and none set `UNWIND_LOST`. Every faulted end state was clean after the owning trace's exit and one reconciliation. No body ran after its opening was claimed, or its exit began, before its store.
- **Revision 3, as specified: 6 of the 15 configurations fail fault-free.** The B1 leak (END_A) and the MF2 false flag appear in open ∥ exit and close ∥ exit (the flag only), in the prune configuration, where the open's body also runs after a prune finalised its opening (P8, F10), and in all three triples that race exit against its own tracer's open. Revision 3's m1 did not contain any of these configurations. With its test and clear split, as an instrument splits them, U1 also fails in every configuration with two sections, or with a section and another tracer's exit or reconciliation (MF1).
- **Every pair the brief names is covered:** open/exit, close/exit, open/close across threads, exit/exit (same tracer and two tracers), and reconcile against open, close, exit and reconcile. Two things are not enumerated. Re-entrant transitions on one thread (a signal handler or finalizer running a transition inside another) are not covered by anything here. REENTRANT and H1 concern transactions only, and re-entrant *opens and closes* inside windows were covered by nothing (revision 5, N8). Tool-id races with other tools (L-MONITOR) are not enumerated either. Revision 5's `m5_modelcheck.py` enumerates both.

**Mutants (`m1_pairs_modelcheck.py mutants`, over the pairs and the three smaller triples).** Each of revision 4's 13 model-level single-rule mutants violates a property somewhere, so each rule is needed in the model:

| mutant | first violations found |
|---|---|
| no announcement; withdrawal before the test; no wait; `_unwind_on` before the wait | U1 and A0 fault-free in open ∥ open, open ∥ open(Y), and the two-sections-and-retire triple |
| `_detach` releases `o.frame` (revision 3) | END_A fault-free in open ∥ exit, the prune configuration, open ∥ exit ∥ exit |
| no step 8 re-check | P8 in open ∥ exit, the prune configuration, open ∥ exit ∥ exit |
| step 8 without the claim test | P8 in the prune configuration |
| step 8 without the `'exiting'` test | P8 in open ∥ exit, open ∥ exit ∥ exit, open ∥ exit ∥ reconcile |
| `_detach` tests the anchor before the event | a false flag fault-free in open ∥ exit, close ∥ exit, the prune configuration |
| no anchor sweep | REPAIR (anchor left) with 2 faults in open ∥ exit |
| anchor sweep without the credit stop | U1 with 1 fault in open ∥ exit ∥ reconcile (F9) |
| no announcement sweep | REPAIR (announcement left) with 1 fault in open ∥ exit, close ∥ exit, open ∥ open |
| no liveness test in the wait | HANG with 1 fault in open ∥ open, open ∥ open(Y), the two-sections-and-retire triple (the implementation's 10 s bound turns it into MACHINERY_BUSY) |

### Found while resolving (not raised by the critique)
- **F9. The anchor sweep needs a credit stop first.** The model check found it. A core can die between the reconciliation's prune loop and the sweep, when its exit is interrupted between X1 and X2. A sweep that only detached then popped a still-crediting section's anchor and cleared the event, so that section's next raising call was lost: U1 violated in 31 faulted paths of open ∥ exit ∥ reconcile. The sweep now calls `_prune(o.core)`, which stops credit first (M3); the mutant `sweep_no_credit_stop` is in the mutant table.
- **F10. B1 has a second trigger: a prune.** In revision 3 an open racing a reconciliation that prunes its dead-facade core (a `run_async` coroutine whose facade was dropped) left `_ANCHORS[None]` too, and ran its body although its opening had been finalised (the model's prune configuration: END 3, P8 113, fault-free). Revision 4's claim test in step 8 covers it (X146c).
- **F11. MF2 is not rare.** The critic reached the false MONITOR_LOST with a hook. `rev4/b1_open_races_exit.py` reaches it naturally in 17–23 of 20,000 open-versus-exit races under revision 3, and 38–40 under revision 2.
- **F12. On 3.12.3, `f_trace_opcodes` set at a frame's `'call'` event takes effect only from the code object's next execution.** Revision 4's opcode-tracing probe primes the code first (`rev4/m2_split_instruments.py`). An exam tool that uses opcode tracing must do the same. Revision 3's first-run probe saw no opcode events on 3.12.3 at all.

**Kept from revision 3.** The CPython #130279 rule, as the H row states. `_commit` has no loop and no `try`. `_await_clearers` has a loop and no `try`, and nothing depends on a `finally` inside it. `_unwind_off` has neither. The reconciliation's sweeps run under `_locked`, which has no `try`.

## Revision 5: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_rev4_2026_09_25.md` (verdict NOT_READY: 0 BLOCKER-for-freeze, 7 MUST-FIX, 8 NOTE). It first says why the mechanism changed rather than being patched. Then comes one row per item in the critique's order, then the CPython #130279 check, the extended model check the brief asked for, and the findings made while resolving the items.

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev5/`, abbreviated `rev5/`, with outputs in `rev5/out_*.txt`. Each was run on CPython 3.12.3 (`rt3/venv3.12`) and 3.13.12 (`rt3/venv3.13`) with the same outcome unless the row says otherwise. The model check `m5_modelcheck.py` is interpreter-independent and was run once. `rev5/mech5.py` is written from this revision's text, not patched from `mech4.py`, and has each revision-5 rule behind a single-rule mutant (`MUT`). `rev5/spec_rev5_eventpath.py` is M7's pseudocode itself, run as Python. `critic5/` is the fifth critic's probe directory. "Reading" means the resolution follows from the spec text and needed no execution. As before, no v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists, so every result is of a model or of CPython itself.

### Why the mechanism changed, and what was rejected

The properties the event path must have are three. **U1:** no call is lost silently while a section is open; a loss is noted, or impossible. **U3:** the event is never set with no anchor, beyond a stated bounded window. **I5:** nothing hangs or deadlocks, including when a finalizer or signal handler opens a section, or when user code blocks inside any window.

Each of revisions 2 to 4 met the critique of its day by adding mechanism, and the new mechanism brought failures the evidence did not model:
- revision 3 made the test and the clear adjacent bytecodes, which instruments split (the fourth critic's MF1);
- revision 4 added a clearer's announcement and an opener's wait. The wait closed cycles through sections opened inside a clearer's window and through user locks (MF1). Its same-thread exclusion lost calls, silently once another section opened (MF2, R19). Its liveness test, clock and thread identity became exam hazards (MF3, MF5, N2, N3).

The root cause was the same each time: a decision ("no anchor is registered") and the action it licenses ("clear the event") were two steps, and Python code could run between them. Every fix either narrowed that gap or made the other party wait until it closed. Revision 5 removes the gap. A read-decide-write on the event is built as a lazy `itertools`/`operator` pipeline, whose construction reads nothing, and consumed by one builtin call. The test and the write then happen inside one C call, which no Python code enters, so nothing needs to wait and nothing needs to be announced. `rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py` tests that premise on CPython with every instrument this spec says coexists, with the cyclic gc and with signals. The same principle then fixes the tool-id races (N1): every write on styxx's id is one call gated on the id's name. *Revision 6 corrections (the sixth critic's B1 and N4).* The registration is not one call in that sense: `register_callback` raises an audit event inside itself, before its exchange (M7, "The registration"). And two read-decide-writes on the event state are deliberately not one call: `_detach`'s close-time read (the event, then the anchor, over three statements, whose precision FALSEFLAG checks in the model), and `_reclaim`'s `_LOST` count, which follows the registration in a separate statement under the mutex. Both are sound as argued where they are specified.

Considered and rejected:
- **Keep the wait, and let an opener nested in a clearer's window fail fast** (MF1's optional fix). This removes one cycle but not the cycle through user locks (`critic5/c3`), which only the 10 s bound breaks. It adds a rule, a note and a case, and keeps every revision-4 hazard (liveness, clock, ident).
- **Prevent nothing and detect everything.** Keep revision 3's adjacent test and clear, and note MONITOR_LOST whenever an opener finds the event clear with another armed section registered (MF2's suggestion, used alone). Under a profiler or a CALL tool, a natural concurrent open would then really lose calls, and the note would appear in fault-free races. X147's, C4's and C8's "no MONITOR_LOST" would become timing-dependent. Losses should be impossible where they can be, and noted only where an outside party causes them.
- **Leave the event conservatively on and clear it only with proof of no anchor and no concurrent opener.** In Python a proof followed by a clear always has a gap between them. The only way to close it without waiting is to make the proof and the clear one C call. That is the chosen design, so this option reduces to it.
- **MF2's suggestion as the critic worded it** ("`_unwind_on` flags a loss if it finds the event clear while another armed anchor is registered"). Taken, but as part of `_unwind_on`'s one call. Read at separate times, it flags an opening armed after the read by a concurrent open that itself set the event: a false note in a fault-free race. X153 witnesses the difference: the split capture notes a section that lost nothing in 20 of 83 trials.
- **No global PY_UNWIND at all (first-LINE confirmation).** It is still the named alternative. It costs a callback per line in declared functions, and it is not needed once the toggle is atomic.

What revision 5 removes:
- `_CLEARING`, `_await_clearers`, the announcement sweep, `_retire` step 6 and `_prune`'s credit stop, which has no observable effect (M3);
- MACHINERY_BUSY at open, R19, the greenlet-clearer residual;
- cases X149, X149b, X150 and V66, and the `"clearing"` field.

What it adds:
- the one-call vocabulary and five one-call functions (`_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take`, `_register`, `_set_local`; revision 6: four, since `_register` runs audit hooks inside it), with `_unwind_on`'s own-anchor gate and its capture of blinded openings;
- `_ensure_tool`'s reclaim of its own id, and `_named` by identity;
- the mutex's clock bound at import;
- the instruction-sweep witness family (Exam harness rules) and cases X137d, X137e, X138b, X146e and X152–X155, with X143, X145 and X148 recast as sweeps.

**Totals.** 16 rows (MF1–MF7, N1–N8, #130279):
- MF1 and MF2 are resolved by removing their mechanism, not by the critique's text fixes. The text fixes are also applied: I5 is restated, #20 lists the remaining cycle shape, and the disclosures are exact. MF2's mechanism suggestion is taken, in a precise form;
- MF3 is resolved by retiring X149 with its rule, plus a harness rule for every case that names a thread by ident;
- MF4–MF7 and N1–N8 are resolved as the critique asked, N1 by mechanism rather than by disclosure alone.

Nothing in the critique is rejected.

The robust mutex is the only wait. I5 is restated. ### The extended model check (the brief's item 2)

`rev5/m5_modelcheck.py` keeps `rev4/m1_pairs_modelcheck.py`'s granularity: every C operation is one step. It adds what the fifth critic found missing, for revision 4 and revision 5 in one model:
- **Same-thread re-entry.** At every step boundary of every thread, windows and blocked waits included, a nested program may run to completion on that thread before the interrupted one continues. The nested program is a whole section (a finalizer or signal handler calling `cov.run`), a `run_async` section left suspended when the nested code returns (R19's shape), a user-lock acquisition, or a whole transaction.
- **Blocking user code.** A thread may hold user lock L around its section or its exit, and nested code may block on L. The busy bounds are a timeout step that a blocked machinery wait may take at any time. User-lock waits have none.
- **Outside parties.** Between any two steps: clear or set styxx's event, free its id, or take a freed id as another tool.
- **Greenlets.** A thread may hold two greenlets and switch between them at any step boundary. A switched-out greenlet's frames are not on its thread's frame chain, so the liveness test sees them as dead.
- **`run_async`.** An async section may suspend in its body with its anchor registered, and any idle thread may resume it: the same thread, or another (a hop).
- **E4, `_retire`, `_reclaim`.** Enter's transaction (reconcile, `_ensure_tool`, mint join or holder, register, local events, install, credit, activate), `_retire`'s steps, and reconciliation's reclaim.

**Properties.** "Without an outside party" means no outside event has happened in the run.
- **U1:** no hit runs with the event clear while its anchor is registered and its core is crediting and alive, without an outside party. With one, such a hit is a LOST hit.
- **A0:** no state has a live, armed, registered anchor of a crediting, live core with the event clear, without an outside party.
- **STALE:** no state has the event set with no anchor registered, unless an outside party set the event or freed or took the id.
- **FALSEFLAG:** a flag is set only on a core for which an armed, registered anchor was seen with the event clear.
- **LOSTNOTE:** at every end, after repair, every LOST hit of a readable trace is noted (MONITOR_LOST or MACHINERY_BUSY) unless an outside set ended its blind interval first. Such a hit is MASKED, the disclosed residual.
- **CLOBBER:** no styxx write lands on an id another tool holds.
- **VALUEERROR:** styxx never raises ValueError from a race with another tool.
- **P8:** step 8, as in revision 4.
- **HANG:** no reachable state where live work exists and no step at all, timeouts included, is enabled. It is not zero by construction: with a bound removed, it finds revision 4's cycles.
- **CYC_OPEN** and **CYC_TX:** states in which only a timeout of an opener's wait, or of the mutex, lets anything progress.
- **END and REPAIR,** as in revision 4, extended to mints (none registered or installed after repair).

Budgets are F faults, I nested programs, X outside events and W greenlet switches per run. Each cell gives the violations at the largest budget run, or "ok", then the informational counts in brackets. LOST and MASKED are counts of end states in which some core lost a hit, or lost one that an outside set masked. CYC_TX and BUSY_TX count the mutex's bounded waits. BUSY_OPEN counts revision 4's open waits that time out. STALE_rev4 counts revision 4's documented windows (its U3). The state counts per budget are distinct 64-bit hashes.

**What the enumeration shows.**
- **Revision 5: 0 violations of any property** in 33 configurations (revision 5 wrote 35; revision 6, M2 v) and 70 runs (4,922,275 state hashes). That includes the two same-tracer triples of revision 4's check, `open(X) ∥ open(X) ∥ exit(X)` (537,217 / 3,081,695 states at 0 / 1 faults) and `open(X) ∥ exit(X) ∥ exit(X)` (18,848 / 73,216). Its only cycle is CYC_TX, the mutex's through a user lock, broken by the bound. Every LOST hit, all caused by an outside party, is noted or MASKED.
- **Revision 4 in the same model** fails in every class the fifth critic reported:
  - U1 in both R19 configurations;
  - a silent loss (LOSTNOTE) when another opener re-sets the event;
  - CYC_OPEN in c3, c8 and the lock-around-open shape;
  - CLOBBER and VALUEERROR with outside parties;
  - U1 and silent losses with a greenlet switch in a close;
  - one gap the critic did not report, a silent loss when the id is freed between E4's reconciliation and `_ensure_tool` (F13).

  So the new dimensions are not vacuous.

Outputs: `rev5/out_m5_main.txt` (`m5_modelcheck.py main`), `rev5/out_m5_triples.txt` (`m5_modelcheck.py only 5 …`, the two added triples), `rev5/out_m5_table.md`. The model is deterministic: a re-run reproduces every state count, on either interpreter.

| a close with a section opened inside ∥ open | ok | ok | ok |

The last-but-two row is the one wait left in revision 5: the robust mutex, whose 10 s bound turns that deadlock into MACHINERY_BUSY (I5, #20).

**Mutants** (`m5_modelcheck.py mutants`, revision 5; `rev5/out_m5_mutants.txt`). The first violations found. *(Revision 6, N5: the committed `mutants` branch skipped the `open(X) ∥ open(X) ∥ exit…` triples, so this table is over the pairs, one triple and the extended configurations; and the committed output was not produced by the committed script: the sixth critic's re-run finds `no_exiting_test` first at `open(X) ∥ exit(X) ∥ exit(X)`, not `open(X)+[exit(X)]`, with the same verdicts. Revision 6's `rev6/out_m6_mutants.txt` is produced by `m6_modelcheck.py mutants` as committed, over every configuration, the triples included.)*

**What is still not enumerated.** Two or more nested programs on one thread at once, and budgets above those in the table. Free-threaded interleavings, which the spec refuses. A real greenlet or gevent library: the model's greenlets are abstract. Pending entries and credit counts: hits carry the attribution outcome, not counts, so over-credit is covered by I1's argument and P8 rather than by a count property. The `record()` and scoring layers.

### Found while resolving (not raised by the critique)
- **F13. `_ensure_tool` re-took its own freed id silently.** The extended model check found it (`ext: enter(X) ∥ open(Y)`, revision 4 and the first draft of revision 5: LOSTNOTE 2). The id is freed after E4's reconciliation and before `_ensure_tool`. A section opening meanwhile gets no event, because the name gate is false, and loses its raising calls. `_ensure_tool` then re-takes the id without counting it in `_LOST`. With the close's event read also name-gated, as revision 4 read it, nothing noted the loss. Fixed: `_ensure_tool` re-takes its own id through `_reclaim`, which counts it (X137e), and the close's event read has no name gate (F14). *(Revision 6, M3: in this shape either fix alone closes the silent loss, and the count's only effect on its own is the note X137e witnesses. The ungated read is also needed alone, when a greenlet switch delays a reclaim's count past another greenlet's exit (X137f). The model shows both: `ensure_nocount` alone is undetected; `blind_read_gated` alone gives LOSTNOTE only in `ext+greenlet: [exit(X), rec] ∥ open(X)`; the two together also in `ext: enter(X) ∥ open(Y)`.)*
- **F14. `get_events` never raises on a freed id,** and `register_callback` works on one; only `set_events` and `set_local_events` raise (`rev5/p_freed_id_calls.py`, both versions). The close's read therefore needs no name test. Revision 4's Tool acquisition prefix named `get_events` among the calls a free could make raise; that was wrong.
- **F15. Revision 4's A0 with a fault under re-entry is not a loss.** A section opened inside a clearer's window whose own close is faulted before its pop leaves a dead, armed anchor. The clearer then clears under it (`close(X)+[open(Y)] ∥ open(Y)`, one fault, 556 states under a first draft of the property). Its frame is dead, so nothing can be lost. The model's A0 now counts only anchors whose owner can still run its body.
- **F16. `_prune`'s credit stop had no observable effect** (M3), so it could have no witness. It is deleted rather than kept unwitnessed. Revision 4's F9 (the sweep needs it) followed from a U1 stated for dead cores whose records nobody can read.
- **F17. N6 is not only a reading hazard.** A shared `succ` dict makes the mutex's second acquisition walk a cycle, so every trace's exit would hang (`rev5/p_txn_shared_succ.py`).
- **F18. `free_tool_id` leaves callbacks registered, and they keep firing.** In revision 5's own atomicity probe, stale trial callbacks on id 5 fired in later trials and could have hidden violations. The exam's id-3 and id-5 tools free their ids after each case, so the harness now clears their events and callbacks first (Exam harness rules).
- **F19. Revision 4 set the event for a whole uncredited body.** When exit's X3 popped an opener's anchor after its step 8, revision 4's `_unwind_on` still set the event, with no anchor, until that body's close (its U3 window). Revision 5's own-anchor gate removes it (X146e).

**Weakest points of revision 5, for round 6.**
1. **The one-call premise.** Nothing runs inside a C call that makes no Python-level call. That is how CPython 3.12 and 3.13 work, and `rev5/t1_onecall_atomic.py` tests it for every coexisting instrument, gc and signals. It is not a documented guarantee, and a patch release could add an audit event or a callback inside a `sys.monitoring` function. The prereg must re-run the atomicity probe on every patch level it lists. *(Revision 6: `register_callback` already has one on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 (B1); the re-run is now the gate G_ATOM, and tracing refuses unlisted patch levels, M5.)*
2. **Readability of the pipelines.** An implementer can build a pipeline that quietly calls Python code, for example an `attrgetter` on a class with a Python `__getattribute__`, or a lambda. G_HYG pins the shape, and the sweeps catch any split, but a pipeline whose inner call runs Python code without splitting the test from the write would pass both. `_Opening` and `_Core` must stay plain slotted classes (M1).
3. **The outside-party residual.** A party that clears styxx's event and sets it again itself, or that restores changed callbacks or local events before exit, loses calls silently. A hostile tool that re-takes styxx's id under styxx's own name object is taken for styxx.
4. **Cost.** 1.6–3.2 µs more per section than revision 4 in the model (`rev5/b5_cost.py`), because every open and close builds about ten iterator objects even when another section keeps the event set.
5. **The model's abstractions.** Greenlets and `run_async` are abstract. At most one nested program and two outside events run per configuration. Counts are not modelled.
6. **The remaining cycle.** The mutex cycle through user code is bounded, not removed. A harness whose audit hook or finalizer takes a lock held around a tracer's exit stalls for 10 s and gets MACHINERY_BUSY.

---

## Revision 6: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_rev5_2026_09_25.md` (the sixth critic; verdict NOT_READY: 1 BLOCKER-for-freeze, 5 MUST-FIX, 7 NOTE). The critic confirmed revision 5's one-call premise for `_unwind_on` and `_unwind_off`, the two steps U1 and U3 rest on, and found it false for `_register` (B1) and unchecked for the bound names (M1). This section gives what changed and what was rejected, one row per item in the critique's order, the CPython #130279 check, the revision-6 model check, the findings made while resolving, and the weakest points left.

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev6/`, abbreviated `rev6/`, with outputs in `rev6/out_*.txt`. Each was run on CPython 3.12.3 (`rt3/venv3.12`) and 3.13.12 (`rt3/venv3.13`) with the same outcome unless the row says otherwise. The model check `m6_modelcheck.py` is interpreter-independent and was run once, on 3.12.3. `rev6/mech6.py` is `rev5/mech5.py` copied with revision 6's changes, each new rule behind a single-rule mutant. Revision 5's witnesses, re-run against `mech6.py` in place of `mech5.py`, give the same verdict for every row on both versions; only the `_register` sweep's trial counts change, because its pipeline did (`rev6/run_w5_on_mech6.py`, `rev6/out_w5_on_mech6_3.12.txt` and `_3.13.txt`). `critic6/` is the sixth critic's probe directory. "Reading" means the resolution follows from the spec text and needed no execution. As before, no v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists, so every result is of a model or of CPython itself.

### What changed, and what was rejected

**B1: option (a), plus a precise disclosure of the residual.** Registration cannot be made atomic from Python. The audit event is raised inside `register_callback`, after its argument checks and before its exchange, and no API removes an audit hook or avoids the event. What revision 6 can do, and does:
- **Bound what an audit hook can cause.** Each exchange is gated on the name immediately before it, inside the same pipeline, so the gate and the exchange are separated only by that exchange's own hook. After the last exchange the owner is read again, still inside the consuming call. Nothing runs between an exchange and the next gate or the owner read, so exchanges 1 to n−1 landed on styxx's id, and only exchange n can have landed on another tool's, whose name the owner read gives. Revision 5's form could replace three of a foreign tool's callbacks in the critic's shape and report nothing; revision 6's replaces at most one and always reports it.
- **Act on the report.** A registration is *complete* only with five exchanges and styxx's name after the last. E4 uses an id only after a complete registration (else it moves to the next id or refuses MONITOR_BUSY); the reclaim counts only a complete one; X5 notes MONITOR_LOST for anything else. Every trace live at a split notes MONITOR_LOST too, because no exit's registration on a taken id can be complete until a reclaim or a rebinding has counted in `_LOST`.
- **Disclose exactly what remains** (L-MONITOR, M7 "The registration", #7, #20): one callback slot of the other tool is left as styxx's, and styxx does not restore it; a raising hook leaves a partial registration and propagates, out of `__exit__` at X5 (M5's heading, X157); the five audit events at every exit are a second site for the mutex's user-code cycle (#20, I5, "Audit hooks and slow owners").

Considered and rejected for B1:
- **Register once, at import or at the first `coverage_trace()`, and never again** (the critic's option (a) example). It moves the audit hooks out of later transactions, but X5's registration is the only detector of a replaced callback, since sys.monitoring has no callback getter, and a reclaim after an intermediate owner, or a rebinding, needs a registration anyway. Dropping X5's would make a replaced callback silent and unrepaired. Keeping the registration and bounding it removes no detector.
- **Restore the one replaced callback.** A restoring `register_callback` runs its own audit hook before its exchange, during which the other tool may register that event again; the restore would then replace the newer callback with the older. No bounded number of retries closes that, so styxx reports and does not restore.
- **Count a split in `_LOST` at the split.** Written in a first draft of this revision. It changes no record: every trace live at the split already notes MONITOR_LOST (above), and the model check's REGSILENT property holds with no such count (F21). It is not in the spec.
- **Verify "all five callbacks are exactly ours" after the call** (the critic's wording). There is no getter; a second registration to read them back would itself run five audit hooks. A complete registration already establishes that each exchange landed on styxx's id; a callback that a party replaces afterwards is the ordinary outside-party case, detected and repaired by the next exit's X5.

**M5: a patch-level list, not a runtime self-test.** The critic offered both. A runtime self-test at import or at the first `coverage_trace()` would need an audit hook to observe audit events inside the steps, and `sys.addaudithook` cannot be undone, so every traced process would carry styxx's hook for good. Revision 6 therefore restricts M0 to `_VERIFIED`, the patch levels on which the frozen dynamic gate G_ATOM passed with its controls failing, and makes G_ATOM a process gate. The cost is over-blocking #6's new line: every other 3.12 and 3.13 patch level refuses until someone runs G_ATOM on it.

**Totals.** 15 rows (B1, M1–M5, N1–N7, H, F20):
- B1 is resolved by mechanism, bounded and detected, plus the text fixes the critic listed (items 1–5), each applied;
- M1–M5 and N1–N7 are resolved as the critique asked, with the choices stated above. M3's request for a witness of F14's rule is met with X137f, a real-greenlet case the revision-6 model found;
- F20 is a spec bug found while building revision 6's model: the process's first reconciliation raised TypeError.

Rejected: the parts of B1's option (a) listed above, and the runtime self-test of M5.

### The revision-6 model check (M2)

`rev6/m6_modelcheck.py` keeps `rev5/m5_modelcheck.py`'s granularity and adds:
- **Registration with audit hooks.** `_register` is called at E4 (first acquisition, a reclaim of styxx's own freed id, an adoption, a rebinding), at the reconciliation's reclaim and at X5. Its five exchanges are abstracted to two, the entry and exit callbacks and the unwind callback, with a step boundary before each exchange where its audit hook runs. At that boundary a fault (a raising hook), a nested program, an outside event, another thread or a greenlet switch may run. Revision 5 gates once and reads nothing after; revision 6 gates each exchange and reads the owner after the last.
- **Callbacks and local events per id.** A hit counts only if its entry and its unwind reach styxx's callbacks on some id: local events and styxx's entry callback, and global PY_UNWIND and styxx's unwind callback. X5 notes a previous callback that was not styxx's, and a held mint without local events on the id.
- **Two ids.** `_ensure_tool` falls through to the other id when the one it holds is taken, with revision 6's count and local events (revision 5 has neither).
- **Outside parties, combined.** With re-entry (`ext+re`), greenlets (`ext+greenlet`) and `run_async` (`ext+async`); a kind may repeat (`free, reclaim, free`); two new kinds replace styxx's unwind callback or clear the mint's local events; `free` also applies to an id another tool holds.
- **Properties.** REGMULTI: more than one exchange of one registration lands on another tool's id. REGSILENT: at an end, a trace live when an exchange landed on another tool's id has a readable record without MONITOR_LOST. REGCLOB counts such exchanges (informational: the disclosed residual). CLOBBER counts every other styxx write on an id another tool holds, revision 5's registration exchanges included, since revision 5 claimed none.
- **The premise is an axiom.** `_unwind_on`, `_unwind_off`, `_take` and `_set_local` are one step each. The model's results hold only if CPython runs nothing inside them; G_ATOM tests that.

**What the enumeration shows.**
- **Revision 6: 0 violations of any property** in 44 configurations and 83 runs (9,625,175 state hashes). Every LOST hit is noted or MASKED. (Revision 7: this held only because no configuration combined an outside callback replacement with a second core. With one, revision 6 violates LOSTNOTE: the seventh critic's B1; the Revision 7 section.) REGCLOB is non-zero exactly where an outside take can land inside a registration, and REGMULTI and REGSILENT are 0 there.
- **Revision 5 in the same model** violates CLOBBER and REGMULTI in the five configurations where another tool can take the id inside a registration (`ext: open(X) ∥ exit(X)` and the four `reg:` configurations), and nothing else. So the new dimensions find the critic's B1 and are not vacuous.
- The 33 configurations of revision 5's model give the same verdicts for revision 5 as before, except `ext: open(X) ∥ exit(X)`, where X5's registration now has its audit-hook boundaries and an outside take lands inside it. Their state counts differ where registration steps were added.

Outputs: `rev6/out_m6_main.txt` (`m6_modelcheck.py main`, 332 s), `rev6/out_m6_table.md`.

**Hang mode** (`m6_modelcheck.py hang`, the robust mutex's bound removed; `rev6/out_m6_hang.txt`). `with L: exit(X) ∥ exit(Y)+[lock]`: HANG 14 for both revisions without the bound, CYC_TX 14 with it: the mutex cycle, which the bound breaks (I5, #20). Every other configuration of the mode, including the four new `ext+re` and `ext+greenlet` ones, has no HANG.

The file is the concatenation of four runs of the same command over disjoint mutant lists, run in parallel (`mutants off_split … gate_split`, `mutants detach_nulls … prune_credit_stop`, `mutants reg_rev5 rebind_count_none ensure_nocount+blind_read_gated rebind_no_local`, `mutants blind_read_gated`); a fifth, duplicate run was stopped. Every revision-5 rule's mutant is detected as before, `no_exiting_test` now also in the triple `open(X) ∥ open(X) ∥ exit(X)`. Of revision 6's rules, `reg_rev5`, `rebind_count_none` and `blind_read_gated` violate a property; `rebind_no_local` does not, and is witnessed by X137g. `blind_read_gated` alone is detected only in the greenlet configuration (M3, X137f).

**What is still not enumerated.** Two or more nested programs on one thread at once, and budgets above those in the table (outside events are at most 3 per run, and at most 1 when combined with re-entry, greenlets or `run_async`). The five exchanges of a registration are two in the model. Free-threaded interleavings, which the spec refuses. A real greenlet or gevent library: the model's greenlets are abstract. Pending entries and credit counts: hits carry the attribution outcome, not counts. The `record()` and scoring layers. And the premise itself, which the model assumes and G_ATOM tests.

### Found while resolving (not raised by the critique)
- **F20. The process's first reconciliation raised TypeError.** Revision 5's reconciliation ended with `_unwind_off(None)` unconditionally. The first enter's E4 step 1 reconciles before `_ensure_tool` stores an id, so `_TOOL[0]` was None and the pipeline's `get_tool(None)` raised TypeError out of the transaction, so no tracer could ever be entered in a fresh process (`rev6/p_first_reconcile.py`, both versions). `rev5/mech5.py` fixed its tool id at 4 and never met this. Fixed by a guard (M3); the mutation audit names X36 as its witness.
- **F21. A count at a split registration has no observable effect.** A first draft of revision 6 incremented `_LOST` at a split whose owner is another tool. The mutant without it was undetected in the model and in every witness: while another tool holds the id, every exit's registration on it is incomplete, and the id becomes styxx's again only through a reclaim or a rebinding, which count. The rule is not in the spec (M7).
- **F22. The rebinding's count is necessary.** Without it, X137h's loss is silent: the blinded close found PY_UNWIND still set on the taken id, and P's exit registers completely on the new id (`rev6/w6_witnesses.py` X137h; the model's `rebind_count_none`).
- **F23. X154's `_register` sweep could not witness B1.** An INSTRUCTION tool cannot run code inside a C call, so the sweep never placed its interference where the audit hook runs; X154 passed under revision 5 while B1 held. X154b sweeps the audit hook itself.
- **F24. The `_LOST` count can land after another trace's exit.** A greenlet switch inside a reclaim (from its registration's audit hook, a finalizer or a signal handler) after the re-take and before the count makes the reclaim's owner look dead, so another greenlet's exit steals the mutex and runs its MONITOR_LOST test before the count lands. The model found it as the `blind_read_gated` mutant's LOSTNOTE; greenlet 3.5.6 reproduces it (`rev6/w6_greenlet.py`). Revision 6 keeps the close's ungated read as the detector and witnesses it (X137f); a stolen mutex remains the disclosed greenlet residual.

### Weakest points of revision 6, for round 7
1. **The registration residual.** One callback slot of another tool can be left as styxx's when an audit hook on `sys.monitoring.register_callback` lets that tool take styxx's id mid-registration. It is bounded to one slot, reported and noted, but not repaired, and the hook runs five times at every exit, inside the mutex.
2. **The premise gate's reach.** G_ATOM tests the one-call steps with the instruments, gc and signals it arms, in the gate outcomes it prepares. (Revision 7 corrects this, M1 item 5: its implementation-facing part arms the instruments only; gc, signals and threads are tested on t1's and D2's reference pipelines.) A CPython path that runs Python code only under conditions the harness does not create (a debugger that preempts C code, an allocator hook) would pass it. The model takes the premise as an axiom.
3. **Over-blocking by patch level.** Tracing refuses every 3.12 and 3.13 patch level except 3.12.3 and 3.13.12 until G_ATOM is run on it. That is most interpreters in the field.
4. **The binding check's strength.** It checks types, names and module identities. A C-level replacement that forges them, or a wrapper installed after the first binding (never called by the machinery, but also never detected), is outside it.
5. **The model's abstractions.** Two exchanges stand for five; greenlets and `run_async` are abstract; at most one nested program and at most three outside events run per configuration; counts are not modelled.
6. **Greenlets and the stolen mutex.** A greenlet switch inside a transaction still makes its owner look dead. Revision 6 found one consequence (F24) and keeps a detector for it, but only one real-greenlet shape is exercised (X137f); gevent is untested.
7. **The remaining cycle.** The mutex cycle through user code is bounded, not removed, and revision 6 shows it has a second trigger at every exit (a lock-taking audit hook on `register_callback`).

---

## Revision 7: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_rev6_2026_09_25.md`, the seventh critic. Its verdict was NOT_READY: 1 BLOCKER-for-freeze, 5 MUST-FIX and 9 NOTE. The critic confirmed revision 6's registration bound, its binding check, F20, the #130279 constraint and every revision-6 witness, all byte for byte. It found one silent loss that the text said could not happen (B1), and a premise gate that could not be written without judgement and could pass vacuously (M1).

The section has these parts:
- what changed and what was rejected;
- one row per item, in the critique's order, then the #130279 check and the findings made while resolving;
- the revision-7 model check;
- the weakest points left.

A self-audit of every normative claim follows as an appendix.

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev7/`, abbreviated `rev7/`, with outputs in `rev7/out_*.txt`. Each was run on CPython 3.12.3 (`rt3/venv3.12`) and 3.13.12 (`rt3/venv3.13`) with the same outcome unless the row says otherwise. The model check `m7_modelcheck.py` is interpreter-independent and was run once, on 3.12.3.

`rev7/mech7.py` is `rev6/mech6.py` copied with revision 7's rules, each behind a single-rule mutant. It also adds mint joining, which `mech6.py` lacked (F27). Revision 5's and revision 6's witness files, re-run on it through module aliases (`rev7/run_prev_on_mech7.py`), give the same verdicts for every row on both versions. The only exceptions are four label or count differences explained where they occur:
- `out_w5_on_mech7_*`: the `_register` sweep has 67 trials instead of 49, because its pipeline grew, and still 0 failing;
- `out_b1_on_mech7_*`: `r6_b1_register.py` prints revision 7's `r`, which no longer lists previous callbacks;
- `out_w6_on_mech7_*`: X137h's revision-6 mutant row now also notes P (F28);
- the tool name, and the patched-sleep count of revision 5's `time_at_call` row, which is timing.

`critic7/` is the seventh critic's probe directory. "Reading" means the resolution follows from the text and needed no execution. As before, no v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists, so every result is of a model, of M7's step functions as written (`rev7/steps7.py`), or of CPython itself.

### What changed, and what was rejected

**B1: the repair is counted, inside the registration's own C call.** The critic's diagnosis holds: `critic7/c1` reproduces on `mech6.py`, and `critic7/c2` and `c2b` reproduce on the model, all three exactly (`rev7/out_c1_rerun.txt`, `out_c2_rerun.txt`, `out_c2b_rerun.txt`). The critic proposed one rule: X5 increments `_LOST` after its registration returns, when `r` is complete and a previous callback was not styxx's. Revision 7's model, with the critic's two-core configuration and one fault, finds that this still loses the note. An asynchronous exception between the registration's return and the increment leaves the slot repaired and uncounted, and the other trace's loss is silent again. The trace: `rev7/m7_modelcheck.py`, `cb2: open(Y) ∥ exit(X) [cbrep, F1]`, mutant `count_after_register`, LOSTNOTE 1; on CPython, X158b.

So revision 7 moves the count into `_register`'s one consuming call. `_LOST` becomes a list read as `len(_LOST)`. For each exchange whose previous callback `is not` the one styxx registers, `_LOST_APPEND(True)` runs in C before the pipeline pulls the next gate. No fault, audit hook, thread switch or greenlet switch can then separate a repair from its count. (Revision 8 correction, the eighth critic's M1: a replaced callback's finalizer could, because `map` released the previous callback before its count. Revision 8's tee form closes that.)

`r` becomes `[None] * k + [owner]`. It is complete iff `owner is _TOOL_NAME`, which implies five exchanges, because a failed gate is followed by the owner read with nothing between them. X5 reads `len(_LOST)` after its own registration, so the third note source of revision 6 folds into the `_LOST` test.

The consequences, each stated in the text:
- the reload paragraph is restated: every trace live at the first exit after a reload notes MONITOR_LOST;
- "What is lost" gains the callback and local-event places and names every silent shape;
- a registration onto a fresh id counts five (its previous callbacks are None). That is harmless, but it makes X137h's revision-6 shape note P without the rebinding's count, so X137h is rewritten to a shape that isolates that count (F28).

Cases: X158 (the critic's shape) and X158b (the fault after the registration). Mutation rows: `cbrep_nocount` and `count_after_register`.

**M1: G_ATOM made objective.**
- **The G_ATOM interface.** A frozen set of private names that G_ATOM alone may read (M10): `_ANCHORS`, `_TOOL`, `_TOOL_NAME`, `_CONSUME`, `_list`, `_Opening`, `_Core`, `_EVENTS5`, `_CALLBACKS5`, `_LOCAL` and `_LOST`. Openings and cores are built with `__new__` and slot assignment.
- **The case matrix.** Enumerated as products: 64 cases.
- **Four pass criteria per case.** (a) The consumer is called exactly once. (b) Inside that call, no Python code runs and no audit event is raised, except `_register`'s one per exchange. (c) Outside it, no Python code runs and no C call is made except the consumer's and `chain.from_iterable`. (d) The state at the consumer's call equals the state before the step, and the state after equals the effect the spec's rule gives for that gate outcome.
- **The slot list.** Fixed to `armed`, `core`, `frame` on `_Opening` and `flags` on `_Core`.
- **Hookup controls K4–K10.** Frozen patches of `ref_v5f.py`, each of which must make the run fail. They include the critic's two vacuous shapes (K5, K6), revision 5's `_register` (K4, which now fails on what distinguishes it from revision 6's) and the critic's post-call count (K10).
- **t1 and D2 get numeric floors.** The BRANCH row is marked as non-evidence.
- **Scope.** What the gate does and does not show is stated, and weakest point 2 of revision 6 is corrected.

**M2: the verified interpreters, everywhere.** "3.12 and 3.13" is replaced by the verified interpreters (3.12.3 and 3.13.12) at every normative site. That covers the grafts, the Delta table, the version table (with a new row for the other patch levels), the exam's interpreters, G2, G_XVER, G_FI, G_SIG, G_SEM, SM2, SM3, G_CLOSURE, the P1 retro and round-5 item 2. The exam runs on exactly the `_VERIFIED` interpreters, with their builds recorded. X37b pins the patch-level refusal, so the exam needs no real unverified interpreter.

**M3: the binding check runs at every `coverage_trace()`.** It reads the vocabulary as the module binds it now and `_MON` as bound. Line 399's claim is corrected. X156c is the reload variant.

**M4: one subprocess per trial.** X154b's five trials each run in their own subprocess, and the callback-read method (exchange and restore, hook disarmed) is stated. The harness rules add the per-trial rule for any case whose trials change tool ownership.

**M5: the model is extended.**
- Two-core configurations for callback replacement (the unwind callback, and the entry callback as a new kind) and for a local-events clear.
- A greenlet switch and a re-entered exit with a replaced callback.
- Registrations at E4 and at the reclaim with a second live core, a raising hook and a repeated free.
- An initial state in which the other id still carries styxx's callbacks.
- The three E4 counting rules are reconciled into one, stated in E4 ("The one E4 counting rule"): while `_TOOL[0]` is set, every take that ends in a complete registration is counted once, whether it is a reclaim, a rebinding or a re-take of the same id. `mech7.py` and the model implement exactly that. The same-id re-take is modelled and witnessed (X154d).

**N1–N9.** Each is fixed as its row says. Found while resolving: F25–F29.

Considered and rejected:
- **The critic's B1 form, a count in X5 after the registration.** It leaves a fault window between the repair and the count (above; `count_after_register`; X158b). The in-call count closes it at no extra call.
- **Excluding None previous callbacks from the in-call count**, so that a fresh id counts nothing. A party that registers None over styxx's callback removes it, which is a loss, and must count. Excluding None without a second pass over the previous callbacks needs a Python-level or hashing test inside the pipeline, which the premise forbids. The cost is redundancy at a first acquisition, adoption or rebinding, which changes no record, plus X137h's rewrite.
- **Counting a reclaim's re-take before its registration**, which would note a loss-free free even when a raising hook cuts the registration short (F25). It would open a window between the take and the count, reachable by an asynchronous exception or a signal-driven greenlet switch. (Revision 8 correction, the eighth critic's N1: that holds only for a count made in a separate statement. An append chained into `_take`'s own consuming call has no window. Revision 8's N1 row records why that form is still not adopted.) The close's ungated read already notes every call lost while the id was freed (X157c). F25 is disclosed and pinned (X157b) instead.
- **Keying `_VERIFIED` on the build** (N2). It would refuse every rebuild of a verified version. The build is recorded and disclosed instead.
- **M3's alternative, re-checking only when the vocabulary changed since the last check.** Checking every time costs about 5 µs and needs no state.
- **M4's alternative, an inter-trial cleanup inside one subprocess.** A per-trial subprocess needs no cleanup protocol, and it matches how X36 and X142 already isolate tool ownership.
- **Freezing an `_Opening` constructor signature** for G_ATOM. `__new__` and slot assignment need none.

**Totals.** 21 rows: B1, M1–M5, N1–N9, H and F25–F29 (revision 8, N6: revision 7 said 20).
- B1 is resolved by mechanism: one counting rule, placed inside the registration's call, stronger than the critic's proposal.
- M1–M5 are resolved as the critique asked, with the choices above.
- The nine NOTEs are fixed.
- Five findings are recorded.

Rejected: the critic's exact B1 form (superseded), and the alternatives listed above.

### The revision-7 model check (M5)

`rev7/m7_modelcheck.py` is `rev6/m6_modelcheck.py` with:
- `rev=7`: the in-call count (a registration exchange whose previous callback is not styxx's appends in the same step) and the same-id re-take at E4;
- mutants `cbrep_nocount` (revision 6), `count_after_register` (the critic's form) and `retake_nocount`;
- a new outside kind, `cbrep0`, which replaces the entry callback;
- an initial state in which the other id still carries styxx's callbacks;
- fourteen configurations, CONFIGS7.

`main` runs revisions 6 and 7 over all 58 configurations.

**What the enumeration shows.**
- **Revision 7: 0 violations of any property** in 58 configurations and 99 runs (10,465,412 state hashes). Every LOST hit is noted or MASKED.
- **Revision 6 in the same model** violates LOSTNOTE in exactly the seven runs that combine an outside callback replacement with a second core, whether by a second tracer, a greenlet switch, a re-entered exit or a fault. It violates nothing else, so the new dimensions find the critic's B1 and are not vacuous.
- The 44 configurations of revision 6's model give the same verdicts for both revisions. Their state counts are identical except in `reg: first enter(X)`, where a first acquisition's registration now counts.

Outputs: `rev7/out_m7_main.txt`, `out_m7_main_rebind2.txt` (the `rebind2:` configuration, added after the main run started) and `out_m7_table.md`.

**Hang mode** (`rev7/out_m7_hang.txt`, the robust mutex's bound removed). `with L: exit(X) ∥ exit(Y)+[lock]`: HANG 14 for both revisions, the mutex cycle the bound breaks (I5, #20). Every other configuration of the mode has no HANG for either revision, and the counts equal revision 6's.

**Mutants** (`m7_modelcheck.py mutants`, revision 7, every configuration; `rev7/out_m7_mutants_a.txt`, `_b.txt` and `_c.txt`, three runs over disjoint mutant lists, each file one run). Every revision-5 and revision-6 rule's mutant is detected as in revision 6, except `rebind_count_none`. Revision 7 affects that one: without `rebind2:` it is undetected, because a rebinding onto a fresh id is also counted in-call, and with `rebind2:` it gives LOSTNOTE 3 (`rev7/out_m7_mutants_rebind2.txt`). The new rows:

**What is still not enumerated.** Two or more nested programs on one thread at once. Budgets above the tables': at most 3 outside events per run, and at most 1 when combined with re-entry, greenlets or `run_async`, except `cb2: … [all, X2]`. The five exchanges of a registration are two in the model. Free-threaded interleavings, which the spec refuses. A real greenlet or gevent library: the model's greenlets are abstract. Pending entries and credit counts: hits carry the attribution outcome, not counts. The `record()` and scoring layers. One mint: two tracers share it or neither holds it, so a local-events clear on a mint held by one trace only is the one-core case. And the premise itself, which the model assumes and G_ATOM tests. Revision 8 (M1) adds one more axiom: the model makes a registration's exchange and its in-call count one step (line 670 of `m7_modelcheck.py`), which CPython guarantees only for revision 8's tee form. `rev8/m8_modelcheck.py`'s `count_after_release` shows what the model finds without it. Revision 6's two omissions named by the critic (outside callback and local-event changes with one core only, and E4's same-id re-take) are now enumerated.

### Found while resolving (not raised by the critique)
- **F25. A raising hook inside a reclaim leaves the free uncounted.** A trace that lost nothing is not told of the free. A lost call is still noted by its section's ungated close read. This is disclosed and pinned (X157b, X157c).
- **F26. The critic's B1 fix had a fault window.** The model found it with one fault, and X158b shows it on CPython. The count moved inside `_register`'s call.
- **F27. `mech6.py` did not join mints.** A second tracer declaring the same function re-minted it. X137g's Q half was not shown until `mech7.py` joined.
- **F28. The in-call count made X137h's revision-6 shape non-isolating.** X137h was rewritten, and `rebind2:` added.
- **F29. The Tool acquisition prefix overstated the next enter's repair.** Found by the self-audit (Appendix A).

### Weakest points of revision 7, for round 8
1. **The premise gate's reach.** G_ATOM now checks the implementation's own steps for Python code, audit events and state access outside their one C call, in 64 gate outcomes, and its hookup controls show that its criteria catch the known ways of splitting a step. Gc, signals and threads are still tested only on reference pipelines of the same construction. A CPython path that runs Python code only under conditions neither part creates (a debugger that preempts C code, an allocator hook) would pass it. The model takes the premise as an axiom.
2. **Builds.** `_VERIFIED` is keyed on the version, and a vendor build of 3.12.3 or 3.13.12 with backported C changes is accepted but unverified. Its build is not refused, only disclosed.
3. **The registration residual.** One callback slot of another tool can be left as styxx's when an audit hook lets that tool take styxx's id mid-registration. It is bounded, counted, reported and noted, but not repaired. At a first acquisition it is surfaced nowhere (X154c).
4. **F25.** A raising audit hook inside a reclaim leaves a free unnoted on traces that lost nothing. No call is lost silently through it, but the MONITOR_LOST note is not a complete record of frees.
5. **The model's abstractions.** Two exchanges stand for five. There is one mint. Greenlets and `run_async` are abstract. At most one nested program and at most three outside events run per configuration. Counts are not modelled.
6. **Over-blocking by patch level.** Every 3.12 and 3.13 patch level but two refuses until G_ATOM is run on it.
7. **The remaining cycle.** The mutex cycle through user code is bounded, not removed, and every exit's registration triggers its audit hooks five times inside the mutex.

---

## Revision 8: critique items and their resolution

This section answers `DESIGN_protocol_v5f_DRAFT_critique_rev7_2026_09_25.md`, the eighth critic. Its verdict was READY_FOR_EXAM_BRIEF, conditional on its five MUST-FIX edits landing before the freeze: 0 BLOCKER-for-freeze, 5 MUST-FIX and 9 NOTE. The critic reproduced every revision-7 output. It found one silent loss the text said could not happen, fail-closed and needing a finalizer that switches greenlets (M1). It also found two self-audit witnesses that do not witness their claims (M2, M3), timing floors with no rule for a void run (M4), and one contradictory case sentence (M5).

The "verified" column cites probes under `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/rev8/`, abbreviated `rev8/`, with outputs in `rev8/out_*.txt`. Each was run on CPython 3.12.3 (`rt3/venv3.12`) and 3.13.12 (`rt3/venv3.13`) with the same outcome unless the row says otherwise. The model check is interpreter-independent and was run on 3.12.3. `critic8/` is the eighth critic's probe directory.

`rev8/mech8.py` is `rev7/mech7.py` with the tee registration and three probe mutants: `reg_rev7`, `count_excl_none` and `pend_bare_id`. It also carries a `take_count` probe (N1), which is not the spec. Revision 7's witness files, re-run on it (`rev8/w8_witnesses.py`, `w8_audit.py`, `w8_greenlet.py`), give byte-identical verdicts on both versions once the tool name is masked. As before, no v5f implementation or `ref_v5f.py` exists, so every result is of a model, of M7's step functions as written (`rev8/steps8.py`), or of CPython itself.

### What changed, and what was rejected

**M1: the registration keeps every previous callback alive until every count is made.** The critic's diagnosis reproduces exactly. In revision 7's pipeline, `map` released the previous callback before `_LOST_APPEND` ran. A replacement referenced only by the slot was finalized there, with the slot repaired and the count not yet made. A finalizer that switched greenlets let another trace's exit run with `_LOST` unchanged, and its loss was silent. The critic offered a mechanism fix or a disclosure. Revision 8 takes the mechanism, the critic's own sketch (`critic8/c7`): `a, b = _tee(<exchanges>)`, then `_compress(a, _map(_is_not, b, _CALLBACKS5))` yields each previous callback that is not styxx's, and `_map(_LOST_APPEND, _map(_is_not, <that>, _repeat(None)))` counts it. The tee buffer holds every previous callback until the pipeline is released after `_list` returns. `r` is still `[None] * k + [owner]`. Costs:
- `_tee` joins the vocabulary and the binding check (M0, M1);
- `_register` gains a statement, which G_HYG fixes;
- G_ATOM gains a finalizer case, and K14 (revision 7's form) must fail it;
- X158d is the exam witness;
- the model states the exchange-and-count merge as an axiom, and a new mutant, `count_after_release`, shows that the model finds the loss when the two are split.

**M2: G_ATOM's criterion (c) says what it sees, and G_HYG is made to catch what it does not.** Both of the critic's options are taken. (c) now also uses the CALL events G_ATOM already arms: every call from the step's code is of a vocabulary constructor, `from_iterable` or the consumer, and none comes after the interval closes. That catches K11. K12, a bytecode read, is G_HYG's. While checking that G_HYG catches it, revision 8 found that G_HYG's name list did not ban operators: the same read moved into the pipeline statement as `(o.frame in _ANCHORS,)` named only allowed names and passed. G_HYG now bans every operator but calls, the named attribute reads and tuple displays in the pipeline statements (K12b; F30). Lines of M7, G_ATOM's scope paragraph and id 99's witness are corrected.

**M3: G_HYG freezes each step's `_MON[0]` indices, and G_ATOM's `_register` cases include S set.** K13 fails both.

**M4: G_ATOM's timed parts are count-bounded, and no run is void.** Parts E and D2 run to their floors, with `T_MAX = 60 s` as a failure bound. Every outcome is PASS or FAIL, and the first committed run on a state is its verdict.

**M5: X158's cell is corrected.**

**NOTEs.** N2 (V70), N3 (part R), N4 (X158c), N5–N8 (text) and N9 (every mutant run twice) are taken as proposed.

Considered and rejected:
- **N1, counting every take inside `_take`'s own call.** The critic is right that the append has no window, and that revision 7's stated reason for rejecting it was wrong. That reason is corrected in the Revision 7 section. The form was prototyped as `mech8.py`'s `take_count`. It closes F25: X157b's P gets MONITOR_LOST. It also counts every reclaim, re-take and rebinding that goes through a take, so the E4 counting rule's own counts stop being observable on those paths. X137h's witness of the rebinding count, and X154d's witness of the re-take count, no longer distinguish their mutants: with `take_count`, `rebind_count_none` and `retake_nocount` leave R and P noted (`rev8/out_w8_new.txt`, both versions). The rebinding count stays necessary on the one path with no take, a rebinding onto an id this copy already names (an adoption). No exam case reaches that path without an injected fault inside an enter. So adopting the append means re-deriving E4's counting rule: which counts become redundant and are deleted, and a new adoption-shaped witness for the one that stays. That is a mechanism change across E4, M3, the model and five cases, which the critic classed as an optional design choice. F25 stays disclosed, pinned (X157b) and fail-closed. The next revision that touches E4 should take this up.
- **M1's disclosure option** (pin "Q silent" as a residual). The mechanism fix costs one statement and one vocabulary name, so revision 8 removes the loss instead of disclosing it.
- **Making G_ATOM see bytecode** (INSTRUCTION events on the step's code, to catch K12). The allowed instruction set would have to be frozen per version, and G_HYG already rejects the shape statically. The critic's division is kept: calls are G_ATOM's, and bytecode is G_HYG's.
- **M4's alternative, a bounded retry rule** ("at most 3 runs, each committed"). Retrying a premise test until it passes weakens it. Count-bounded parts remove the timing dependence, and a FAIL is final.

**Totals.** 18 rows: M1–M5, N1–N9, H and F30–F32.
- M1–M5 are resolved as the critique asked. M1 takes the critic's mechanism option.
- Eight of the nine NOTEs are fixed. N1 is answered with a prototype and not adopted, and its wrong rationale is corrected.
- Three findings are recorded.
