### The revision-8 model check

`rev8/m8_modelcheck.py` is `rev7/m7_modelcheck.py` with:
- the mutant `count_after_release`. A registration exchange whose previous callback is another party's (`F`) is followed by a step boundary, where faults, re-entry and greenlet switches may land, before its count and gate i+1;
- one configuration, `cb2+greenlet: [exit(X), exit(Y)] ∥ open(Y) [cbrep]` (F32).

Nothing else changes. The model's revision-7 step, with exchange and count in one step, is revision 8's registration, so `rev=7` in the model stands for revision 8. The merge is an axiom (U1; "What is still not enumerated").

**Main** (`rev8/out_m8_main.txt`, all 59 configurations, revisions 6 and 7 of the model). Every row of revision 7's main run (`rev7/out_m7_main.txt` and `out_m7_main_rebind2.txt`) reproduces line for line, apart from the timings. The new configuration gives:

| configuration | budgets (F / I / X / W) | rev 6 | rev 7 (= revision 8) |
|---|---|---|---|
| cb2+greenlet: [exit(X), exit(Y)] ∥ open(Y) [cbrep] | 0 / 0 / 1 / 2 | LOSTNOTE=14  [LOST=26] (2,718,792 states) | ok  [LOST=26] (2,742,680 states) |

The model's revision 7, which stands for revision 8, violates no property in 59 configurations and 100 runs (13,208,092 state hashes). Revision 6 violates LOSTNOTE in 8 runs, the 7 of revision 7's table plus the new configuration (B1), and nowhere else.

**Mutants** (`m8_modelcheck.py mutants`, every configuration, run twice as N9 requires, in two processes started together). The two runs, `rev8/out_m8_mutants_run1.txt` and `_run2.txt`, are byte-identical. Every verdict of revision 7's mutant table reproduces. The new configuration adds a violation to three mutants:

| mutant | first violations |
|---|---|
| revision 8: `count_after_release` (revision 7's CPython registration: a replaced callback's finalizer may run between the exchange and its count) | cb2: open(Y) ∥ exit(X) [cbrep, F1] F1: LOSTNOTE=1; cb2+greenlet: [exit(X), exit(Y)] ∥ open(Y) [cbrep] F0: LOSTNOTE=8 (the eighth critic's c1, fault-free) |
| revision 7: `count_after_register` | as in revision 7, and now also cb2+greenlet: [exit(X), exit(Y)] ∥ open(Y) [cbrep] F0: LOSTNOTE=8 (a greenlet switch between the registration and X5's count) |
| revision 4: `no_anchor_prune` | as before, and cb2+greenlet: [exit(X), exit(Y)] ∥ open(Y) [cbrep] F0: U1=1, A0=3, LOSTNOTE=6 |
| revision 6: `rebind_count_none` | rebind2: F0 LOSTNOTE=3 in both runs. The eighth critic's single `NOT DETECTED` (its N9) did not recur |

`ensure_nocount`, `prune_credit_stop`, `rebind_no_local` and `retake_nocount` are not detected by any configuration, as in revision 7. Their witnesses are X137e, (none: the rule changes nothing), X137g and X154d.

### Found while resolving (not raised by the critique)
- **F30. G_HYG did not ban operators in the pipeline statements.** A test-then-act split written with allowed names passed it. The operator ban and K12b close that.
- **F31. X137h's other traces.** Q's note also depends on the rebinding count. The case now states every trace's outcome.
- **F32. No model configuration switched to the losing trace's exit inside X5.** One configuration is added.

### Weakest points of revision 8, for round 9
1. **The premise gate's reach.** G_ATOM checks calls, audit events and Python code, and G_HYG checks bytecode shape. Gc, signals and threads are still tested only on reference pipelines of the same construction. A CPython path that runs Python code only under conditions neither creates would pass both. The tee form adds one CPython behaviour to the premise: a tee buffer keeps its values until both branches are released. G_ATOM's finalizer case tests that on each listed patch level.
2. **F25.** A raising audit hook inside a reclaim still leaves a loss-free free unnoted (X157b). The fix is known and window-free, but it means re-deriving E4's counting rule and its witnesses (N1).
3. **The self-audit's keywords.** The wider extraction (*nothing, no, none, only, every*) is required before the freeze and was not run in revision 8. (The verifier ran it after revision 8, and Appendix A, "The wider extraction", records the result: seven findings, W1–W7, each resolved in the text.)
4. **Builds.** Unchanged from revision 7: `_VERIFIED` is keyed on the version, and the build is recorded, not refused.
5. **The model's abstractions.** Unchanged from revision 7, with one axiom more: the exchange and its count are one step.
6. **Over-blocking by patch level, and the remaining cycle.** Unchanged from revision 7.

---

## Appendix A: self-audit of the normative claims (revision 7)

**Why.** Revisions 5 and 6 each had a blocker of one class: a normative sentence ("the loss is silent only when…", "no one-call step raises an audit event") that no faithful implementation satisfies, and that no case or model property checked. Revision 7 therefore audits every normative absolute in the text, not only the ones a critic named.

**Method.** A script extracted every occurrence of *must*, *never*, *always*, *cannot* and "0 violations" (in any case) from the normative text: everything from the title through "What round 5 should attack first". That is 305 hits. The audit ran on the revision-7 text before this appendix and the Revision 7 section were added (`rev7/audit_claims.json`; the classified list is `rev7/audit_claims_classified.txt`, one line per hit, and `rev7/audit_classify.py` holds the classification as data). Each hit is in one class:

| class | meaning | hits | what counts as its witness |
|---|---|---|---|
| P | a property of the mechanism ("X never happens", "Y always holds") | 104 | a named exam case whose outcome differs if the property fails, or a model-check property, or a static G_HYG clause |
| D | a disclosure: an over-block, a limit or a residual ("never credited", "cannot tell") | 25 | the pinned case or residual that fixes the disclosed outcome |
| C | a fact about CPython the design relies on | 18 | the probe that measured it, or the frozen gate that re-measures it |
| G | a rule that a gate, the harness or the exam author must follow ("the run must fail", "must equal the table") | 81 | the gate's own execution; there is nothing further to test |
| N | a case's own expected outcome ("the body never ran") | 32 | the case itself |
| S | a summary row (Delta table, per-finding disposition, closure) | 18 | the cases its own row names, and the section it summarizes |
| H | history, rationale, a heading, or a quoted earlier claim | 27 | none needed |

**Revision 8 notes on the method (the eighth critic's N5).**
- *Keywords.* The extraction keyed on *must, never, always, cannot* and "0 violations". The false sentence behind the eighth critic's M1, "Nothing runs between exchange i, its comparison and count" (M7, "The registration"), is phrased with "Nothing" and was never a hit. The audit that must run before the freeze also extracts *nothing, no, none, only* and *every*, and classifies every new hit by the table above. Revision 8 did not run that wider extraction; the verifier ran it on the revision-8 text, and "The wider extraction" below records it. Its own new normative sentences are listed with their witnesses in the Revision 8 table.
- *Classes.* Id 121 ("the machinery's correctness arguments never use the absence of an eval-breaker check between two bytecodes") is a property, P, not H. Id 56 (the vendor-build disclosure, N2) is D, not H. Both rows are added to the table below. The counts become P 105, D 26 and H 25; G 81, N 32, C 18 and S 18 are unchanged, 305 in all.
- *Provenance.* `rev7/` in this appendix is the scratch directory the Revision 7 section names (`/tmp/…/scratchpad/v5f/rev7/`). `audit_claims.json` and `audit_claims_classified.txt` are there, and re-running `audit_classify.py` on them gives the stated counts, as the eighth critic checked. The committed copy, `papers/first-afference/protocol_v5f_design/rev7/`, has `audit_classify.py` but not its two inputs. Revision 8 edits only this file, so they are not added there. Whoever commits the evidence must copy both inputs with it.
- *Sampled witnesses.* The eighth critic checked about 25 P, D and C rows. Ids 99 and 107 did not hold as cited, and are corrected below (M1–M3). Id 177's mutant row rests on reading, as the SM1 list already says, and its row now says so.

"0 violations" in the Revision 1–7 sections are records of runs, not normative claims. Each cites its output file, and the seventh critic re-ran every revision-6 output with the same result (N9). They are not in the 305.

**Result.** Of the 147 P, D and C hits, 127 had a witness before this audit. The other 20 make up twelve findings: A1–A3, A5–A9 and A13–A16. Reading the sentences around each hit found four more, which the keywords do not mark or which sit in a summary row: A4 in the R1-B4 row, A10, A11, and A12 (the critic's B1). Each of the sixteen is resolved below: given a witness, weakened, or corrected. After the audit every P and D claim names a witness, with two exceptions: A13, open under SM1's pre-freeze procedure, and one D claim that cannot be tested in-process (frames that never return). Two C claims rest on probes that no frozen gate re-runs (A14). Revision 8 closes A13 with V70 (N2). It freezes A14's recursion claim as G_ATOM part R (N3); its gc claim is re-measured by part D2.

**What the audit shows about the method.** Of the sixteen findings:
- six had no witness because the property was argued rather than tested (A1, A2, A3, A7, A8, A9);
- four were false or overstated sentences carried from an earlier revision after the mechanism changed (A4, A10, A11, A16);
- three were disclosed behaviours with no pinned outcome (A5, A6, A13);
- two rest on probes or on reading (A14, A15);
- one was the critic's B1 (A12).

No critic had raised A1–A11, A13, A14 or A15. The audit is repeatable: `rev7/audit_classify.py` holds the classification, and a later revision should re-run the extraction and classify every new hit before it is frozen.

### The wider extraction (revision 8's pre-freeze duty, run by the verifier)

**Method.** `verify8/extract_wide.py` (`/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/1230efe6-3e44-5eb6-ad91-35910fea5db9/scratchpad/v5f/verify8/`, abbreviated `verify8/`) ran on the revision-8 text as the verifier received it (3,212 lines), before any edit below. It takes the normative text, from the title through "What round 5 should attack first" (lines 1–2183), code blocks included. It splits it into sentences, table cells and list items, and keeps every one that contains *nothing, no, none, only* or *every* as a word, in any case (so *None* counts). It works by sentence, not by hit, so a sentence with three keywords is one claim. `verify8/classify_wide.py` holds the classification as data, and `verify8/wide_claims_classified.txt` has one line per sentence with its class and witness.
- 1,241 sentences contain a wide keyword. 92 of them also contain a revision-7 keyword, so the 305-hit audit covered them, or, for sentences revision 8 added, the Revision 8 table did. That leaves **1,149 new claims**. By keyword they contain *no* 458 times, *every* 319, *only* 315, *none* 149 and *nothing* 89.
- The same rules as revision 7's classify by section: Exam cases (N or G), the semantic-mutation gate and the process gates (G), the Delta table and the disposition and closure tables (S), and "What round 5 should attack first" and the P1 retro (H). Every other sentence was read and classed by hand. That is 666 sentences in Grafts, Decisions, Verified, Target identity, Attribution, Mechanism, Exception-safety, Version policy, Reason codes, Over-blocking and Stated limits.

| class | new claims |
|---|---|
| P | 297 |
| D | 62 |
| C | 40 |
| G | 315 |
| N | 164 |
| S | 80 |
| H | 191 |
| total | 1,149 |

**Result.** Each of the 399 P, D and C claims now names a witness: a case, a gate clause, a model property, or (for C) the probe that measured it. Ten sentences, making up seven findings, had no witness or were false as written. Each was given a witness or corrected, as the table below shows. Three claims rest on reading alone, and are disclosed as such:
- the two sentences of M7's MemoryError disclosure ("In `_unwind_off` that can leave S set with no anchor …", "This matters only when the process is out of memory"), which need an allocation-fault injector that no gate has. They are D, like id 182;
- the E3 remedy's remark that `import_module` takes no module lock for a module already imported (C, from CPython's `_find_and_load`; it is advice, and no property depends on it).

| # | claim (section; wide ids) | before | action |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | "If both ids then deliver an event of one minted frame, the pending entry is keyed by the frame, so the second delivery finds no entry and publishes nothing: no double count" (E4, rebinding; id 273) | no case: in X137g the other tool registers its own callbacks, so only id 3 delivers | **X137i** (new): the other tool registers nothing, so both ids deliver f's events. Spec P `{f:1}`, Q `{f:1, g:1}`. Mutant `pend_get` (a confirmation that does not pop) `{f:2}` and `{f:2, g:1}`. Mutation row |
| W2 | "Revision 5 (N2) lists every machinery site that raises an audit event", and "the open and close paths still raise no audit event" (Exception-safety, Sources; ids 485, 493) | **false**: `_run`'s own `sys._getframe()` (M7 code) raises `sys._getframe` at every open. `id()` raises `builtins.id` and a frame's `f_code` read raises `object.__getattr__` on both versions (`verify8/probe_open_audit.py`: 23 events in one `run()`). The machinery makes such calls in `_open`'s walk, in every callback, in `_alive` and at the mint keys | **corrected**: the Sources list names the missed sites and where each lands. At open, all precede `_open`'s append, so a raising hook leaves nothing behind (**V71**, new, with variants raising at each of the three events; mutant `frame_after_append` leaves a stray opening). In callbacks it is L-DELIVERY and I6. Inside transactions it is the "slow owners" and I5 class (W5). No one-call step makes such a call (G_ATOM (b)), so no property changes |

Mutant `cbrep_nocount`: only the first exit is noted |

The model's lock-taking configuration stands for any such site, and I5's bound covers them |

Spec PASS `{f:1}`, `tool == 4`, no note. Mutant `adopt_no_register`: NOT_EXERCISED with MONITOR_LOST |

Each new case was run on `rev8/mech8.py` under the spec and under its single-rule mutant, on 3.12.3 and 3.13.12, with identical verdicts (`verify8/out_w_new_cases.txt`, `verify8/out_v71.txt`). The four cases are added to the case tables, the placement list, the Mutation audit and the SM1 list. As with every revision-8 result, these runs are of the prototype, not of `ref_v5f.py`.

**What the wider extraction shows.** Of the seven findings, four are false or stale sentences that no earlier keyword marked (W2, W4, W5, W6), and three are argued properties with no case (W1, W3, W7). Every finding sits in a sentence that the wider keywords mark and the revision-7 keywords do not. The two keyword sets together are the audit's scope. Each extraction must run on the text that is frozen, after its last edit, and `verify8/extract_wide.py` and `classify_wide.py` rerun it. The sentences this subsection and W1–W7 add name their own witnesses.
